#CH 101
#TITLE: A Lively Qiqiao Banquet
Having repaid her debt to Prince Yi, the four parted ways outside Qiaotou Wa.
Qiao Wenxian took Prince Yi and Princess Jiahe straight back to Qiao Manor for the Qiqiao Banquet, while Qianli Xun hurried back to Zhu Manor.
Inside the carriage, Qianli Xun studied Kuqiao without blinking...
Flushing under her mistress's stare, Kuqiao confessed at once: "Don't look at me like that, Miss. I do like Bai Feng, and I wasn't trying to hide it from you today."
"When did this start?"
"The last time I went to West Third Alley, he blocked my carriage, talked my ear off, and gave me a clay figurine. That's when I agreed."
"One clay figurine won you over? Are you even my maid? If you want clay figurines, I can buy you piles of them."
"It wasn't about the figurine! I like Bai Feng, and besides, he's Prince Yi's guard with a bright future ahead. From what I've seen so far, he treats me pretty well."
Qianli Xun let out an easy laugh at last. "Now that sounds more like my maid—didn't let love go straight to your head."
Kuqiao grew bold: "Funny, Miss, you seem like the one who let love go to her head."
"You dare talk back to your own mistress? Watch me tickle you!"
Qianli Xun tickled Kuqiao until she burst into giggles, shrieking over and over, "Miss! Mercy! Spare me!"
Mid-tickle, Qianli Xun caught sight of Xiaguo forcing a smile beside them to play along. She paused. "What's wrong with you? You look out of sorts."
"Both Miss and Sister Kuqiao have someone you love, while I..."
Qianli Xun laughed. "Didn't you say you have someone too? Tell me who!"
How could Xiaguo dare admit it? She covered it up: "I just made that up on the spot to turn Nan Shuang down. Don't take it to heart, Miss."
"I see. Well, no need to envy us. You're pretty—just work on cultivating your inner self, and when the time is right, I'll make sure to find you a fine match."
Xiaguo looked curious. "Miss, how do you cultivate your inner self?"
"Your inner self comes down to two things: learning and character. You already know most characters, so flip through the books in my room whenever you're free. As the saying goes, a mind steeped in poetry and books gives a person natural grace. Over time, cultivation follows. As for character, remember four words: Right Thought, Right Mindfulness. Keep them in mind in everything you do."
Xiaguo seemed to grasp it and nodded. "I'll remember."
"I'll also have Kuqiao teach you how to whisk tea. Once an opening comes up, I'll pick out a good family for you."
Panic struck Xiaguo. "Miss doesn't need to pick a family for me! I only want to follow you. When you marry into the Qiao family, I'll follow you right there."
Kuqiao scolded her: "Xiaguo, which word out of your mouth is true and which is false? One second ago you envied Miss and me for having sweethearts, and the next you refuse when Miss offers to find you a good match. What's going on?"
Qianli Xun jumped in to explain for her: "She just wants to find someone she likes on her own, same as you. Still, Xiaguo, you can look on your own, but he has to pass my inspection first. If I say no, neither of you is allowed to marry—hear me? That goes for Bai Feng too!"
Kuqiao protested, "Bai Feng is a great guy. Miss doesn't need to worry."
"Ever heard 'like master, like servant'? I know what kind of man Prince Yi is better than you do. Stay sharp! Until you know Bai Feng inside and out, no holding hands, no..." Qianli Xun bit off the rest. "Anyway, nothing too intimate, or you'll live to regret it."
"Ah! So many rules, Miss—what if Bai Feng and I already held hands?"
"Fine, I'll allow holding hands, and nothing else—hear me?"
"Loud and clear, Miss. Don't worry."
Running Qianli Fist Manor had given Qianli Xun the habit of fussing over her disciples, and she had brought that habit straight into the Song dynasty, worrying over her two personal maids' love lives without even trying.
Back at Zhu Manor, they headed straight for the Qiqiao Banquet.
The servants had all returned, filling Zhu Manor with lively cheer once more. Lanterns and silk festoons glowed everywhere, brightest of all where the banquet tables stood, and the entire household had gathered there.
By the time Qianli Xun arrived, the Qiqiao Banquet stood laid out, though no one had taken their seats yet.
Matron Wei spotted her first and hurried over to take her hand. "At last you're back, Young Lady—everyone was waiting on you." Turning toward the hall, she called out, "Master, Madam, the Young Lady is back!"
Zhu Xiyan, Madam Xiang, Zhu Jingyuan, and Chen Wenxin stepped out of the hall, lining up in order before the incense table.
A servant lit three sticks of incense and handed them to Zhu Xiyan. Holding them, he bowed three times to the moon while the rest followed suit, then planted the three sticks one by one into the censer.
Only after the offering did everyone take their seats to enjoy the Qiqiao Banquet.
Once the masters finished bowing, the maids offered their prayers, followed by the male servants.
Afterward, the maids picked up needles and thread to try threading them by moonlight. Kuqiao and Xiaguo joined in, laughing and having the time of their lives.
Meanwhile, the male servants placed offerings and the Four Treasures of the Study on a table holding a clay statue. A maid cradling Laibao knelt down and helped him kowtow, chanting three times: "Eldest grandson of the Zhu house, Laibao, prays for wit and wisdom!"
Everyone in the Zhu family burst into wide smiles. After praying for him, the maid handed Laibao back into Chen Wenxin's arms, and the male servants took their turns kneeling before the altar to pray for cleverness.
Eyeing the clay statue, Qianli Xun asked Matron Wei beside her, "Who is that statue modeled after?"
Matron Wei answered, "That's the Cowherd. When boys and men kneel to him, they pray for intelligence."
Eating her way through the Qiqiao Banquet, Qianli Xun marveled at the wisdom of the ancients, who gave even an ordinary festival such an uplifting meaning. Not like the era she had crossed from, where a fine title like "Cowherd" (niulang) had taken on a sleazy meaning—male escort! What a decline in morals. Looked at from that angle, was history moving forward or backward? Nobody could say for sure.
Just like human life itself—maybe the healthiest state was being like little Laibao. Growing up looked like gaining wisdom, yet hearts grew tangled and complicated until people marched toward death in that tangle. Growing up looked like progress, yet it was a step backward too.
Qianli Xun realized one Qixi Festival was turning her into a philosopher, brooding over history and the meaning of life.
Kuqiao's thrilled voice rang out: "Whoa! I caught a spider!"
The maids crowded around, and Lutao brought over a small wooden box. "I hunted forever and couldn't find one—you're so lucky! Take this box, and tomorrow you have to let me see the web it spins so I can borrow some of your good luck!"
Kuqiao took the box. "Thanks, Sister Lutao. Tomorrow I'll let you peek first when I open it."
The masters shared in Kuqiao's cheer; even Zhu Xiyan wore a broad smile. For one thing, keeping a stern face on Qixi Festival brought bad luck. For another, his daughter had asked Prince Yi to step in and settle Xiaguo's trouble, proving Prince Yi looked kindly on the Zhu family—with Prince Yi as his backing, his seat as Prefect of Lin'an stood solid as Mount Tai.
Best of all, he had heard the Qiao family meant to propose marriage to the Zhu family. Becoming in-laws with imperial relatives was a windfall beyond his dreams. He even regretted marrying Shuzhen off to the Lin family in a rush—had he known Young Master Qiao had feelings for her, he would never have done it. Thank goodness everything was turning out right. In high spirits, Zhu Xiyan drank several extra cups with his son, Zhu Jingyuan.
On Qixi night, the servants stayed up reveling late, while Qianli Xun turned in early.
In the middle of the night, she had a dream. A man stood with his back to her. Mistaking him for Qiao Wenxian, she shouted, "Wenyuan!"
When the man turned around, it was Shen Jianchen. She ran up and grabbed him. "Shen Jianchen, where have you been? Did you cross over too?"
Shen Jianchen reproached her: "Why didn't you come look for me?"
"Where was I supposed to look?"
"You have someone you love now—have you forgotten me already?"
"I do have someone I love, but I haven't forgotten you."
"Then tell me: do you like him, or do you like me?"
Qianli Xun let go of his hand. "I never said yes to you! Same answer as always: I'll marry you when pigs climb trees!"
Shen Jianchen vanished, replaced by Prince Yi staring at her with a teasing gleam: "Youqi Jushi, I love you!"
Qianli Xun swung a punch straight at him—and jolted herself awake, eyes flying open to find her arm thrust straight into the air.
Dawn had not broken yet, and pitch darkness filled the yard outside. Qianli Xun lowered her arm, rolled over, and tried to fall back asleep, yet sleep refused to come. Thinking back on that absurd dream, she could not help laughing.
Dreaming of Shen Jianchen left her missing him. Where on earth was he? When would she ever cross paths with him again? And if they met and he found out about Wenyuan, would it break his heart?
Then again, maybe Shen Jianchen had already built a new life somewhere else and forgotten all about her. Why borrow trouble worrying over nothing? Better to get some sleep.
#CH 102
#TITLE: A Day in the Life of a Noble Lady
At the first hint of dawn, the servants of Zhu Manor were up and about. Yinxing and Lutao led several maids into the small courtyard, crowding around Kuqiao as they waited for her to open the wooden box, eager to see how the spider had spun its web overnight.
Xiaguo had no interest at all. With her mistress still asleep and nothing to do, she sat idling in the maids' quarters, daydreaming about Young Master Qiao.
When the maids lifted the lid and saw a big, round web inside, cheers erupted, and their laughter woke Qianli Xun.
She opened her eyes to daylight. Waking from that dream last night had left her tossing and turning; she had no idea when she had drifted back off.
Infected by the maids' bright laughter, Qianli Xun climbed out of bed ready to welcome a fine day. She wanted a look at the spiderweb too, for good luck.
Still in her nightgown, she stepped out of bed and opened the door.
At the sound of the door, the maids hushed their laughter and bowed to their mistress.
Qianli Xun said, "Carry on, don't mind me. Once you're done looking, bring the box over so I can see too."
"We've already seen it and need to get to work, so we won't disturb Miss." With a smile, Lutao led Yinxing and the other maids away.
Quiet returned to the courtyard.
Carrying the wooden box, Kuqiao stepped up to her mistress. "Look at it indoors, Miss. Autumn is here and the mornings are chilly—don't catch cold."
Qianli Xun took the box from Kuqiao, stepped inside, and opened it. Sure enough, tucked in the corner sat a web spun overnight—big and round for the size of the box.
"You girls sure know how to have fun, making a game out of spiderwebs."
Making the bed as she spoke, Kuqiao said, "Take a close look, Miss. The web is tight, fine, and round—anyone who sees it gains skill and cleverness."
Qianli Xun looked again; it really was. Setting the box aside, she said, "So I get skill too? Too bad I can't thread a needle to save my life, let alone embroider."
"Everyone's skill is different. For Miss, it's writing poetry and ci."
"Fair point."
Xiaguo carried in a basin of washing water, overheard their chat, set the basin on a stool, and stood ready with a towel in her hands.
"Miss was born clever and honed it through study—that's why she writes great poetry and ci. It has nothing to do with spiderwebs. Back in the countryside I saw webs twice as big and round, and nobody gained a lick of skill from them. Don't blow an ordinary spiderweb out of proportion, Sister Kuqiao."
Helping her mistress change, Kuqiao retorted, "What a killjoy you are! It's a custom handed down by our ancestors."
Xiaguo pressed on: "That custom makes zero sense and doesn't even leave the poor spider in peace. It'd be better if everyone forgot it."
Once dressed, Qianli Xun let Xiaguo help her wash her face.
As she washed, Qianli Xun said, "When a custom survives, it has its reason; if it fades away one day, that has its reason too. Enjoy it while it's here, and don't fret if it's gone. Go with the flow in life."
Drying her face, she handed the towel back to Xiaguo and sat down at the vanity while both maids styled her hair and makeup.
Her remarks left the two maids unable to bicker any further.
Once dressed and fed, Qianli Xun settled at her desk to work while Kuqiao whisked tea and Xiaguo watched to learn.
Whenever Kuqiao made tea, Xiaguo sat beside her studying every step. Today she wanted to try her own hand: "Sister Kuqiao, let me whisk a bowl for you in a moment?"
"Sounds wonderful! I make tea for Miss all day and never get a bowl made for me. I'll treat myself today."
After serving her mistress's tea at the desk, Kuqiao yielded the tea table to Xiaguo. Following the steps she had memorized one by one, Xiaguo turned out a respectable bowl.
Kuqiao took a sip and praised her: "Better than my very first try."
Xiaguo beamed. Getting praised on her very first bowl meant that with a bit more practice, she could surpass Kuqiao.
Thrilled, she asked her mistress for a book to read in the maids' quarters. Even though much of it went over her head, she pored over every line, driven by one wish: to be worthy of Young Master Qiao someday.
While Xiaguo was out of the room, Qianli Xun handed her latest finished manuscript to Kuqiao to deliver to West Third Alley. Kuqiao tucked it away, hitched a carriage, and set off.
With nothing pressing to do, Qianli Xun was about to go chat with her sister-in-law Chen Wenxin when Matron Wei arrived alone.
"Where are you headed, Young Lady?"
"Thought I'd go sit with Sister-in-law in her courtyard."
"Go in a bit. Madam sent me to confirm something with you."
"What is it? Come inside and talk, Matron."
Once seated inside, Matron Wei saw no maids around and asked, "Where are Kuqiao and Xiaguo? Not a soul in sight?"
"I sent Kuqiao out on an errand, and Xiaguo is reading in her room—I gave her permission."
Matron Wei chided her: "I don't mean to lecture you, Young Lady, but kind as you are, you shouldn't spoil maids until they forget their place. That girl Xiaguo is restless enough as it is; let her read books and soon she'll fancy herself a mistress!"
"You're making too much of it, Matron. I just want her to read a little so I can find her a good match later."
"Don't waste your energy on her. That girl aims sky-high—she turned her nose up at a Military Training Officer, so Heaven knows who she's set her sights on. Never mind her; let's talk real business. Tell me the truth, Young Lady: is the Qiao family planning to come propose marriage at Zhu Manor?"
Wenyuan had only told her yesterday—how had Matron Wei caught wind of it so fast?
"How did you hear about that, Matron?"
"So it's true!" Matron Wei burst into a grin. "While you were out strolling yesterday, Madam and I didn't sit idle either—we went to the Vice-Prefect's residence for Madam Qin's Qixi Tea Banquet. Over tea, someone mentioned the Qiao family had been picking a bride for their son and halted out of nowhere, telling the matchmaker to return all the young ladies' birth horoscopes because they had already settled on their future daughter-in-law."
Secret glee bubbled in Qianli Xun's chest. Even if the Qiao family's bride selection could not rival a prince choosing a consort, it was huge news in Lin'an, depending on both pedigree and luck—and she, Qianli Xun, had turned out to be the lucky one.
Matron Wei went on: "Later, Intendant An's wife whispered to Madam that she heard from the Qiao family's matchmaker that they plan to propose to the Zhu family. Madam couldn't believe her ears, and couldn't ask you in front of everyone last night, so she sent me over today."
Seeing no need to hide it from her family, Qianli Xun repeated every word Wenyuan had told her yesterday.
Matron Wei slapped her knee, roaring with laughter. "Haha! If you ask me, the Qiao family is in quite a rush! You only went through your mutual divorce a short while ago—waiting half a year would be just right, haha! Just goes to show how outstanding our Young Lady is; the Qiao family is afraid someone else will snatch you up first! Haha!"
Matron Wei's reaction made Qianli Xun realize a month or two was not long at all, and she felt far too shy to admit she wished the Qiao family would come tomorrow.
Matron Wei chatted a while longer without seeing hide or hair of Xiaguo, and before leaving reminded her mistress once more not to pamper the servants.
Qianli Xun snapped to attention—she had almost forgotten her appointment with Prince Yi from yesterday! Good thing she remembered in time. With lunch approaching, visiting her sister-in-law would have to wait for another day.
Only when Lutao and the other maids set out lunch on schedule did Xiaguo emerge from the maids' quarters to wait on her mistress.
Standing beside her mistress as she ate, bitterness welled up in Xiaguo's chest. Why was she born to serve while her mistress was born to be served? Heaven was so unfair.
Just then Kuqiao walked in from delivering the manuscript. Seeing her, Xiaguo snapped, "All you do is run off and play, Sister Kuqiao, only strolling back now! Don't you know Miss needs waiting on?"
Kuqiao shot back, "Miss sent me on an errand! Unlike certain people who grab a book and hide out in the maids' quarters acting like a noble lady."
"You!" Stung, Xiaguo burst into tears. "Miss gave me permission to read, and you know it! Fine, starting tomorrow I won't read a single page! Waaah!"
No peace even at lunch! Quarreling maids were twice the headache of male martial-arts disciples—she could neither hit them nor scold them harsh. Qianli Xun set down her chopsticks. "What's gotten into you two today—swallowed gunpowder? Looks like you two just can't get along. Tell you what, Xiaguo, why don't you go stay back at Wangyue Pavilion for now."
With a thud, Xiaguo dropped to her knees. "Please don't send me away, Miss! I'll never fight with Sister Kuqiao again—I'll wait on you with all my heart!"
Kuqiao chimed in: "Miss, I promise I won't bicker with Xiaguo anymore either."
"That's more like it. Behave yourselves and get along."
Ignoring them both, Qianli Xun went back to eating.
The two maids turned docile on the spot, one serving dishes and the other ladling soup.
When the time drew near, Qianli Xun prepared to head for the Divine Willow to meet Prince Yi. She took only Kuqiao, telling Xiaguo to stay behind, mind the courtyard, and get some more reading done.
Only after asking whether her mistress was going to meet Young Master Qiao and hearing a firm no did Xiaguo settle back in the courtyard, returning to the books she did not quite understand.
#CH 103
#TITLE: A Rendezvous Beneath the Millennial Divine Willow
Walking down the hillside path toward the Divine Willow, Qianli Xun felt a pang of guilt.
The Divine Willow stood not far from Youlin Retreat. Yet here she was, not sneaking out for a tryst with Wenyuan, but meeting Prince Yi in secret. If Wenyuan ever found out, he was sure to be upset.
And then there was Princess Jiahe, whom Qianli Xun already regarded as a close confidante. Devoted as the princess was to Prince Yi, how heartbroken would she be if she knew he had invited Qianli Xun out here?
The red silk ribbons were gone from the willow. Bare of ribbons, the tree reminded her all the more of her brawl with Shen Jianchen beneath it before crossing over.
Thinking of Shen Jianchen while gazing at the figure standing with his back to her under the willow left Qianli Xun in a trance. If not for the Song robes and Bai Feng standing beside him, she would have sworn Prince Yi was Shen Jianchen.
Bai Feng proved far livelier than his master, stepping forward with a bow. "Youqi Jushi, may I take Kuqiao off to play?"
Qianli Xun looked Bai Feng up and down. "If you ever dare let Kuqiao down, I won't spare you!"
"Put your mind at ease—I treat Kuqiao like gold! Just promise me you won't marry her off to anyone else."
Qianli Xun warned him, "Until I agree to let you marry Kuqiao, no holding hands and nothing closer—hear me? If word reaches my ears, I don't care who your master is, I'll still beat you up!"
"Mercy, I surrender!" Bai Feng stuck out his tongue and winked at Kuqiao, and the two headed off to sit on the stones by the pond and chat.
While Bai Feng spoke, Shen Jianchen had already turned around, drinking in the sight of the woman he loved, searching that familiar face for glimpses of the lively soul he knew.
Seeing Prince Yi stay silent and just flirt with his eyes, Qianli Xun frowned. "Here you go again, Prince Yi. Nobody else is around today, so let me give you a piece of my mind. I have someone I love, and you have someone too—why do you keep staring at me like that? Don't you think it's shameless?"
"Is that how you see me?"
Qianli Xun dodged his gaze. "Of course I do! Eating from your own bowl while eyeing the pot—that's pure greed. Since you asked me here today, let's put our cards on the table. I'm grateful for your help, sure, but one thing is separate from another. You can't expect me to ditch Wenyuan for you just because you did me a favor. I'm a one-man woman: I love one person for life and marry one person for life."
Every word struck straight at Shen Jianchen's heart. The truth rose to his lips, only for him to swallow it back down.
After a silence, he said, "Youqi Jushi misjudges me. I'm devoted to one person too. The pity is that I don't love the one who loves me, while the one I love is about to marry another man—and by my own hand. Can you understand that pain?"
Qianli Xun had to hand it to him: in two sentences he painted himself as the tragic victim.
"Did Prince Yi summon me here just to claim you're a faithful man? Haha, spin that tale to Princess Jiahe and she might buy it, but my sharp eyes see right through you—you're a fickle playboy."
Shen Jianchen stepped closer. "Does Youqi Jushi dare look me in the eye?"
"What's there to dare? I just can't be bothered staring at you..." Qianli Xun backed away.
Shen Jianchen baited her: "Don't tell me you've fallen for me and feel too guilty to admit it?"
"Me, fall for you? Hilarious! Look you in the eye? Fine—what are you going to do, eat me?" Qianli Xun locked eyes with Prince Yi—and a jolt like an electric shock shot through her whole body. Why had she never felt anything like this with Wenyuan?
"Qianli Xun, will you be with me?" Speaking through Zhao Pu's lips, Shen Jianchen voiced his heart once more.
Dazed all over again, Shen Jianchen's name flashed across her mind. Blinking into focus, all she saw was a fickle royal prince—and in a rush of panic, her fist flew out, landing smack on Prince Yi's cheek as she shouted, "Shameless!"
Hearing the shout, Kuqiao and Bai Feng shot up and came running.
Seeing the bruise blooming on his master's left cheek, Bai Feng cried, "Your Highness, are you all right?"
Seeing her mistress had punched a prince, Kuqiao dropped to her knees at once. "Your Highness, spare her!"
A wave of delayed dread hit Qianli Xun too. She had just punched an imperial prince—one who had helped her, no less! If he pressed the issue, Zhu Xiyan could be dragged down with her.
Yet Prince Yi spoke up: "Get up, go further off with Bai Feng, and pay us no mind. Youqi Jushi and I still have things to discuss."
Amazed at Prince Yi's magnanimity, Kuqiao sighed in relief for her mistress, rose, and walked with Bai Feng to the far side of the pond to sit on the rocks and talk love.
Qianli Xun said, "If Prince Yi hadn't said something so flippant, I wouldn't have hit you."
"Sooner or later you'll understand: I wasn't being flippant. Every word came from my heart."
"My advice is to treasure Princess Jiahe. The Qiao family will be coming to Zhu Manor to propose soon—I hope Prince Yi never breathes another word of what you said today."
"I know it's impossible now. I just wanted you to know my heart—to know someone loves you in silence..."
"What good does knowing do? It only brings headaches."
"Has Youqi Jushi remembered my question yet?"
"What question?"
Dejection crossed Shen Jianchen's face. "You never take my words to heart. Fine, I won't hold it against you. I'll ask again: after Youqi Jushi lost her memory, does the new soul inside her remember ever loving someone?"
Qianli Xun paused to think. Could Prince Yi have guessed she crossed over? Impossible—he didn't look like some prophet who saw past and future. Last time he had mentioned the theory that amnesia meant a new soul entering the body; who knew where he picked up such a plausible idea?
Settling her thoughts, Qianli Xun shot back, "Why does Prince Yi keep harping on that? Whoever I loved has nothing to do with you."
Stepping closer still, Shen Jianchen gathered his nerve. "I want to know who Qianli Xun ever loved."
Dazed once more by a wave of uncanny familiarity, she breathed out three syllables without meaning to: "Shen... Jian... chen?"
A satisfied smile lit Shen Jianchen's face. "Is he the one you used to love?"
Trapped in that haze of familiarity, Qianli Xun searched Prince Yi's features for traces of Shen Jianchen, half deaf to his question.
Shen Jianchen went on: "No need to look at me like that. I am—"
Before he could finish, Qiao Mengrong's voice cut across the clearing: "Why is Brother here with Youqi Jushi?"
Qianli Xun snapped wide awake in an instant. Missing Shen Jianchen so much while standing beneath the Divine Willow must have made her hallucinate and mistake Prince Yi for him!
How on earth could Prince Yi be Shen Jianchen? Shen Jianchen's cocky swagger didn't fit a noble imperial prince at all. Blame Prince Yi for being such a smooth seducer that even her own self-control had wobbled today.
With the moment shattered by Qiao Mengrong, Shen Jianchen had no right to complain and turned around. "Mengrong, what brings you here?"
Spotting the bruise on his cheek, Qiao Mengrong gasped, "What happened to Brother's face? Does it hurt?"
"It's nothing. I bumped into a tree branch walking through the woods." Shen Jianchen tossed off an excuse.
Qiao Mengrong sensed at once that her arrival was unwelcome. "Doesn't Brother want to see me?"
Shen Jianchen did not know what to say; he hated lying to her.
When he offered no denial, tears welled in Qiao Mengrong's eyes. "Brother really didn't want to see me?"
Seeing the impasse, Qianli Xun stepped in and bowed to Princess Jiahe. "Please don't misunderstand Prince Yi, Princess. I came to the Divine Willow today to check on my red ribbon before heading to Youlin Retreat to see Wenyuan, only to find every ribbon gone! Just as I was about to go ask the nunnery what happened, Prince Yi happened to walk up. Since we know each other, we chatted a bit, and Prince Yi was just talking about you..."
Qiao Mengrong brightened in an instant. "Really?"
Qianli Xun kept spinning: "Of course! Prince Yi said the greatest fortune of his life was meeting Princess Jiahe, and he even invited me to your wedding."
Shen Jianchen thought, "Crossing over sure sharpened your lying skills. Do you really think fooling Mengrong is for her own good?"
Qiao Mengrong stepped up beside him, a single tear rolling from the corner of her eye. "Brother, do you really think meeting me is the greatest fortune of your life?"
Still silent, Shen Jianchen pulled out a handkerchief and wiped the tear from her cheek. Qiao Mengrong's arrival reminded him yet again that he had no right to love Qianli Xun.
Finishing, he glanced up at the rising moon and realized dusk was closing in.
"Is Youqi Jushi still going to see Wenyuan?"
Without missing a beat, Qianli Xun answered, "I'd better head back to Zhu Manor."
Qiao Mengrong blinked. "Didn't you plan to see Young Master Qiao? Why not go?"
With Prince Yi wrecking her mood, Qianli Xun feared Wenyuan would notice something off. "I did mean to see Wenyuan, then remembered my father wants to talk to me tonight. I don't want to get back late and upset him."
"As Youqi Jushi wishes." With that, Shen Jianchen took Qiao Mengrong's hand and walked away.
Having parted from Bai Feng, Kuqiao walked back from the far side of the pond.
Watching Prince Yi and Princess Jiahe disappear down the path, Qianli Xun sighed. "Cherish what you have in front of you! Stop stirring up trouble when you're bored! I swear I will never meet you alone again—mark my words!"
#CH 104
#TITLE: A Sudden Turn of Fortune
Time flew past, and over a month slipped by. The air grew brisk, and women across Lin'an changed into autumn attire, Qianli Xun included.
For over a month, her days had rolled along smooth as silk: when not with her family, she was out on dates with Wenyuan or attending poetry salons with him.
Since that meeting beneath the Divine Willow, she had not seen Prince Yi once. He seemed to be avoiding her on purpose, which suited Qianli Xun just fine.
That was more like it! Her face-to-face lecture must have worked wonders. Turning a royal prince around—Qianli Xun felt pretty impressed with herself.
Even better, she had not run into Chen Haoyu once at Zhu Manor all month. She had visited her sister-in-law several times without spotting him, nor had she crossed paths with him on the streets; he seemed to have vanished from her life overnight. Qianli Xun thanked her lucky stars—Heaven must be skimming the trash out of her life.
Yet when days went too smooth, unease crept in, and she kept waiting for the other shoe to drop.
With Mid-Autumn Festival nearing, Qiao Shouren made up his mind at last to propose at Zhu Manor. He spent five days preparing betrothal gifts, instructed the matchmaker, fixed a date, and sent an invitation card over to Zhu Manor.
Seeing the card, Zhu Xiyan grinned from ear to ear.
When the appointed day arrived, the Zhu Manor servants scrubbed the estate spotless from front to back, and Zhu Xiyan invited his clansmen over. Yet they waited and waited until noon, with no sign of the Qiao family.
Madam Xiang fretted: "Husband, shouldn't we send someone to Qiao Manor to check what happened?"
Zhu Xiyan nodded and dispatched a servant to inquire.
No sooner had the servant stepped out the side gate of Zhu Manor than he ran into the Qiao family's matchmaker. Grabbing her, he asked, "The Qiao family promised to come propose today—why aren't they here yet? The whole house is waiting on pins and needles!"
The matchmaker said, "What proposal? Since you're here, you'll save me a trip facing the Prefect. Pass on word to His Lordship: Miss Zhu's reputation outside is ruined, so the Qiao family won't be coming to propose anymore."
The servant seized her arm. "You matchmaker—how dare you slander our Young Lady! I'm dragging you straight to my master!"
Wrenching free, the matchmaker bolted, leaving the servant no choice but to return and report the truth.
Zhu Xiyan slammed the table in fury: "The Qiao family has gone too far! They asked to propose, picked the date, stood us up, and cooked up this excuse! Don't think being imperial relatives makes them gods—I'm the Prefect of Lin'an, and my word still rules this city!"
Madam Xiang sat weeping beside him. How had such a happy day fallen apart in an instant?
Matron Wei urged, "Don't lose your temper yet, Master—wait until we get to the bottom of this. Even if the Qiao family did hear wild gossip and backed out, it only proves they're shortsighted and gullible, ready to swallow any rumor. I wouldn't feel safe letting our Young Lady marry into a house like that anyway."
Just then Zhu Jingyuan walked in from outside, stepped up to his father, and whispered into his ear.
Zhu Xiyan's face went dark as thunder. "Go tell the clansmen to head home."
Glancing at Madam Xiang dabbing her eyes, Zhu Jingyuan shook his head and went out to handle it.
Zhu Xiyan snapped, "What are you crying for? Look at the fine daughter you raised!"
Madam Xiang sobbed all the harder: "Waaah! Why is my Shuzhen's fate so bitter! A mutual divorce from the Lin family, and now treated like this by the Qiaos! Waaah!"
Matron Wei steadied her. "Don't cry, Madam. Master knows what to do—let me help you back to your room."
Word that the Qiao family had canceled the proposal spread across Zhu Manor in no time, and Kuqiao rushed in to tell her mistress.
Qianli Xun's brush slipped from her fingers onto the paper. "Why hasn't the Qiao family come yet?"
"Servants are saying it's because Miss's reputation outside is ruined." Outrage burned on Kuqiao's face. "Pure nonsense! Miss is beautiful and kind—the Qiao family must just be making up excuses because they didn't want to propose!"
Hearing the Qiao family would not propose, Xiaguo wept harder than her mistress, slumping over the tea table: "Waaah! Does this mean Miss can't marry into the Qiao family anymore?! Waaah!"
With no energy to mind Xiaguo, Qianli Xun racked her brains over how her reputation had been ruined. Could it be that line, "falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed"?
"Kuqiao, go fetch Eldest Brother. He's bound to know the full story."
After Kuqiao left, Xiaguo kept sobbing. Qianli Xun said, "Who knew you'd take it harder than me? Fine—cry all the tears I'm supposed to cry so I can think straight about what to do next."
Xiaguo still would not stop, fraying Qianli Xun's nerves. "Go back to your room to cry. Eldest Brother will be here in a minute to talk to me."
Still sobbing, Xiaguo rose, returned to the maids' quarters, flung herself onto her bed, and wept even harder: "If Miss can't marry Young Master Qiao, what am I going to do?! Waaah!"
Before long, Zhu Jingyuan entered and told Kuqiao to shut the door.
Her brother's grim manner tightened the knot in Qianli Xun's chest. "Brother, what happened?"
"Don't take it too hard when you hear this, Sister. It looks like things between you and Qiao Wenxian are over." Zhu Jingyuan tried to tread with care, yet the truth hurt no matter how gentle he made it.
"What... what did the Qiao family hear... to call off the proposal out of nowhere?" For the first time, Qianli Xun heard her own voice tremble.
"Did you write a certain line of ci?"
Just as she feared. Qianli Xun nodded, steadying herself. "I did, back when Wenyuan and I were together. I wrote it for him."
"Nobody cares about that. Everyone is condemning you for lacking wifely virtue and corrupting morals—even calling you unchaste..."
"Does Brother think I'm to blame too?"
"My opinion doesn't matter anymore. The point is everyone thinks so. Stop writing poetry and ci from now on, Sister, or you'll grow old alone inside Zhu Manor."
Qianli Xun remembered how the historical Zhu Shuzhen had died of grief inside Zhu Manor. Was her life doomed to the same end? Never! She pushed herself to her feet. "Don't worry, Brother. I'll never let myself die an old maid inside Zhu Manor."
With that, she started for the door.
"Where are you going?"
She did not even know—she just needed out of Zhu Manor. "For a walk."
"Don't go out! The streets are overflowing with nasty rumors about you."
"Rumors?" Holding back the tears pressing at her eyes, Qianli Xun spun around. "One line of ci wasn't enough—there are rumors too?"
"Yes."
"Tell me what they're saying."
"Better not. Stay indoors and don't go anywhere. Father is furious right now—don't cross his path either."
"If Brother won't tell me, I'll go out and hear for myself."
"Fine, I'll tell you! Folk all over town are saying you're a tigress, that you had affairs while married to the Lin family and traded secret glances with Lin'an's scholars, and that you deserve to be pointed at by a thousand fingers and spat on by ten thousand..."
Fury exploded in Qianli Xun. She slammed her fist down on the chair beside her; the wood cracked clean through, scraping her knuckles raw.
Kuqiao rushed forward and grabbed her hand. "Miss, are you hurt?!"
"See what I mean? You're this furious indoors—step outside and you'll punch the first person you see, proving the 'tigress' rumors right! Listen to Brother: stay put and don't go anywhere. Father and I will handle this." Trying to comfort her, Zhu Jingyuan drew a bottle of salve from his sleeve. "I happened to buy medicine. Use this first."
Kuqiao took the bottle, and once Eldest Master left, sat her mistress down and applied the salve.
Seeing her mistress neither weep nor rage frightened Kuqiao even more, and the more scared she got, the faster she talked.
"Don't worry, Miss. Rumors come fast and fade fast. Young Master Qiao's heart belongs to you—if the Qiao family didn't come today, they'll come another day. I'll stay right here with you and wait."
When her mistress stayed silent, Kuqiao rattled on: "Back in our village, folks once spread rumors I wasn't my mother's real child, claiming she picked me up beside a ditch. My family paid it no mind, and after a while everyone got bored and forgot. I swear, within half a month nobody will breathe another word—"
Before Kuqiao could finish, a throng of servants burst through the door, sweeping manuscripts off the desk and poems and paintings out of wooden boxes, ransacking chests and drawers and hauling out every sheet of paper with ink on it into the yard.
Frozen in shock, Qianli Xun stood watching them like a band of looters stripping away every poem and painting of Zhu Shuzhen's.
Kuqiao barked, "Have you all lost your minds?! How dare you ransack Miss's room!"
One servant said, "Forgive us, Miss. Master ordered us to haul every single one of your manuscripts out into the yard and burn them."
"Burn them?" Qianli Xun repeated the words under her breath. In her memory, Zhu Shuzhen's manuscripts were burned only after she died of heartbreak past the age of forty.
Yet here she was, crossed over at just twenty, watching it happen right now. Could today be the turning point where Zhu Shuzhen's fate ended and Qianli Xun's true fate began?
#CH 105
#TITLE: Burning the Manuscripts and Facing Family Punishment
Stripped bare from the room, the manuscripts lay heaped in the courtyard. A servant pulled out a tinder tube, blew a spark into flame, and bent to light the pile—only for Kuqiao to dash forward and knock it straight from his hand. The spark hit the cold stone tiles and died at once.
"These manuscripts are years of Miss's heart and soul! How dare you burn them like trash?"
Xiaguo ran out of the maids' quarters and threw herself in front of the stack. "If you want to burn the manuscripts, burn me first!"
Confronted by the two maids, the servants hesitated to move.
One urged, "Blocking us won't help. Master's orders—every poem Miss ever wrote has to burn today, not a single piece spared. Our hands are tied."
The burning could not be stopped, and Qianli Xun had no wish to stop it. Whatever was meant to survive would survive anyway.
Burning books and burying scholars had never wiped out Confucianism; how could one bonfire erase Zhu Shuzhen's verse? The beauty of words was how they etched themselves into memory, passing from mind to mind like an undying flame.
Qianli Xun stepped out of the room, eerily calm. "Step aside, you two. If it's Father's wish, let them burn."
Kuqiao and Xiaguo moved away from the mound and stood beside their mistress as the servant retrieved the tinder tube, blew it to life, and touched flame to paper. Fire caught with a fierce crackle, leaping higher by the second.
Watching sheet after sheet crumble into ash, a strange hope took root inside Qianli Xun—as if Zhu Shuzhen's tragic fate were being devoured by the flames, leaving only Qianli Xun's own path behind.
Just as she felt a dark rush of relief watching paper turn to ash, Zhu Xiyan stormed into the yard and slapped her hard across the face.
"I taught you poetry and ci from childhood—not so you could drag our family honor through the mud! And look what you've done: writing lewd lines, ruining your own name, and leaving the entire Zhu family mocked by all of Lin'an! Who will ever come propose at Zhu Manor now?! As Prefect of Lin'an, I can't even show my face before officials or commoners because of you!"
Cupping her stinging cheek, Qianli Xun offered no resistance. This storm had to be weathered; once it passed, it would be over.
Kuqiao and Xiaguo trembled for their mistress, yet before their master's wrath neither dared utter a peep.
"Haha! Burn them! Burn every last scrap—leave not a single character!" Staring into the fire, Zhu Xiyan laughed a bitter laugh, then barked at the servants, "Go! Haul out everything in her room tied to writing poetry and ci and burn it all!"
A servant hesitated: "Master, even the brushes?"
Zhu Xiyan roared, "Burn them! Drag out every book in the room too!"
The servants filed back inside, hauling out brushes and every single book to hurl into the blaze.
Lowering her hand from her cheek, Qianli Xun murmured in her heart: "Burn it all. Burn everything that belonged to Zhu Shuzhen."
Zhu Xiyan thundered, "Kneel!"
Against her will, Qianli Xun dropped to her knees, with Kuqiao and Xiaguo kneeling right beside her.
Zhu Xiyan ordered, "Bring the whip!"
Only then did Qianli Xun notice a servant carrying a whip trailing behind Zhu Xiyan. At the command, the servant stepped forward and offered it.
Zhu Xiyan gripped the handle. "You two, get out of the way!"
Terrified, Kuqiao and Xiaguo scrambled to their feet and backed off.
Putting all his strength into his arm, Zhu Xiyan swung the whip down with ruthless force...
A searing stripe of fire tore across Qianli Xun's back.
Just as he raised his arm for a second strike, Matron Wei hurried in supporting Madam Xiang, who was sobbing her heart out.
"Waaah! Husband! How can Shuzhen survive a whipping like this? Please spare her! Waaah!"
Zhu Xiyan brought the whip slashing down a second time...
Madam Xiang threw herself straight over her daughter's back. "Waaah! If you want to beat her, beat me to death first! Lin Jue already beat our old daughter into amnesia—won't you even leave me this daughter who came back to us?! Waaah!"
Zhu Xiyan's arm froze in midair. If his daughter had never lost her memory, perhaps she would never have written "falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed."
He flung the whip to the ground and told Madam Xiang, "Get up. I have words for Shuzhen."
Matron Wei helped Madam Xiang onto a rattan chair, where she kept weeping and dabbing her eyes.
Gazing down at his daughter kneeling in silent pain, Zhu Xiyan remembered the day she had thrown her arms around his neck and kissed his cheek—the moment he had accepted this changed daughter with his whole heart.
Yet she had written shameless lines, earning the scorn of all Lin'an and dragging the Zhu name into the dirt. Keeping her under his roof would taint every generation to come.
"Leave, Shuzhen. Never set foot in Zhu Manor again. From this day on, the Zhu family has no daughter like you."
Though Qianli Xun had not spent long in Zhu Manor, real family warmth had grown there. Hearing Zhu Shuzhen's father cast her out sent reluctant tears spilling down her cheeks.
Madam Xiang panicked: "Husband! If you throw Shuzhen out, how will she survive all alone?! Waaah!"
Eyes glistening with tears, Qianli Xun spoke with firm resolve: "Rest easy, Father. Starting today, I move out of Zhu Manor. Even if Zhu Manor disowns me, in my heart this will always be my home, and you, Mother, Brother, and Sister-in-law will always be my family."
Her words made Madam Xiang sob even harder: "Waaah! Why is my Shuzhen's life so cruel?! Waaah!"
Swallowing back his own tears, Zhu Xiyan strode toward the gate, tossing one parting order over his shoulder: "Be out of Zhu Manor before dark, or I'll have your belongings dumped in the street!"
The other servants trailed out of the courtyard behind him.
The fire kept crackling, sinking lower as the heap of ash grew deeper.
Leaning on her two maids, Qianli Xun struggled to her feet and put on a brave, easy front.
"Don't cry, Mother. Two lashes from Father are nothing. Besides, I'm not going far—I'll still be right here in Lin'an. Anytime Mother wants to see me, just come to Wangyue Pavilion."
Matron Wei added, "Rest easy, Madam. With me watching over her, our Young Lady won't live poorly outside Zhu Manor."
"I'm not worried about her livelihood—her old dowry is plenty, and I can hand over more estates and shops if need be. I just worry about her marriage, with no elders left to stand behind her!" Fresh grief shook Madam Xiang.
Her own heart aching from the sudden blow, Qianli Xun forced herself to comfort Madam Xiang. "Don't worry, Mother. A person doesn't come into this world just to get married—there's plenty else to do. If nobody wants me, I'll live on my own and do whatever makes me happy."
Madam Xiang wiped her eyes. "Easy to say, yet a woman is born to wed."
Matron Wei soothed her: "From now on, I'll serve as Young Lady's elder in place of you and Master. I'll keep her marriage close to heart."
Only then did Madam Xiang nod in relief, turning to the two maids: "Hurry up and help Miss indoors to put medicine on her back!"
Only after soothing Madam Xiang did sharp stings flare across Qianli Xun's back. Supported by both maids, she went inside for medicine.
Matron Wei helped Madam Xiang back to her quarters as well.
Helping her mistress out of her robe, Kuqiao stared at the two angry welts oozing blood across her back. "How could Master strike so hard?!"
Xiaguo brought over the salve bottle, her mind still stuck on Young Master Qiao. "Miss, what will happen with you and Young Master Qiao now?"
Too much had piled up today for Qianli Xun to sort out her future with Wenyuan yet. At Xiaguo's question, she paused and said, "Once my wounds heal, I'll meet Wenyuan and decide."
Taking the bottle, Kuqiao dabbed salve onto her mistress's back. "Young Master Qiao is the sole heir of the Qiao line. As long as he wants to be with you, his elders won't be able to stop him."
Xiaguo perked up at once: "Now that's what I love to hear, Sister Kuqiao!"
No sooner had the medicine been applied than a troop of maids filed in. Their leader said, "Miss, Master ordered us to help pack your things and see you out of Zhu Manor at once."
Xiaguo bristled: "Didn't Master say before dark? It's nowhere near dusk—what's your rush?!"
The lead maid replied, "Master's orders, Miss. We have to obey."
Zhu Xiyan was in a rush to throw her out just to protect the Zhu name: once all of Lin'an saw the family expel an "unvirtuous" daughter, folk would praise the Zhu household's strict standards and spare future generations from gossip.
Qianli Xun found it rich with irony. She had followed Master Jingguang's "Right Thought, Right Mindfulness" to the letter, never doing a single immoral deed—yet here she was, slandered for loose conduct and thrown out of her own father's house.
Where had it all gone wrong? One line—"falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed"—could never carry that kind of blast radius on its own. Common folk didn't follow poetry; that line would have stayed among literati, not swept the whole city.
The real weapon was the smear campaign, spun up by someone with a grudge—someone who hated her enough to invent filth out of thin air.
At the word "hate," Chen Haoyu popped straight into her head. He was the one person she had clashed with since crossing over. Could he be behind the rumors?
Then she remembered Chen Haoyu's parting taunt—promising to send her a "big gift" before long...
"Young Lady, what are you thinking about?" the lead maid asked.
Qianli Xun snapped back. "Oh, nothing. If it's my father's orders, go ahead and pack."
The maids busied themselves emptying chests and wardrobes without needing Kuqiao or Xiaguo to lift a finger. For the first time, the two personal maids stood idle while others packed their mistress's clothes—learning for the first time that not every spell of idleness felt sweet.
#CH 106
#TITLE: Leaving Zhu Manor Amid Family Warmth
Once the maids finished packing, they stacked bundles and wooden chests around the room and called male servants to haul everything out to carriages waiting beyond the gate.
Unwilling to be hurried by the maids any longer, Qianli Xun walked out toward the gate on her own—reluctant to leave, yet resolute all the same.
Burning the manuscripts was supposed to happen after Zhu Shuzhen's soul slipped away—her final exit from Zhu Manor through death.
Now the burning had come early, and so had Qianli Xun's departure. Only by walking away could she step onto the destiny that belonged to Qianli Xun.
Eyeing her mistress's scraped knuckles and picturing the whip marks across her back, Kuqiao wondered which deity on earth her poor mistress had offended to get hurt twice in a single day.
"Once we settle in, Miss, go burn incense at the nunnery to pray for protection—or go see Master Jingguang again."
Qianli Xun saw right through Kuqiao: feeling sorry for her mistress and out of ideas, she was grasping at incense sticks in a pinch.
Kuqiao's mention of Master Jingguang reminded Qianli Xun of the Old Ancestor. Ever since returning to Zhu Manor, she had stayed away out of respect for the elder's quiet devotion. Leaving now, she ought to say goodbye.
Halfway down the path, both maids watched their mistress stop in her tracks and turn back.
Kuqiao asked, "Miss, where are you—"
"I'm going to bid the Old Ancestor farewell."
Without looking back, Qianli Xun headed straight for the Buddhist shrine.
Both maids hurried after her.
Qianli Xun knew every path in Zhu Manor by heart now. Just as she had grown used to calling it home, she had to leave—it was hard not to feel a tug of sorrow.
Maybe her crossed-over life was meant to be a drifting one: from Lin Manor back to Zhu Manor, from Zhu Manor to Wangyue Pavilion, and who knew where after that. Still, anywhere beat dying; as long as she drew breath, hope remained.
Qianli Xun treasured this second shot at life Heaven had handed her. She meant to make every step count and face whatever storm came head-on.
Lost in thought, she reached the Buddhist shrine. From inside came the steady clack of a wooden fish, unchanged from her very first visit.
Kuqiao knocked on the door for a long while before it swung open.
Matron Xiuzhen stood there again, though this time she did not shoo them away. "Ah, it's the Young Lady. What brings you here?"
Qianli Xun stepped forward. "I'm leaving Zhu Manor, perhaps for good. I wanted to see the Old Ancestor before I go."
"Wait a moment, Young Lady." Matron Xiuzhen shut the door again.
Moments later, the clacking of the wooden fish halted, then resumed after a brief pause.
The door opened again and Matron Xiuzhen stepped out. "The Old Ancestor says there's no need to meet, and wishes you a safe journey. She asked me to leave you with these words: life is only a journey from birth to death, and no road has just one waystation. At whichever station you reach, remember to pause and enjoy the scenery."
Both solace and timeless wisdom—gratitude swelled in Qianli Xun's chest. "Shuzhen takes her teaching to heart. My thanks to the Old Ancestor."
The shrine door closed once more. A deep certainty told Qianli Xun she might never see the Old Ancestor again. Remembering how the old woman had reached out to save her at her darkest hour, she dropped to her knees and kowtowed.
Both maids knelt and kowtowed alongside their mistress.
Outside Zhu Manor's side gate waited four carriages: two loaded with bundles and trunks, two for passengers.
Stepping outside, Qianli Xun saw neither her mother nor her brother and sister-in-law—only Matron Wei waiting there. No doubt Zhu Xiyan had forbidden them from seeing her off.
Matron Wei hurried up. "With Master inside, Madam dared not come out. Once you're settled in, Madam will slip over to Wangyue Pavilion to visit you."
Qianli Xun nodded and climbed into the carriage.
Both maids rode with her while Matron Wei took the carriage behind.
The wheels began to turn, carrying Qianli Xun step by step away from Zhu Manor—the place her heart had come to call home. The Old Ancestor was right: even home was just a waystation, and to reach the next, one had to leave the last behind.
As the carriage rolled through the streets, Qianli Xun paid no heed to the bustle outside; her thoughts clung to Wenyuan. With the Qiao family calling off the proposal, how was he holding up? Was he heartbroken, or pleading with his parents right now?
Master Jingguang had once foretold she would walk several paths of love. Were she and Wenyuan destined to meet without being meant to stay? The thought deepened her ache.
Through the grief, a quiet vow hardened in her chest: whatever the outcome, she would not give up without a fight. She would fight for her happiness so that even if it came to nothing, she would never look back in regret for failing to try.
The carriage came to a halt. Kuqiao lifted the curtain. "Why did we stop?"
Before the driver could reply, she spotted Zhu Jingyuan and Chen Wenxin outside and dropped the curtain. "Miss, Eldest Master and Young Madam are out there!"
Never expecting her brother and sister-in-law to come see her off, Qianli Xun hopped down at once. After greeting them, they drew her into a quiet side lane.
Chen Wenxin said, "Your brother and I didn't dare see you off at Zhu Manor's gate for fear Father would bring down the rod on us. So we made an excuse to slip out and intercept you halfway—to send you off and have a few words."
"Shuzhen is grateful beyond words for Brother and Sister-in-law's kindness."
Zhu Jingyuan said, "What's there to thank us for? We're family."
"You two still claim me? Aren't you afraid I've ruined the Zhu family's name?"
Chen Wenxin laughed. "Others might not know you, but don't I? Outsiders are talking rubbish. As for that line of ci you wrote, I love it! I memorized the whole lyric—want me to recite it for you?"
"Didn't Sister-in-law say you never liked my ci? Why take a shine to this one?"
"This one's different—it showed me a new side of you, bold and fiery! Your brother and I came to tell you: even if Father threw you out of Zhu Manor, we—and Mother too—are still your family. Anytime you need anything, come straight to us."
Deeply moved, Qianli Xun bowed again. "With the whole city spitting on my name, having Brother and Sister-in-law stand by me makes me..." Words failed her; before true family love, speech felt pale and thin.
Chen Wenxin caught her arms. "Your brother and I didn't come out here to make you bow. As for those filthy rumors, they make my blood boil! While waiting for you, we heard some idiot gossiping and I tore him a new one. Don't take any of it to heart—it's all idle chatter. You're famous in Lin'an, so the tiniest breeze gets blown up and twisted beyond recognition."
Hearing her sister-in-law's warmth made Qianli Xun feel guilty for ever suspecting Chen Haoyu.
"Don't worry, Sister-in-law, I won't let rumors get to me. An upright tree casts a straight shadow, and time reveals every heart. As long as I walk a straight path, people will see the truth one day."
Zhu Jingyuan signaled a servant standing nearby. "Bring it over."
The servant stepped up and presented a wooden box.
Taking it, Zhu Jingyuan handed it to his sister. "Take this. Father told me to give it to you."
"Father?" Qianli Xun accepted the box. "What's inside?"
Zhu Jingyuan explained, "With rumors flooding the city and the Qiao family calling off the proposal, tongues are bound to wag harder. Father had to throw you out to protect the family name, yet it broke his heart too. He told me to give you these land and property deeds."
Qianli Xun could not make heads or tails of this father. "He forbids you and Sister-in-law from seeing me off, then sends you after me with deeds? Doesn't he contradict himself?"
Zhu Jingyuan said, "Try to understand his bind. If we saw you off at the gate, he'd have to punish us—that's why we made an excuse to meet you here, and I bet Father counted on it."
"What a hypocrite!"
"Don't speak of Father like that."
"My old dowry is more than enough—why hand me these? I don't want them." Even knowing Zhu Xiyan's dilemma, stubborn pride flared in Qianli Xun, and she pushed the box back into her brother's arms.
Zhu Jingyuan pressed it right back into hers. "Why turn away Father's goodwill? Throwing you out already tore him up; if you refuse this, his conscience will weigh even heavier."
"So Father only sent these to ease his own guilt? Fine, I'll take them. Since I call him Father, putting his mind at ease counts as filial duty." Qianli Xun accepted the box and passed it to Kuqiao. "Keep it safe."
Zhu Jingyuan added, "Father also said he's sending men to hunt down whoever started these rumors. He suspects someone is targeting the Zhu family's reputation on purpose—once caught, he won't show mercy."
Just as she was about to dismiss her suspicion of Chen Haoyu, her brother's words stirred it right back up. Even her father suspected foul play.
Yet with zero proof and a sister-in-law who treated her like gold, she could never voice that suspicion aloud—better to let her father investigate. If it did trace back to Chen Haoyu, relations between the Zhu and Chen families would turn ugly, leaving her sister-in-law trapped in the middle.
Qianli Xun glanced at Chen Wenxin, whose warm smile melted every ounce of resentment inside her. Maybe she was overthinking things; with no proof, that troublemaker Chen Haoyu might have nothing to do with it at all.
"Tell Father I'm grateful for his trouble."
Bidding her brother and sister-in-law farewell, Qianli Xun boarded the carriage once more.
Kuqiao passed the wooden box to Matron Wei in the rear carriage, then climbed in beside her mistress.
Opening the box to find a thick stack of land and house deeds, Matron Wei sighed, "Master may look hard on the outside, yet he still cares for our Young Lady inside."
The carriages rolled onward through the bustling streets, yet to Qianli Xun all the clamor outside felt quieter than the storm in her chest.
#CH 107
#TITLE: The Marriage Proposal Called Off
The Qiao household had prepared everything for the proposal, rising at crack of dawn to get busy. Betrothal gifts stood arrayed in neat rows across the courtyard, and young clansmen had gathered to accompany the family to Zhu Manor.
Qiao Wenxian had hurried back to Qiao Manor at dawn too—today was the big day for him and Shuzhen, and he wouldn't miss going with his family for the world.
Just as everything stood ready for departure, Concubine Mi rushed back from outside in a fluster, claiming she had urgent news. She ushered Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian indoors and had a maid shut the door.
Qiao Wenxian frowned in puzzlement. What on earth had happened? Would it disrupt his happy day with Shuzhen?
Inside, Qiao Shouren snapped, "What kind of stunt are you pulling now? Sulking because I wouldn't let you come to Zhu Manor? I already explained it isn't proper for you to go."
Concubine Mi retorted, "Master thinks too little of me! It's nothing like that! First thing this morning, my maid told me Miss Zhu's name is mud out on the streets—the whole city is gossiping about her! Because everyone heard the Qiao family was proposing to the Zhus, nobody dared tell us. I didn't believe it, so I went out for a turn myself, and sure enough, every lane, teahouse, and tavern is buzzing about her!"
Madam Qian gasped in alarm, "What are they saying about Miss Zhu?"
"Pure filth!" Concubine Mi laid out every word she had heard.
Qiao Shouren's face darkened to ash, and he said not a word.
Madam Qian said, "So many clansmen showed up today, yet none of them breathed a word."
"Wait right here, Sister and Master." Concubine Mi rose, opened the door, and instructed a maid, "Go fetch one of the younger clansmen, and summon Young Master too."
Moments later, Qiao Wenxian walked in with a young kinsman, and the maid shut the door behind them.
Baffled, Qiao Wenxian asked, "Father, Mother, Mama—why are you hiding indoors instead of setting off for Zhu Manor to propose?"
None of the elders replied, leaving Qiao Wenxian standing there.
The young kinsman, barely in his teens, sized up the room and asked, "Has Uncle heard the rumors about Miss Zhu?"
A cold chill struck Qiao Wenxian. "Rumors? What rumors?"
The boy said, "Brother Wenyuan doesn't know? Miss Zhu wrote a line—'falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed'—and it's spread all over Lin'an. Everyone's talking about it."
Qiao Wenxian laughed. "I thought it was something serious! I know that line—I was right beside Shuzhen when she wrote it. She wrote it for me!"
With a sharp crack, Qiao Shouren slammed the table. "Such a wanton woman! How could she ever marry into the Qiao family?!"
Qiao Wenxian panicked: "Father, it's just one line of ci! How can you judge her character from that? Besides, she wrote it while with me—I know every detail of what happened..."
Concubine Mi broke in: "Son, that's not the half of it! You haven't gone out to hear how folk are tearing her apart—it's so vile I can't even repeat it!"
Qiao Shouren turned to the young kinsman: "Do the rest of the clan know what folk are saying about Miss Zhu?"
The boy nodded. "Every single one."
Qiao Shouren barked, "Why didn't anyone tell me?! You almost let me make a disastrous mistake!"
The boy explained in a small voice, "Uncle's house is imperial kin, Master Zhu is the Prefect of Lin'an, and everyone heard Prince Yi acted as go-between. Who would dare bring that up to Uncle?"
In a grim voice, Qiao Shouren ordered, "Go out and tell everyone the proposal to the Zhu family is canceled. Send them all home."
Before the boy could leave, Qiao Wenxian cried out, "Father! You already gave your word—how can you go back on it?!"
Qiao Shouren roared, "Why can't I go back on it?! Do you want to bring a woman of loose morals into this house?!"
Terrified, the boy slipped out the door and shut it behind him.
"Father wouldn't even give Prince Yi face?" Qiao Wenxian tried playing Prince Yi's card again.
"Of course I give Prince Yi face—yet when Prince Yi persuaded me, these rumors hadn't broken out! Had I heard them back then, I would have staked my old life before letting you marry Miss Zhu!"
Last night Qiao Wenxian had lain awake from pure joy, leaping up at dawn to hurry home for the proposal. He had thought it was a done deal—never dreaming it would crash down like this.
Qiao Wenxian dropped to his knees. "Father, don't believe those rumors! I know Shuzhen better than anyone—someone must be slandering her..."
Qiao Shouren would hear none of it, snapping back: "Slandering her? Flies don't buzz around a seamless egg! If she's spotless, why would folk talk about her out of nowhere? Clearly the fault lies with her! Go back to the retreat and cut off all contact with Miss Zhu. Disobey me, and I'll lock you inside Qiao Manor until we pick another bride and get you wed!"
Qiao Wenxian begged, "Mother! Mama! Say a word for me—persuade Father! Shuzhen is a good girl!"
Determined to make her son give up, Concubine Mi asked, "Son, do you even know what people outside are saying about Miss Zhu?"
Qiao Wenxian stood firm: "I don't care what outsiders say—all that matters is that I know she's good."
Concubine Mi hardened her heart: "If that's your attitude, then hear it for yourself! Everyone out there says she traded secret glances with other men and had affairs before divorcing Lin Jue, sprawled in a man's arms in public to write 'falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed,' and acted like a tigress to force the Lin family into divorce! Tsk, listen to that—how could the Qiao family ever marry a woman like that?"
"Pure lies! Don't believe a word of it, Mama!"
Concubine Mi urged, "Son, you left Lin'an right after Miss Zhu wed, and she divorced right after you returned—how could you know what happened? Be good, stop making a scene, listen to your father, and stay away from Miss Zhu."
"Mother, please put in a word for me with Father!"
Madam Qian let out a sigh. "Wenyuan, none of us three wanted this match in the first place since she had been divorced. We only went along to give Prince Yi face. You saw we were ready to go propose, yet who knew her reputation was this foul? Blame fate—you two aren't meant to be under one roof. Let her go."
With all three elders united against him, Qiao Wenxian saw no amount of pleading would move them.
Rising to his feet, he bowed. "Father, Mother, Mama—I'll return to Youlin Retreat right now. Don't dream of making me marry anyone else, though. In this life, I'll marry Shuzhen or no one at all!"
With that, he flung open the door and strode out.
The betrothal gifts still stood lined up in the courtyard, yet the guests had dispersed, leaving the festive display looking like a mirage. Qiao Wenxian felt trapped in a nightmare—how else could it feel so unreal?
Only when the matchmaker appeared before him—"Young Master Qiao, is the proposal really called off?"—did reality crash home.
He gripped the matchmaker's arm. "You're a matchmaker, skilled at smoothing things over—go inside and help convince my father to propose to the Zhu family!"
The matchmaker replied, "For days I debated whether to tell Master Qiao about the gossip surrounding Miss Zhu, never daring to speak up. Thank goodness he found out in time, or my conscience wouldn't have let me sleep arranging this match."
"Even you believe it? I'll go out on the streets right now and see who's spreading these lies!" Cold water doused Qiao Wenxian's heart. Releasing her arm, he bolted out of Qiao Manor.
Worried for his master, Nan Shuang chased after him.
Just as the matchmaker turned to head inside, Qiao Shouren stepped out.
"Trouble yourself to go to Zhu Manor and tell the Prefect that with Miss Zhu's reputation in tatters, the Qiao family cannot presume to aim so high."
The matchmaker hesitated: "What if the Prefect has me beaten with boards?"
"You're only delivering a message—furious as the Prefect may be, he still listens to reason. Go on, you'll be paid well, and I'll still need your help with Wenyuan's future match." Qiao Shouren tossed her an ingot of silver. "For your trouble."
Catching the silver, the matchmaker agreed at once. "I'll head straight to Zhu Manor and deliver your message."
Outside Qiao Manor, Nan Shuang watched his master shun the carriage and stride toward the busiest streets, muttering as he walked: "All lies! Who's gossiping about Shuzhen? They just didn't want to propose and cooked up an excuse!"
Nan Shuang hurried ahead to tug his sleeve. "Young Master, let's go back."
Qiao Wenxian seized Nan Shuang by both shoulders and shook him. "Nan Shuang, tell me you've never heard anyone gossiping about Shuzhen!"
"I'm with you all day, Young Master—if you haven't heard it, neither have I! If I had, I'd have told you."
"Look at all these people on the street! Everyone's busy enough making a living—who has spare time to gossip about others..."
Before he could finish, a woman buying vegetables nearby handed over her coins and said, "Heard the latest about the Prefect's daughter?"
The vegetable vendor replied, "Who hasn't? Everyone on this street knows!"
The buyer said, "I always thought highborn ladies had refinement and virtue—turns out the Prefect's daughter is no better than those women in the entertainment quarters."
"Right? What's her name again? Ah, Zhu Shuzhen!" The vendor shook her head. "Nice name, shame she hasn't an ounce of wifely virtue. Whoever marries her is in for misery."
An old man walked over and scolded them: "You women gather together and wag your tongues nonstop—careful His Lordship the Prefect hears and hauls you off for a beating!"
The buyer scoffed, "When does the Prefect ever set foot in a vegetable market? He'll never hear a word of our chatter."
"You old bachelor, what nerve lecturing us! Just yesterday you said the fouler her name gets, the better—when nobody wants her, you'll go propose, and the Prefect will toss his daughter to you like wilted greens! Cheap bargain for you, haha!" The vendor waved a rotting cabbage leaf under the old bachelor's nose.
#CH 108
#TITLE: Stepping Up for Wenyuan Once More
Unable to stomach another word, Qiao Wenxian stormed up, face dark with rage, and shouted, "Every word out of your mouths is garbage! Shuzhen isn't like that! Say one more word and I'll flip your vegetable stall!"
Terrified, the vendor shrank back: "No more! I won't say another word! Don't overturn my stall, Young Master!"
Clutching her basket, the buyer fled, muttering, "Where did this lunatic pop out from?"
Unfazed, the old bachelor sneered, "Looks like Young Master knows Zhu Shuzhen! Did she toss secret glances at you too? Fall asleep in your arms too?"
Qiao Wenxian seized the old bachelor by the collar. "Say one more word and I'll rip your mouth apart!"
Spittle flying, the old bachelor barked back, "What's the use bullying me? Every soul on this street is saying the same thing—go listen for yourself if you don't believe me!"
Qiao Wenxian whipped his head around in disgust. People on all sides were pointing at him, whispering behind their hands...
He could punch one man and curse a dozen, yet he could never silence a whole city's tongues. Heartbreak drained the strength from his fingers, and the old bachelor slipped free and ran.
Qiao Wenxian stumbled down the street, wilted like a frostbitten eggplant.
Were he and Shuzhen never going to be together? Would all his dreams of growing old beside her remain nothing but empty dreams?
He decided to go find Shuzhen—she had to be suffering inside Zhu Manor.
Nan Shuang urged him to hire a carriage, yet Qiao Wenxian refused; only walking until his legs gave out could numb the pain in his chest.
When he reached Zhu Manor, a gatekeeper informed him, "Young Master Qiao came too late. Master has already driven our Young Lady out of the house."
"Where did she go?"
The servant shook his head. "No idea."
Grief crashed over Qiao Wenxian. Cast out by her own family, how would Shuzhen get by? He could picture her walking out of Zhu Manor with tears streaming down her cheeks like rain on pear blossoms.
The more he imagined it, the sharper his chest ached. With her cast out and no elders to arrange their marriage, how could they ever be wed with honor?
Qiao Wenxian meant to go see the Prefect and swear his resolve to marry Shuzhen.
The gatekeeper blocked his path: "Master's orders—no one from the Qiao family is allowed inside."
Qiao Wenxian backed away with heavy steps. What could he do now? Only one person came to mind: Prince Yi. He had to beg him for help again.
He could not walk all the way back to Youlin Retreat on foot. With no Qiao carriage nearby, Nan Shuang hired a carriage on the street to carry them beyond the city gates.
All the way out, his master said not a single word.
Nan Shuang felt heartbroken for his master and Youqi Jushi too. He could not fathom why everyone was talking about her—and saying such ugly things.
"Young Master, could someone have spread those rumors on purpose to wreck the proposal?"
His master stayed silent, deaf to the world around him.
Nan Shuang kept guessing: "My bet is it's some girl who likes you, Young Master. If you proposed to Youqi Jushi, she'd lose her shot, so she cooked up this dirty trick."
Qiao Wenxian still did not answer. Whoever slandered Shuzhen didn't matter to him right now—all he cared about was what the two of them would do next.
"Blame it on you being too outstanding, Young Master, and the Qiao family being imperial kin—so many ladies have their eyes on you. Cheer up, this will blow over." Nan Shuang kept trying to comfort him as he speculated.
...
Ever since meeting Qianli Xun beneath the Divine Willow, Shen Jianchen had not stepped out of Youlin Retreat, keeping his walks to the grounds nearby, going no further than the willow.
Today was the Qiao family's proposal day, and Shen Jianchen discovered that hiding from Qianli Xun did nothing to help him forget her. With her marriage to Wenyuan about to become fact, the ache inside him only deepened.
Since Wenyuan left at dawn, Shen Jianchen had holed up in his room. At noon Bai Feng laid out lunch, yet he had zero appetite and never lifted his chopsticks, leaving Bai Feng to carry the dishes back untouched.
Qiao Mengrong stopped by several times, only to be turned away at the door. He could not bear letting her see him moping over another woman, and feared seeing her would push harsh words out of his mouth. He only claimed he was busy thinking through Lin'an's military and civil affairs and wanted no interruptions.
Just as Shen Jianchen sat nursing his own misery, the door flew open. Wenyuan stumbled in, dropped to his knees before him, and begged in a choked voice: "Prince Yi, help me—only you can help me..."
Startled, Shen Jianchen asked, "What happened? Isn't today the proposal?"
Wenyuan was too choked with grief to get the words out.
Nan Shuang spoke up for his master: "Prince Yi doesn't know—the streets are flooded with filthy rumors about Youqi Jushi! When Master found out, he called off the proposal to the Zhu family, and the Prefect even threw Youqi Jushi out of the house—nobody knows where she went!"
"What?!" Realization snapped Shen Jianchen straight to attention, fear for Qianli Xun surging through him. "Something like that happened?"
Weeping, Wenyuan cried, "Help me, Prince Yi! If I can't be with Shuzhen, what's the point of living?!"
Shen Jianchen stepped forward and hauled Wenyuan to his feet. "Pull yourself together. Stay here at the retreat while I find out what's going on and decide what to do."
Qiao Wenxian wrapped his arms around Prince Yi. "You have to persuade my father again! He only listens to you!"
Shen Jianchen reassured him: "I'm heading to Qiao Manor to see Grandfather right now."
Qiao Wenxian hugged him tighter. "You're the finest prince under heaven! Once this works out, I'll hang an inscribed plaque in your honor!"
"All right, let go of me—how am I supposed to get to Qiao Manor with you clinging to me?" Shen Jianchen thought something had to be twisted in his own head: half of him wanted to help Wenyuan, while the other half felt glad the proposal had fallen through.
Qiao Wenxian released him and wiped the tear streaks from his cheeks.
Shen Jianchen ordered, "Nan Shuang, take your master to rest, and go fetch Bai Feng."
Standing right outside the door, Bai Feng stepped in at once. "No need to fetch me, Your Highness—I've been waiting right outside."
"Hitch the carriage. We're going to Qiao Manor."
"Awesome! Your Highness is leaving the house at last! I get to step out of Youlin Retreat too!"
"Less chatter—get moving!"
Watching Prince Yi leave with Bai Feng, hope flickered back to life in Qiao Wenxian's eyes.
On the road to Qiao Manor, Bai Feng asked from the driver's box, "Your Highness, I haven't seen Kuqiao in over a month. Can I see her today?"
Not just Bai Feng—Shen Jianchen hadn't seen Qianli Xun in over a month either. With all this crashing down on her, how was she holding up? Nan Shuang said she'd been thrown out of Zhu Manor; where had she gone?
"Sure. Once we find Youqi Jushi, you'll see Kuqiao."
"Find her? Did Youqi Jushi go missing?"
"Stop asking questions and drive faster to Qiao Manor."
Bai Feng stuck out his tongue. Here was a prince who never visited the prefecture office and cared nothing for government business, yet threw himself into Young Master Qiao's romance—what an odd prince.
Once inside the city gates, Shen Jianchen went straight to Qiao Manor.
Hearing Prince Yi had arrived, Qiao Shouren led Madam Qian and Concubine Mi out to welcome him.
Shown into the main hall, Qiao Shouren beat Prince Yi to the punch: "Did Prince Yi come over the proposal?"
Shen Jianchen nodded. "Wenyuan told me it fell through. I came to ask Grandfather—are you calling off the proposal to Zhu Manor over nothing more than street rumors about Miss Zhu?"
Qiao Shouren answered, "Precisely. Last time Prince Yi talked me round, and the Qiao family agreed to the match with all our hearts. Who knew folk outside were spreading such foul talk about Miss Zhu? How could the Qiao house take in a bride of such disrepute?"
"Has Grandfather considered they're just rumors?"
"Of course I have—yet flies don't buzz around a seamless egg! Why didn't rumors target anyone else, only Miss Zhu? Clearly she brought it on herself."
Shen Jianchen heard the iron resolve in his grandfather's tone. To test how firm he was, he probed: "Wenyuan has eyes only for Youqi Jushi. He even said life has no meaning without her."
Concubine Mi panicked: "Master, if Wenyuan is talking like that, what do we do?!"
Qiao Shouren barked at her: "Know your place! When I'm speaking with Prince Yi, who gave you leave to butt in? Withdraw at once!"
Displeasure flashing across her face, Concubine Mi bowed to Prince Yi and left with her maid.
Only then did Qiao Shouren say, "Don't take Wenyuan's words to heart, Prince Yi. He's young and bound to talk like a lovesick fool; once he marries, he'll get over it."
Shen Jianchen pushed further: "What if I went to His Majesty and asked for an imperial marriage decree for Wenyuan..."
Before he could finish, Qiao Shouren shot up and dropped to his knees, with Madam Qian kneeling beside him.
"If Prince Yi asks for an imperial decree, I'll smash my head open right in front of you! Even at the cost of this old life, I will never let Miss Zhu step across the Qiao threshold!"
Shen Jianchen rose and let out a sigh. "Get up. This is Qiao family business—I won't meddle in it again."
Exhaling in relief, Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian climbed to their feet and called out to Prince Yi's retreating back in unison: "We humbly see Prince Yi off!"
#CH 109
#TITLE: Leaving Zhu Manor to Buy a Gift
With Qiao Shouren digging in his heels, further persuasion was pointless.
More important, Shen Jianchen did not want to keep persuading him. If Heaven itself threw roadblocks in front of this proposal, maybe Wenyuan was not Qianli Xun's final destination—maybe Shen Jianchen still had a shot.
With fresh hope stirring, Shen Jianchen told Bai Feng to drive to Zhu Manor.
After driving his daughter out, Zhu Xiyan had gone to the prefecture office. Sitting alone in his study, he mulled over whom to send after the rumor-mongers.
This was private Zhu family business—not something to mobilize official yamen runners for. Yet Zhu Manor's servants were good enough for waiting tables, useless for tracking down schemers.
Then Zhu Xiyan thought of Lin Jue: a former son-in-law, eager to please, and best of all, with soldiers under his command who could track leads with ease. Leaving the prefecture office, he headed straight for the Military Training Office.
Guided by a soldier to the Commandant's quarters, he ran right into the Vice Commander-in-Chief stepping out.
Seeing the Prefect arrive, the Vice Commander-in-Chief bowed with utmost deference. "Lord Prefect."
Zhu Xiyan exchanged pleasantries: "Long time no see. How has the Vice Commander-in-Chief been keeping?"
"Same as ever—buried in official duties, as Lord Prefect knows all too well."
"Indeed. Local officials like us serve the court; idle moments are scarce."
"May I ask what brings Lord Prefect here?"
"Oh, I have a word for Commandant Lin."
"Commandant Lin is right inside. Please go ahead, Lord Prefect—if you need anything at all, just send word to me."
Zhu Xiyan nodded in satisfaction. Even military officers outside his direct chain of command treated him with full respect.
Stepping into the Commandant's office, he found Lin Jue working at his desk. Looking up to see his former father-in-law, Lin Jue jumped up to greet him: "Lord Prefect's sudden visit is a pleasant surprise! Please take the seat of honor!"
