Zhu Xiyan felt pleased; his former son-in-law's biggest virtue was always his deference.
Once Zhu Xiyan sat, Lin Jue ordered a soldier to serve tea, then stood waiting at attention.
After the soldier served tea and left, Zhu Xiyan glanced at the guards still by the door.
Catching the hint that this was confidential, Lin Jue waved them off: "All of you, step outside."
Once they withdrew, Lin Jue shut the door and said with care, "All clear now. Please speak, Lord Prefect."
Zhu Xiyan offered congratulations first: "Only a short while since we met, and you've leaped from Training Officer to Commandant. Well done."
Lin Jue replied with modesty, "All thanks to the trust of the Military Commissioner and my superiors."
"The Military Commissioner was involved?" Zhu Xiyan blinked in surprise. Prince Yi never touched civil or military affairs—how had he backed Lin Jue's promotion?
Lin Jue smiled. "Lord Prefect wouldn't know. One day the Military Commissioner came by to address the troops, and I happened to be on hand to host him. The senior commanders hurried over to pay respects too; with the Commandant's post vacant, they all recommended me, and the Military Commissioner approved."
"Ah, so that's how it happened." Unwilling to linger on small talk, Zhu Xiyan moved to business. "I came to ask a favor of you."
"Say the word, Lord Prefect—anything within my power, I'll give my all." Even though Lin Jue had let Zhu Shuzhen go, he was not about to let go of his former father-in-law; nobody climbed in officialdom without backing.
Since he was asking a favor, Zhu Xiyan could not keep Lin Jue standing. "Come, sit down and talk."
Lin Jue sat down at once.
Once he was seated, Zhu Xiyan paused a beat before saying, "I came on a private matter. You must have heard the rumors about Shuzhen. I want you to send men to find out who started them."
Mention of the rumors stirred mixed feelings in Lin Jue. On one hand, the rumor-monger was despicable for dragging the Lin family into the mud too; on the other, Zhu Shuzhen had it coming—refusing to live a quiet wife's life in the Lin house, pining for dashing scholars all day, and throwing herself into Qiao Wenxian's arms the second they divorced. Hardly noble-lady conduct.
Annoyed as he felt, his career came first, so he agreed on the spot: "Rest easy, Lord Prefect. I'll send men to investigate from top to bottom—just wait for my good news."
Zhu Xiyan said, "Even though you and Shuzhen divorced, I still think of you as my own boy. Whenever a young lady catches your eye, tell me, and I'll speak on your behalf."
Lin Jue scoffed to himself: when he wanted a single maid, Zhu Xiyan couldn't handle it and sicced Prince Yi on him! Now that he needed a favor, out came the sweet talk. If not for the Prefect's clout in officialdom, he wouldn't bother bowing and scraping.
"Thank you for your kindness, Lord Prefect. If someone catches my eye, I'll be sure to let you know."
Zhu Xiyan smiled, then let his smile vanish into a cool, steady tone: "I failed to raise Shuzhen right. After this scandal, I cast her out of Zhu Manor; she has no ties to us anymore. Yet the rumors remain, and I will not tolerate anyone targeting the Zhu family's good name. Pull this off for us, Commandant Lin, and within a year the Division Commander's post will be yours."
Another promotion dangling right in front of him! Thrilled, Lin Jue sprang up and bowed even lower: "Rest easy, Lord Prefect! The Zhu family's business is my business!"
"Good. The moment you find a lead, come straight to Zhu Manor and report to me."
"Yes, sir!"
Back at Zhu Manor, before even changing out of his official robes, Zhu Xiyan summoned Zhu Jingyuan and told him to dress several servants as ordinary tea-drinkers to hang around teahouses fishing for leads. Servants might not turn up much, yet every extra pair of ears helped.
No sooner had he arranged it than a servant ran in to report: "Master, Prince Yi is at the gate asking to see you!"
Straightening his robes, Zhu Xiyan hurried out with his son. Reaching the side gate, he saw Prince Yi waiting outside and stepped forward to bow. "This humble official greets Your Highness Prince Yi. Forgive me for failing to welcome you sooner!"
Shen Jianchen said, "I came in a rush with no time to send word ahead—don't mind it, Lord Prefect."
"Prince Yi's visit brings honor to Zhu Manor! If Your Highness doesn't disdain our humble home, please step inside for a seat."
"I have other business today; I'll impose another time."
Puzzled why Prince Yi had come without stepping inside, Zhu Xiyan asked with deference, "May I ask what instructions Your Highness has for me?"
Shen Jianchen said, "I just came from Qiao Manor and heard what happened between the two houses. I hope Lord Prefect won't hold it against the Qiao family."
So that was it! How could Zhu Xiyan not resent the Qiaos? Yet before Prince Yi, he would never admit it. "No one to blame but myself for raising my daughter poorly. Having this trouble Prince Yi fills me with shame."
"Don't blame yourself, Lord Prefect. In my eyes, you raised a fine daughter. As for the street gossip, don't pay it too much mind."
With the whole city tearing his daughter apart, hearing Prince Yi speak up for the Zhu family overwhelmed Zhu Xiyan. He dropped to his knees. "Your Highness is wise and gracious!"
Shen Jianchen helped him up. "Get up, Lord Prefect. I came on Wenyuan's behalf. He told me you sent Youqi Jushi away—where did she move?"
A tear of genuine emotion slipped down Zhu Xiyan's cheek. Not only did Prince Yi defend the Zhu house, he was tracking down Shuzhen for young Qiao—proving that boy really did love Shuzhen! His tear was pure relief for his daughter.
Seeing his father too moved to speak, Zhu Jingyuan stepped in: "Your Highness, the new house Shuzhen bought is in the east of the city—it's called Wangyue Pavilion."
"I have errands to run, Lord Prefect. We'll talk another day." Having got the vital piece of news, Shen Jianchen wasted no time, climbing back into his carriage and ordering Bai Feng to head east.
Zhu Xiyan watched him go, marveling at how fast this prince came and went.
Still, Prince Yi's words lifted a boulder from his chest. At least there were still reasonable men like Prince Yi in this world.
Passing through a busy market, Shen Jianchen told Bai Feng to pull over—he wanted to buy a gift for Qianli Xun.
Mocked by the whole city, thrown out of Zhu Manor, her marriage to Wenyuan blown to pieces—she had to be miserable. A little gift might cheer her up.
Never having bought a gift for a girl before, Shen Jianchen felt stumped. Stepping down, he asked, "Bai Feng, what kind of gifts do girls like?"
"Is Your Highness buying for Princess Jiahe?"
"Quit guessing—just tell me what gift makes a girl happy."
Bai Feng thought a second. "I gave Kuqiao a clay figurine, and she loved it."
"A clay figurine it is, then."
Spotting a clay figurine shop right nearby, the two stepped inside, surrounded by shelves of lifelike painted figures in every pose imaginable.
A clerk hurried over with an eager grin. "Right this way, gentlemen! We carry every kind of clay figurine—take your pick!"
Scanning the shelves, Shen Jianchen's gaze locked onto a peeing-boy figurine: round eyes, round mouth, round head, mid-pee—so cheeky and adorable that one glance made him grin. It was guaranteed to crack Qianli Xun up.
"Clerk, wrap that figurine up."
One look at it left Bai Feng baffled. Why on earth would His Highness give Princess Jiahe a peeing boy? Shouldn't he get a pair of lucky wedding dolls—sweet and auspicious?
#CH 110
#TITLE: Visiting His Beloved to Speak His Heart
While the clerk fetched a small wooden box to pack the peeing-boy figurine, two young women strolled in, chatting as they browsed.
"This shop's clay figurines are wonderful! Which one should I buy for him?"
"Have you no shame? Careful folks don't start gossiping about you the way they do about Youqi Jushi!"
"How can you lump me in with her? My parents arranged this betrothal—all the matchmakers and betrothal gifts proper and accounted for!"
"Sigh. Who knew Youqi Jushi writes great poetry while behaving so scandalously?"
"Tell me about it! Nothing matters more to a maiden of the inner chambers than her chastity, yet she's bold enough to put it in verse and act it out—putting the rest of us to shame! Makes you wonder what the men she traded secret glances and had affairs with think..."
Shen Jianchen couldn't listen to another word—hearing "traded secret glances," wasn't that about him?!
Qianli Xun hadn't spared his own yearning glances a second thought, so how could he let two strangers smear her?
He marched right up to them. "Two young ladies blessed with faces fair as flowers—pity your words don't match your looks. Keep running your mouths behind people's backs and you might never find a husband's house; even if you do, once your in-laws learn their future daughter-in-law is a gossipmonger, they'll regret the match and might even call off the wedding."
Flattered at first by the praise of their looks, both girls bristled at the sting that followed.
One snapped, "What's the use lecturing us? Everyone on the street is talking about her! Are all the girls in Lin'an going to end up unwed?"
The other grabbed a lucky-doll figurine and handed it to the clerk. "Wrap this up. What rotten luck today, running into a meddler!"
Taking her doll, the clerk handed the wooden box holding the peeing boy to Bai Feng.
Bai Feng paid the silver, annoyed at how rude the girls had been to His Highness, and snapped back, "Keep badmouthing folks behind their backs and watch your mouths rot off tomorrow!"
Both girls flared up hotter, one barking, "What business is it of yours if we talk about Youqi Jushi? Dogs catching mice—mind your own business!"
Bai Feng yelled back, "Gossipmongers! A pair of gossipmongers!"
Shen Jianchen didn't stop him; they had it coming for slandering the woman he loved.
The clerk jumped in as peacemaker: "Now, now, folks—harmony brings wealth! Sir, it isn't right for the young ladies to gossip, sure, yet the whole city's buzzing right now. Can you police every last person? Blame the Prefect's daughter for crossing lines no respectable lady should cross and inviting talk."
Shen Jianchen asked, "You gossip about her too?"
The clerk chuckled. "Who doesn't? Everybody's talking. Heard the Prefect even threw his own daughter out to save the Zhu family's name."
Taking her wrapped figurine, the second girl snorted, "Even her own father doesn't want her—what's wrong with us saying two words? Hmph!"
Paying up, both girls flounced out in a huff.
Dread deepened in Shen Jianchen's chest—how hard must Qianli Xun be hurting right now? Something about these rumors smelled fishy, as if engineered on purpose.
"Clerk, how did you hear these rumors about Miss Zhu?"
"Running a shop, folk stream in and out all day—hear enough talk and it sticks. Honest truth, I'm busy minding the counter and never cared a fig about poets. Don't laugh, sir, I don't know a single poem by heart, yet now even I remember 'falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed'! Haha—the only difference between refined scholars and common folk like us is dressing up dirty deeds in fancy words, haha..."
Unable to stomach another word, Shen Jianchen walked out without reply, boarded his carriage, and headed east.
He kicked himself for holing up in Youlin Retreat for over a month. Had he strolled the streets sooner, he might have caught the rumors early and snuffed them out before they engulfed the whole city.
Yet without those rumors, the proposal would have gone through, Qianli Xun would belong to Wenyuan, and he wouldn't be riding east right now with a gift in hand...
Shen Jianchen gave his own cheek a light slap and muttered, "How can you think like that? You're an engaged man! Wenyuan is suffering, Qianli Xun must be hurting worse, and here you are feeling glad—shame on you!"
Yet he could not crush that stubborn spark of gladness—glad that one sliver of hope still survived between him and Qianli Xun.
Reaching the east quarter, it took no effort to find the wealthy neighborhood and locate Wangyue Pavilion.
Compared with Zhu Manor, Wangyue Pavilion was tiny, yet its layout held an understated grace.
Matron Wei had picked the house straight to her mistress's tastes: graceful orchids and slender bamboo filled the grounds, and chrysanthemums of every hue stood in full bloom across the yard, vying in beauty. It combined the best touches of Zhulan Residence and her old courtyard at Zhu Manor.
Qianli Xun loved it—above all, her bedroom was arranged identical to her old room at Zhu Manor, wrapping her in the comfort of home.
While the maids unpacked and Matron Wei set down house rules for the staff, Qianli Xun didn't have to lift a finger and collapsed onto bed to sleep. One day had dragged her through heaven and hell; she needed rest, hoping sleep might wipe the mess away.
Deep in slumber, she dreamed her huaben stories had taken the Song dynasty by storm—books selling out the instant they left the press, a copy in every hand down every alley, her name headed straight into the history books...
"Miss! Miss, wake up! Prince Yi is here to visit!" Kuqiao's voice yanked her out of her dream.
Opening her eyes and sitting up, she glanced out the window at gathering dusk. "Who's here?"
Kuqiao repeated, "Prince Yi."
"Prince Yi? How did he know I moved to Wangyue Pavilion? Don't tell me you leaked it to Bai Feng!"
"You wrong me, Miss! However long you haven't seen Prince Yi, I haven't seen Bai Feng either."
Remembering her vow never to meet Prince Yi alone again, Qianli Xun said, "Go tell Prince Yi I'm in a foul mood and have no time to see him."
"Miss, he's Prince Yi—turning him away like that might not go over well."
Qianli Xun felt stuck: see him, and he'd start tossing sparks at her again; refuse him, and she risked offending a royal prince.
Kuqiao handed her the perfect excuse: "Better see him, Miss—you can ask him how Young Master Qiao is doing."
Qianli Xun swung out of bed at once. "Help me freshen up! I want Prince Yi to see me looking my absolute best."
Dressed and polished to perfection by Kuqiao, she stepped into the reception hall, where Prince Yi sat waiting with Matron Wei keeping him company.
Seeing her mistress enter, Matron Wei rose. "Since Prince Yi has words for our Young Lady, I'll take my leave."
Shen Jianchen nodded, his gaze lingering on Qianli Xun. Clearly she had taken real care dressing up—wait, had she dressed up for him? Could she have feelings for him after all?
A second later he saw through it: she was only wearing bright makeup to mask the ache inside.
On the ride over, Bai Feng had figured out the clay figurine was for Youqi Jushi. Baffled at first, he had rationalized it as His Highness visiting on Young Master Qiao's behalf and not wanting to arrive empty-handed—still, what on earth was the idea behind a peeing boy?
Spotting Kuqiao, he dropped the puzzle, set the wooden box on the table, tossed Kuqiao a wink, and slipped outside with her.
Alone at last, Shen Jianchen stood up. "How is Youqi Jushi holding up?"
Skipping an answer, Qianli Xun bowed. "How did Prince Yi find Wangyue Pavilion?"
"Anyone I want to find can't stay hidden from me."
Right—he was a royal prince; what a pointless question.
"How is Wenyuan?"
Two sentences in and she was already asking about Wenyuan. Stung, Shen Jianchen answered anyway: "With the proposal off, Wenyuan is taking it hard. He begged me to go persuade Grandfather at Qiao Manor, yet Grandfather swore he'd give up his life before allowing your marriage. There was nothing I could do."
A wave of bitterness choked Qianli Xun silent.
Spotting the bruise on her hand, Shen Jianchen said, "Youqi Jushi needs to take better care of herself."
Swamped in heartache, she had no urge to talk, yet etiquette before a prince demanded a reply.
"Thank you for your concern, Prince Yi. Please pass word to Wenyuan to meet me beneath the Divine Willow tomorrow."
To Shen Jianchen, the Divine Willow belonged to him and Qianli Xun alone. "Why meet beneath the Divine Willow?"
"Why not beneath the Divine Willow?" Qianli Xun thought he was meddling way too much—now policing where she and Wenyuan met? What a busybody!
"Fine, suit yourself." Shen Jianchen picked up the wooden box. "Brought you a gift. Guaranteed to lift your spirits once you open it."
"Did Wenyuan send it?"
"Can't it be from me?"
Qianli Xun hesitated to accept. "Prince Yi shouldn't spend money on me."
Shen Jianchen pressed the box into her arms. "Hardly any money at all—buying you a gift makes me happy. Take it, or I won't pass your message to Wenyuan."
He turned toward the door.
Clutching the wooden box, tangled emotions churned inside Qianli Xun. "Prince Yi!"
Shen Jianchen halted without looking back. "Yes?"
"Wenyuan is the one I love."
"I know."
"Princess Jiahe is a wonderful girl. Don't you dare let her down."
"No need for you to remind me."
With that, Shen Jianchen strode out of the hall and across the courtyard. Seeing him leave, Bai Feng bade Kuqiao a hasty farewell and followed his master out of Wangyue Pavilion.
#CH 111
#TITLE: Xiaguo Spots Young Master Chen
Entering the hall, Kuqiao found her mistress standing in a daze with a wooden box in her hands.
"What's wrong, Miss? Did Prince Yi scare you?"
Qianli Xun neither approved of Prince Yi's ways nor meant to accept his feelings, yet she could not deny a warm spark of gratitude toward him.
The Qiao family calling off the proposal had stirred a sliver of self-doubt. Prince Yi was the first visitor to set foot in Wangyue Pavilion—and a royal prince at that. His visit, his words, and his gift had restored her footing.
"Why would he scare me? I'm just thinking I might be the first commoner to get a gift from a prince."
"Aren't you going to open it, Miss? Bai Feng said it's funny."
Qianli Xun lifted the lid and broke into a smile. "He actually gave me this—what kind of prince pulls a stunt like this?!"
"You smiled, Miss! Whatever Prince Yi gave you must be hilarious—let me see!"
Qianli Xun lifted the figurine up before Kuqiao. "Look, it's this."
Seeing the bare-bottomed clay boy mid-pee with a smug, blissful grin, Kuqiao slapped her hands over her eyes. "Why would Prince Yi give something like that—how shameless!"
Qianli Xun tucked the figurine back inside and shut the lid. "Your mind's in the gutter! Little kids are pure and adorable—look at it with pure eyes."
"Miss has a point; my bad." Lowering her hands, Kuqiao took the box. "I'll put it away for you."
For half a second Qianli Xun thought Prince Yi wasn't half bad, just barking up the wrong tree.
Then she caught herself: "Not half bad, my foot! He's engaged and still trying to seduce me—coming here today just to swoop in while I'm down! Dream on!"
"Kuqiao, bury Prince Yi's gift at the bottom of a trunk. I don't want to see it again."
Kuqiao blinked—one second ago Miss was smiling, why the sudden flip?
"Yes, Miss. I'll stash it at the bottom of a trunk we never open."
"And don't breathe a word of this to Bai Feng."
"Don't worry, Miss. Close as I am to Bai Feng, I know what to say and what to keep shut."
"Good."
Stepping out of the hall toward her room, mistress and maid ran into Xiaguo in the courtyard.
Matron Wei had sent Xiaguo out to buy household supplies. Having dropped off her parcels, she was hurrying to her mistress's room when they crossed paths.
"Miss, guess who I saw outside!"
"Who?" Qianli Xun asked.
"Young Master Chen! Chen Haoyu!"
Just when she thought he had vanished from her life, Xiaguo had spotted him.
"How did you run into him around here?"
"On my way back, almost at Wangyue Pavilion, I saw a bunch of men heading into a manor nearby—the plaque read Yongning Residence. I didn't think much of it, just glanced out of curiosity, and under the big lanterns by the gate I saw Chen Haoyu leading the pack!"
Walking toward her room, Qianli Xun muttered, "Yongning Residence? A Chen family property? Xiaguo, go fetch Matron Wei."
Xiaguo hurried off.
Back in her chamber, Kuqiao stashed the wooden box at the bottom of a trunk and began whisking tea.
Sitting at her desk waiting for Matron Wei, Qianli Xun had no heart to pick up a brush or open a book. Having her worst nemesis popping up right next door left her uneasy.
Matron Wei walked in with Xiaguo, speaking before even sitting: "Xiaguo told me on the way over. Seeing Young Master Chen around here is no surprise—the Chen family is rich and owns houses all over Lin'an. Yongning Residence is bound to be theirs."
"Have a seat, Matron. Kuqiao, serve tea."
Kuqiao had just finished a bowl and brought it to Matron Wei before starting another for her mistress, with Xiaguo hovering close to watch.
Taking a sip, Matron Wei set the bowl down. "If you dislike being near a Chen house, Young Lady, I can hunt elsewhere another day—worst case we sell this place and buy another."
She had barely moved in; uprooting again so soon felt too dreary.
Besides, buying and selling houses sounded easy, yet handling it was a huge chore. Matron Wei already worked hard enough managing her properties—no need to pile more hassle on her.
"No need. If fate put me in Wangyue Pavilion, I'll put down roots here—I'm tired of drifting. Chen Haoyu is obnoxious, sure, yet with so many Chen properties he won't live here full-time; he probably just drops by now and then. Besides, why should I fear him? He's the one who ought to fear me."
Disliking Chen Haoyu had almost morphed into dreading him—Qianli Xun refused to let that mindset take hold.
Hmph! Who did Chen Haoyu think he was? So what if they crossed paths? If he acted cocky, she'd just beat him up again!
"That's the spirit, Young Lady." Matron Wei lifted her tea bowl for another sip.
Before long Kuqiao served her mistress a fresh bowl. After chatting with Matron Wei a while longer, Qianli Xun turned in for the night.
Right now, nobody in Lin'an felt happier than Chen Haoyu—plan executed, sweet revenge achieved.
Over the past few days he had strolled the streets with his lackeys, listening to the rumors snowball bit by bit until today they hit peak blast.
Thrilled, he summoned the gang bosses from every quarter back to Yongning Residence—where he had handed out the job—to reward them.
The Yongning Residence cook laid out a lavish feast. Sitting in the host's chair, Chen Haoyu presided over the gang bosses crowding the table.
A lackey pulled out pouches of silver and tossed one into each boss's lap, lighting up greedy grins all around.
"Thanks, Young Master Chen! Anytime another sweet gig like this comes up, call us first!"
Chen Haoyu declared, "I'm in a great mood today—eat and drink your fill, and afterward my boys will take you all over to Chunxiang Brothel!"
"Generous as ever, Young Master Chen! Food, drink, and fun—what more could a man ask from life!"
"Come on, bottom's up!" Chen Haoyu raised his cup and drank with the crowd.
Draining his cup, one boss grinned: "Young Master Chen's scheme was nasty as—"
A lackey barked, "Watch your mouth!"
The boss pivoted on a dime: "—brilliant as hell! Got the whole city tearing Miss Zhu's name to shreds—good luck finding a husband now!"
Another chimed in: "Heard the Qiao family was set to propose today and you blew it sky-high! Heheh, Miss Zhu's still in the dark, and trying to trace the mastermind? Like fishing a needle out of the sea!"
Chen Haoyu crowed, "Haha! Got that venom off my chest at last! The whole city's cursing her out for me—let's see her act cocky in front of me again!"
A lackey beside him added, "Not just the city—it's reached the countryside too! Peasants out there are cursing her too!"
Cheers rose louder around the table as everyone drank harder and dug in deeper.
Right in the middle of the celebration, a lackey burst in shouting, "Young Master Chen! Disaster! Disaster!"
Chen Haoyu barked, "Watch your mouth! I just crushed Zhu Shuzhen—life's grand! What disaster?!"
The lackey panted, "I bumped into Lin Jue at Chunxiang Brothel and overheard him telling Chun Jinqiao that His Lordship the Prefect ordered him to hunt down whoever started the rumors!"
Tension snapped across the gang bosses' faces. One stammered, "What do we do, Young Master Chen? Will they trace it to us?"
"What's there to panic about? They won't find a thing!" Setting down his cup, Chen Haoyu pointed around the table. "Listen up: don't act guilty, play dumb, and go home and burn every single rumor sheet I gave you—don't leave a scrap!"
One boss piped up, "Those sheets got tossed or lost already—some of the boys even used them in the latrine!"
"Gone? Even better! Carry on like normal, act like nothing happened. After tonight's reward, don't come looking for me unless I call."
Chen Haoyu kept drinking and feasting with them until the small hours before they broke up.
The rest headed for Chunxiang Brothel while Chen Haoyu stayed the night at Yongning Residence, planning to visit his sister at Zhu Manor tomorrow to fish for news.
#CH 112
#TITLE: Charging Lin Jue and Carrying Word
Leaving Wangyue Pavilion, Shen Jianchen did not head straight back to Youlin Retreat; he told Bai Feng to drive to Lin Manor.
All the way there, those rumors nagged at him. Spreading across the whole city like wildfire meant someone was pulling strings behind the scenes.
Who in Lin'an had so much time on their hands as to drag Youqi Jushi through the mud? Most likely an enemy of the Zhu family who hated seeing them thrive.
Shen Jianchen recalled Qianli Xun's expression just now. Even wearing Zhu Shuzhen's face, he could feel how heavy her heart was.
How could anyone stay cheerful after a blow like that?
When Qianli Xun hurt, how could Shen Jianchen rest easy? He was going to stamp out these rumors across Lin'an for her.
Outside Lin Manor, Bai Feng stated their purpose to a gatekeeper, who looked awkward: "You picked a bad time—Third Master isn't home at this hour."
Bai Feng asked, "Where did he go?"
The servant didn't even blink: "Chunxiang Brothel."
Shen Jianchen directed them straight to Chunxiang Brothel.
Even after nightfall, Lin'an stayed lively—no quieter than the capital.
Thinking about it, since crossing over, he hadn't even toured Bianliang's night markets before rushing down to Lin'an. He had now spent longer in Lin'an than in Bianliang—proof fate still tied him to Qianli Xun. Wasn't the Qiao proposal collapsing proof too?
No sooner did that spark of cheer rise than Qiao Mengrong's voice calling "Brother" echoed in his ears.
Shen Jianchen grumbled at Zhao Pu in his head: "Zhao Pu, oh Zhao Pu, why did you have to check out the exact second I crossed over, dumping me in your body to babysit your little fiancée?"
He let out a sigh. "Ah, stop overthinking—we'll cross that bridge when we come to it."
Hearing his master speak without catching the words, Bai Feng asked, "Did Your Highness say something? I didn't catch it."
"Nothing. Between Bianliang and Lin'an, which has livelier nights?"
"Didn't Your Highness ever stroll Bianliang's streets at night?"
"I was sick the whole time—where would I get the energy or mood to go out? And the second I recovered, I came down to Lin'an before getting a night out, so I wouldn't know." Pausing, he wondered why he was explaining so much to Bai Feng. "Why all the questions? Answer what I ask and quit prying!"
"Yes, yes, I won't pry into Your Highness's business." Driving on, Bai Feng puffed out his chest: "Bianliang is the capital, so of course it's grander—nights are livelier than days! Lin'an can't hold a candle to it! Once Your Highness returns to the capital, I'll take you out at night to Fanlou Tower and the Goulan Wasi—the food and entertainment blow Lin'an out of the water!"
Hearing the hometown pride of an ordinary Bianliang lad, Shen Jianchen scoffed, "Sounds like common folk are mighty happy with life these days—just because the eating and playing are good?"
"Well, there's plenty they're unhappy about too."
"Go on—tell me what they're unhappy about."
"If I say it, Your Highness can't punish me."
"Speak your mind."
"Bianliang looks dazzling, sure, yet common folk are still dirt poor. All that glitter belongs to the rich, nothing to do with little folk like us. If common folk were happy with life, nobody would be rising in rebellion."
"Well said! For that remark alone, once we're back in Bianliang I'm treating you to a feast at Fanlou Tower!"
"Thank you, Your Highness!" Thrilled yet baffled, Bai Feng wondered if His Highness was really the Emperor's son—he had just criticized flaws in His Majesty's realm, and His Highness loved it! What an unusual prince.
Chatting along, they pulled up outside Chunxiang Brothel.
Unwilling to step inside, Shen Jianchen stayed in the carriage and sent Bai Feng in to summon Lin Jue.
It was Bai Feng's first time stepping into a house of pleasure, leaving him equal parts curious and nervous.
No sooner had he crossed the threshold than the madam swept up. "Never seen this young master before—stranger the first time, regular the second! Every girl in my house is top-notch. What type strikes your fancy?"
Uncomfortable, Bai Feng stepped back: "I have someone I like—I'm not here for girls. I'm looking for Lin Jue."
"A faithful lad, eh?" The madam pointed across the room. "There—Commandant Lin is right over there."
Chun Jinqiao sat pressed up beside Lin Jue, watching dancers on stage.
Awkwardness pricked Bai Feng. "No wonder Youqi Jushi divorced Lin Jue—looks like he's a fixture at Chunxiang Brothel."
Bai Feng stepped over to Lin Jue. "Commandant Lin, Prince Yi requests your presence."
Absorbed in his pleasure, Lin Jue shot up at the mention of Prince Yi, recognizing the prince's personal bodyguard.
"Where is Prince Yi?"
"Waiting in his carriage outside."
Ditching Chun Jinqiao on the spot, Lin Jue hurried out of Chunxiang Brothel.
A courtesan stepped into Bai Feng's path: "Stay and have a little fun with me, handsome?"
"Get off, get off—I'm on duty!" Pushing past her, Bai Feng headed out too.
The courtesan huffed, "What a prude! Why step into Chunxiang Brothel if you're going to act saintly?"
Chun Jinqiao chuckled, "Pick your targets better, sister. One look tells you that kid's a first-timer—pouncing on him like that was bound to scare him off!"
"Easy for Sister to say, hogging Commandant Lin so the rest of us don't get a look-in! Hmph!" The second fresh customers walked in, her pout flipped straight into a beaming welcome.
Spotting the carriage parked on the street, Lin Jue stepped up and bowed. "Greetings, Prince Yi."
"Climb inside. I have a word for you."
Lin Jue climbed aboard and sat across from Prince Yi, tense lest the prince scold him for visiting a brothel.
"I came to ask a favor."
Relieved Prince Yi skipped mention of Chunxiang Brothel, Lin Jue exhaled. "Name it, Prince Yi—anything you ask."
Shen Jianchen said, "The whole city is slandering Youqi Jushi, and something smells off about it. I want you to investigate the source of these rumors on the quiet and bring whoever started them straight to me."
Prince Yi had come over this too! Entrusted in a single day by the two highest-ranking men in Lin'an filled Lin Jue with pride.
Still, he wondered: Zhu Xiyan was acting for the Zhu family, so why did Prince Yi care? Probably on Qiao Wenxian's behalf—everyone of standing in Lin'an knew how tight those two were.
"Rest easy, Prince Yi. His Lordship the Prefect came to me today too, and I've already sent men out. We'll drag the mastermind into the light."
"Good. Go back to your evening, Commandant Lin."
Embarrassed by the parting remark, Lin Jue bowed: "I take my leave."
Once Lin Jue exited, Bai Feng didn't need to ask—he drove straight back to Youlin Retreat.
Outside Youlin Retreat's gate, Qiao Wenxian stood craning his neck with Nan Shuang beside him.
Butler Li had urged his young master indoors several times to no avail before giving up, ordering Nan Shuang to bring out a thick cape against the autumn night chill, then heading off to bed himself.
Just as Nan Shuang draped the cape over his master's shoulders, the creak of carriage wheels sounded down the road.
Qiao Wenxian bolted out into the lane, forcing Bai Feng to haul the horse to a stop.
Before Prince Yi could step down, Qiao Wenxian scrambled into the carriage and clutched his arm. "Did my father agree?!"
Shen Jianchen wouldn't lie to him: "Grandfather threatened his own life. There was nothing I could do."
Strength drained from Qiao Wenxian's hands. His eyes turned red and his voice shook: "Everything was going fine—why did it turn out like this?! Tell me, Prince Yi... tell me this is all a bad dream!"
Ignoring the plea, Shen Jianchen said, "Don't worry—I'm going to track down whoever started these rumors and haul him right in front of Grandfather. Maybe then he'll change his mind."
Hope flared again, and Qiao Wenxian threw his arms around Prince Yi. "You really are my best brother..."
Unable to watch Wenyuan suffer, Shen Jianchen added a bright note: "Here's some good news—I found Youqi Jushi. She moved to the east side of town, a place called Wangyue Pavilion, and asked me to tell you to meet her beneath the Divine Willow tomorrow."
The best news since the proposal collapsed! Qiao Wenxian hugged him even tighter. "Back when you were sick, I never knew how much you'd mean to me; who knew once you recovered, you'd become my lifeline? I can never repay this debt—I'll serve you like an ox or a horse in the next life!"
Wenyuan's gratitude stabbed Shen Jianchen with guilt, knowing his own motives weren't pure. "Cut the melodrama—you don't owe me anything. Since when do we stand on ceremony? Keep thanking me and I won't help next time."
A smile broke across Qiao Wenxian's face at last, and he let go. "Princess Jiahe came looking for you several times—go see her."
That familiar leaden weight dropped back onto Shen Jianchen's chest. Without another word, he stepped out of the carriage and walked into the retreat—toward the duty he could not escape.
#CH 113
#TITLE: Enter the House of Chen
Night grew deep with no sign of Prince Yi returning, and Qiao Mengrong could not sleep a wink, waiting in her chamber. She knew he had gone to help Young Master Qiao, yet staying out this late—what if some girl in town had caught his eye? Lin'an was full of fresh, pretty young women.
Her thoughts spiraled, stirring restlessness that fed deeper worry, leaving her pacing on pins and needles.
At last Bai Feng's voice sounded outside: "Princess Jiahe, Prince Yi is here to see you."
Before Xilan could reach the door, Qiao Mengrong flung it wide. Seeing him standing there, she threw herself into his arms as if they had been parted for months: "Brother was gone so long—you worried me to death!"
What he dreaded most always came true: the more he feared Qiao Mengrong hugging him, the faster she rushed into his arms, leaving him zero excuse to push her away.
"I'm a grown man who knows martial arts—nothing's going to happen to me. Quit worrying over nothing."
Clinging tight, Qiao Mengrong refused to let go: "From now on, no matter how late Brother goes out, take me along, all right?"
How could Shen Jianchen agree to that?
"Bringing you along on errands isn't convenient. I'll promise you this, though: whenever I go strolling the streets, I'll take you."
Relieved he hadn't turned her down flat, Qiao Mengrong smiled: "Brother keeps his word."
"Of course." Eager to slip out of her embrace, Shen Jianchen teased, "First Wenyuan hugs me outside, and now you're hugging me in here—does everyone think my frame is sturdy as a big tree to lean on? Even a big tree gets tired of being leaned on."
Qiao Mengrong released him with a shy blush: "Me hugging Brother isn't the same as Young Master Qiao—I'm the woman going to marry you."
She kept bringing up their engagement as if terrified he might forget. Shen Jianchen understood why, yet hearing it always seemed to drain the color out of his life.
"I'm beat, Mengrong—I need to get some sleep. Turn in soon too."
With that, he turned and left.
Standing in the yard watching his back vanish into the dark, Qiao Mengrong murmured in low spirits, "Xilan, doesn't it feel like Brother hates it whenever I bring up our engagement?"
"Princess is overthinking things—His Highness was out late and must just be worn out."
"You have a point."
...
First thing the next morning, Chen Haoyu crawled out of bed, ate breakfast at Yongning Residence, and—mindful of the Prefect hunting the rumor-mongers—decided to drop by Chen Manor first before visiting his sister at Zhu Manor to fish for news.
No ancestor of the Chen clan had ever held public office, yet their businesses spanned the empire; as the wealthiest household in Lin'an, their fortune dwarfed ordinary rich families.
In this generation stood three Chen brothers: the eldest, Chen Jianglin; the second, Chen Jiangshu; and the third, Chen Jiangmu. Chen Jianglin ran the family empire, assisted by his two younger brothers.
Chen Haoyu and Chen Wenxin were Chen Jianglin's children; Chen Jiangshu had a son named Chen Haodong; and Chen Jiangmu had a son named Chen Haofei.
The eldest soul in the house was Old Madam Xia, past sixty and living in pampered comfort. She doted on Haoyu above all others because he was the spitting image of her late husband, pouring all her love for her departed spouse onto her grandson until she spoiled him into an incorrigible rake.
Normally Chen Haoyu avoided home like the plague, yet with the Prefect investigating the rumors, he figured he'd better play the dutiful son at home for a stretch to build goodwill—just in case the trail reached him and he needed family cover.
Everyone in Lin'an knew Chen Manor was the largest estate in town, its halls and gardens unmatched by any other family.
The second Chen Haoyu stepped inside Chen Manor's gate, maids and page boys beamed in greeting: "Young Master is home at last!"
Chen Haoyu only flirted with the maids: "Gone a few days and you've gotten even prettier!"
The maids giggled, flattered yet shy.
Heading toward his mother Yao Qingyan's courtyard, he ran right into his personal page boy, Qinggua.
Personal page boy in name only—Chen Haoyu only let him tag along inside Chen Manor, never beyond the front gate. Even the name Qinggua ("Cucumber") had been slapped on him on a whim because Chen Haoyu happened to be crunching a cucumber when the lad entered service.
"Young Master is back! I have someone to wait on at last!"
"Why are you such a glutton for punishment? Love waiting on people that much?"
"Nobody else—I only like waiting on Young Master!"
"Don't think I don't know Father and Mother planted you beside me to spy on me."
"Young Master wrongs me! Master and Madam placed me here, sure, yet my heart belongs to Young Master. Just take me along when you go out—I want to go have fun with you too!"
Chen Haoyu halted. "Mean that?"
Qinggua stopped too. "Truer than solid gold and silver!"
"Then keep following me around Chen Manor." Chen Haoyu strode on toward Yao Qingyan's rooms.
Qinggua slumped: "All that talk and Young Master still won't let me go out with you."
Ignoring him, Chen Haoyu kept walking.
Chen Manor was vast; even though Yao Qingyan lived in the main courtyard, reaching it from the front gate took quite a trek.
Halfway there, he bumped into his second uncle's son, Chen Haodong.
Chen Haoyu greeted him first: "Where are you off to, Brother Dong?"
"Haoyu's home!" Returning the greeting, Chen Haodong pulled him aside. "Out carousing day and night, even hitting gambling dens—watch out Eldest Uncle doesn't tan your hide!"
"Relax, Brother Dong. Father knows how I am—he's used to it and won't bat an eye."
"Heard you've been messing with young virgins out there, keeping the ones you fancy as maids in your west-city house and selling the rest to brothels—true?"
"Slander! Pure slander! Sure, I put a few in the west-city house as maids, yet selling girls to brothels? Never happened! No idea who's spreading lies to make everyone think that."
"Level with me—those maids over there, haven't you already..." A teasing smirk curled Chen Haodong's lip.
Chen Haoyu didn't even blink: "Goes without saying!"
A flash of scorn crossed Chen Haodong's mouth before turning back into a warm grin. "Little brother, knock it off already. Eldest Uncle is going to hand the Chen empire to you sooner or later—turn over a new leaf and treasure the gift Heaven dropped in your lap."
"Pass! I'm not the only male heir—there's you and Haofei, plenty between you two! All I want is freedom, good food, and fun. Gotta run see Mother—catch you later!"
With Qinggua at his heels, Chen Haoyu headed on toward the main courtyard.
Watching his cousin's back, Chen Haodong let out a cold sneer. "A hopeless rake soaked in dandy habits—hand the Chen fortune to you and it'd go up in smoke! Still, staying just as you are suits me fine."
Yao Qingyan's greatest joy in life was marrying Chen Jianglin—not for his fortune, but for his undivided devotion.
Chen Jianglin had never taken a concubine. Outside running the family empire, he spent all his hours by her side, even putting her ahead of business deals, earning the envy of half the noblewomen in Lin'an.
Yao Qingyan's greatest sorrow was bearing Chen Haoyu, a shiftless ne'er-do-well who drifted outside all day. Frustrated as she was, she never had the heart to beat him—and with her mother-in-law shielding him, her hands were tied anyway.
The only soul in the manor tough enough to discipline Chen Haoyu was Chen Jianglin, yet he was a filial son who dropped the rod whenever Old Madam Xia spoke, and a doting husband who stayed his hand the instant his wife pleaded.
So Chen Haoyu grew wilder by the day, staying out as long as he pleased—the Chen clan had endless estates to sleep in and endless shops to draw silver from.
Husband and wife were chatting indoors when the maid Yingxue stepped in to announce, "Master, Madam, Second Young Master is home and waiting outside."
Beaming at his wife a second ago, Chen Jianglin's face snapped grim at mention of his wayward son. "Send him in!"
Yao Qingyan chided him: "Don't be like that, Husband. Our son comes home once in a blue moon—keep glaring at him and he'll hate coming home even more."
Chen Jianglin broke into a smile at once. "Fair point."
Bursting through the door, Chen Haoyu threw himself into his mother's arms: "Mother, I missed you so much!"
"Sweet-talking me again! If you missed your mother, you wouldn't be out carousing." Though her words scolded, Yao Qingyan's eyes brimmed with warmth.
"Cross my heart—this time I'm staying home for good!"
"The sun must be rising in the west! If you last two whole days at home, I'll burn incense in thanks."
Unable to hold back, Chen Jianglin darkened his brow: "Stop hanging on your mother the second you walk in! Stand up straight and answer my questions."
Giving her husband face in front of their son, Yao Qingyan said, "Go stand straight and answer your father."
Chen Haoyu snapped upright. "Ask away, Father."
Chen Jianglin spoke in a stern tone: "Confess—what have you been up to out there?"
"Eating, drinking, having fun—nothing bad."
"Not harassing decent girls? Not visiting brothels? Not haunting gambling dens?"
Every question hit dead center, yet Chen Haoyu dared not admit a word. "Father, I love having fun and staying out, sure, yet I'd never touch places like that—somebody's framing me!"
"With a reputation like yours, who'd need to frame you?!"
Tempers flaring, Chen Jianglin moved to stand and box his son's ears, only for a look from his wife to stop him. Steeling his breath, he said, "Your sister told me you even picked on Miss Zhu—true or not?"
Chen Haoyu pouted: "She's the one who picked on me, all right?!"
"Miss Zhu is a famed female ci poet brimming with talent! Treat her with respect when you see her and learn from her! I don't expect you to turn into a grand scholar—even if you just marry a refined, talented girl half like Miss Zhu someday, I'll die happy."
Chen Haoyu curled his lip: "Which eye of yours sees her as refined, Father? Step outside and listen—the whole city's cursing her out! Filthy stuff!"
"Since when do we trust street gossip? Haven't I met Miss Zhu myself? Don't I know what kind of girl she is? Besides, your sister praises her to the skies—your mother and I might doubt anyone else, never your sister!"
Yao Qingyan smiled. "Your father's right. Your sister rarely praises anyone; someone who wins her over can't be bad."
Right on cue, Chen Wenxin walked in with a maid carrying Laibao...
#CH 114
#TITLE: Returning to Chen Manor to See the Family
With the whole city gossiping about Shuzhen and dragging the Zhu family down too, her father-in-law Zhu Xiyan stayed in a black mood, smothering Zhu Manor in gloom. Afraid of crossing her father-in-law or making her mother-in-law cry more, Chen Wenxin decided to go stay at her maiden home for a few days.
With Zhu Jingyuan's blessing, she bade her in-laws farewell, claiming her grandmother was getting on in years, missed Laibao, and had asked her back for a short visit.
Neither Zhu Xiyan nor Madam Xiang had reason to refuse, only asking her to pass their regards to the Chen elders.
Stepping into the room to find her brother standing at attention, Chen Wenxin guessed Father was lecturing him and teased, "Well, well! Look who's behaving at home today! Did you make Father mad again?"
Chen Haoyu grumbled, "What's with that tone, Sister? Acting like you never see me! Even when I don't come back to Chen Manor, I stop by Zhu Manor all the time to see you, Brother-in-law, and Laibao."
"Fair enough." Stepping beside her father, Chen Wenxin said, "Don't waste anger on him, Father—it's rare to have the whole family together."
Seeing his daughter lit up Chen Jianglin's face: "For your sake, I'll let the brat off today and settle accounts another time."
Yao Qingyan beckoned the maid to bring Laibao over to her lap.
Charmed by their adorable grandson, both grandparents forgot all about Chen Haoyu.
Delighted his sister had saved him a trip to Zhu Manor, Chen Haoyu tugged Chen Wenxin's sleeve: "Come outside a sec, Sister—got something to ask you."
Chen Wenxin followed him out, with Qinggua trotting right behind—inside Chen Manor, Young Master had no excuse to shoo him off.
Out in the courtyard, Chen Wenxin asked, "Why drag me out here?"
"I was going to swing by Zhu Manor after seeing Father and Mother, so you saved me the trip."
Suspicion narrowed Chen Wenxin's eyes. "Since when did you grow so thoughtful, coming home to see our parents?"
"I've always been thoughtful! Decided to stay put at Chen Manor for a while and keep the family company."
Suspicion sharpened further: "The sun really is rising in the west—why does this feel fake? Don't tell me you pulled some colossal stunt and ran home for shelter!"
Chen Haoyu marveled at how well his sister read him, though he'd never admit a thing: "No way! Hanging around outside gets boring."
"Spit it out—what did you want to ask?"
Chen Haoyu probed with care: "Sister, why is everyone outside gossiping about Zhu Shuzhen? Saying such ugly stuff I can't even stand listening!"
Indignation blazed on Chen Wenxin's face: "Even you can't stand it—imagine my father-in-law and mother-in-law! Who knows who Shuzhen offended to get smeared like this, dragging the whole Zhu family down with her! Father-in-law suspects foul play and has men investigating. Hey, Haoyu—you roam outside all the time and know loads of people. Help sniff around for whoever's behind this! Catch that rat and I'll slap their face raw and kick them black and blue!"
Chen Haoyu practically felt the slaps and kicks landing on his own hide: "Uh, Sister, isn't that a bit harsh?"
"Hmph, that's getting off light! If Father-in-law catches them, he'll flay them alive!" Scowl fierce, Chen Wenxin looked ready to shred the culprit on the spot.
Nerves jangling, Chen Haoyu put on a cool front: "If you ask me, the Prefect shouldn't waste his energy. What rumor-monger? Zhu Shuzhen brought it on herself with that line of verse! If she hadn't written trash and hung out with Lin'an's scholars all the time, would her name stink? Careful the Prefect digs and digs only to land right back on his own daughter—what a laughingstock that'd be!"
Studying her brother's face, Chen Wenxin frowned: "One second ago you were defending Shuzhen—why flip your tune so fast?"
Chen Haoyu scrambled to cover: "You know me, I speak my mind! Quit staring at me like that, Sister—it's embarrassing."
Chen Wenxin poked his forehead. "Since when does a rascal like you know embarrassment? Now that you're home, go see Grandmother—she's been missing you nonstop."
"I'm going, I'm going—just tell me one more thing first."
"What?"
"How badly did the rumors hit Zhu Shuzhen?" Though he had already heard, he wanted confirmation straight from his sister.
"How badly? Catastrophic! The Qiao family canceled the proposal, and Father-in-law threw Shuzhen out of the house—what do you think?!"
Elation burst inside Chen Haoyu. "Ha! Zhu Shuzhen, try messing with me again—how does defeat taste?! Hahaha!"
Chen Wenxin eyed him askance: "Haoyu, why do you look like you're gloating?"
Chen Haoyu hurried to mask his grin: "Me? No way! Going to see Grandmother now!"
Chen Wenxin caught his sleeve. "I'm warning you—no matter how much you clash with Shuzhen, don't you dare pick on her while she's down! She's suffered enough—hear me?"
"Heard you, heard you! I'm staying home anyway, so I won't even cross paths with her—how could I pick on her? Besides, whenever we cross paths, she's the one beating me up!" Breaking free, Chen Haoyu bolted off in a flash.
"That little menace!"
Qinggua offered, "Eldest Miss, I'll go keep Young Master company."
"Go on, and keep a close eye on him so he doesn't cause trouble."
Qinggua nodded and scampered off.
Returning inside with a smile, Chen Wenxin sent the maid out to play with Laibao, then sat down with her parents to talk over the Zhu family's troubles.
"Father, Mother, you must have heard what happened to the Zhu family."
Yao Qingyan sighed, "How could we miss it? Gossip's flying everywhere—poor Miss Zhu."
Chen Jianglin asked, "How does your father-in-law plan to handle it?"
"Jingyuan told me Father-in-law sent men to track down whoever started the rumors."
Chen Jianglin shook his head. "How can you trace that? A rumor passes from one mouth to the next until nobody remembers who spoke first. Better for your father-in-law to ignore it—rumors flare fast and die fast. Give it time and folks will forget."
"I just worry about poor Shuzhen getting stood up by the Qiaos—how will she find a husband now? Why is her fate so hard? First an unhappy marriage, and just when a fine match comes along, it falls apart!" Grief misted Yao Qingyan's eyes as if it were her own daughter.
Chen Jianglin smiled: "Your mother's too soft-hearted—can't bear seeing anyone suffer."
"Not just anyone—our in-laws' daughter!" Yao Qingyan insisted. "Why don't we pay the Zhus a visit in a day or two to comfort Wenxin's in-laws?"
Chen Wenxin waved her hands at once: "Better not! Going right now would be awkward—wait a bit."
Chen Jianglin agreed: "True. Wait until the gossip dies down and the Zhus calm down and sort things out, then we'll call on them."
Weighing their words, Yao Qingyan saw the sense in it and nodded.
Meanwhile, a maid had already carried news of Second Young Master's return to Old Madam Xia. Grabbing her cane, the old woman started out to find her grandson, her maid hurrying to steady her.
Right at the doorway, they bumped into Chen Haoyu walking up.
Chen Haoyu threw himself into her arms: "Grandmother, I missed you to death!"
Old Madam Xia laughed while scolding him: "Sweet-talking me again! Missing me while staying away all the time—good thing the Chen family owns houses everywhere, or you'd be sleeping in the streets!"
Chen Haoyu steadied her elbow: "Let me help you inside, Grandmother—we'll talk indoors."
Once inside, the maid settled Old Madam Xia onto the couch.
Chen Haoyu kicked off his shoes, climbed up cross-legged, and barked at a maid: "I'm parched—go brew a pot of tea."
The maid hurried off while Qinggua stood beside them listening to grandmother and grandson chat.
Holding her grandson's hand, Old Madam Xia peered close: "Let Grandmother look at you—you've lost weight! Have fun all you want, just take care of your health."
"Don't worry, Grandmother, I'm fit as a fiddle."
"Tell Grandmother the truth: what keep you busy out there?"
How could he tell his grandmother about his real antics? Chen Haoyu spun a yarn: "Making friends, attending poetry salons now and then with Lin'an's scholars."
"Splendid! My grandson even attends poetry salons! I heard not just anyone gets invited—my boy's turning into a real scholar! Maybe one day you'll pass the exams and bring honor to our house!"
Guilt prickled Chen Haoyu under her praise. Just in time, the maid brought tea and poured a cup; Chen Haoyu lifted it to his lips to hide his sheepish face.
Doting eyes glued to her favorite grandson, Old Madam Xia noticed nothing and rattled on: "Your older cousin is already wed—when are you bringing Grandmother home a granddaughter-in-law? I'm waiting to hold a great-grandson!"
Glad for the change of subject, Chen Haoyu set his cup down. "Don't worry, Grandmother—sooner or later I'll bring you home a granddaughter-in-law you'll adore."
Old Madam Xia beamed brighter: "Do you have a girl in mind already?"
"Not yet. The second I do, Grandmother will be the first to know."
"Since you don't, I'll push your parents to get serious and look over Lin'an's noble daughters for you."
"Pass! Those highborn ladies in Lin'an are stuck-up prudes who talk all coy and think they're hot stuff—total snoozefests."
"Sounds like my boy has someone in mind after all, haha!" Old Madam Xia squinted with glee.
Chen Haoyu had never given marriage a single thought: "No way! I haven't had my fill of fun yet—why would I be dumb enough to shackle myself?"
"I heard you've been buying up maids and visiting brothels—don't you dare bring home a maid or a courtesan! Much as I spoil you, I'll never allow that." Even while lecturing, her face overflowed with affection; she could never get enough of her second grandson.
Mixing half-truth with lie, Chen Haoyu vowed, "Don't listen to gossip, Grandmother. I didn't buy maids and I don't haunt brothels. When I marry, it'll be a girl I love from a solid family—maids and courtesans are only for passing the time."
"Good that you know! Don't go wild!"
"Relax, Grandmother, I know the score."
Old Madam Xia swallowed every word without a trace of doubt, convinced her grandson was every bit as sharp and sensible as his late grandfather.
#CH 115
#TITLE: Drinking Tea and Overhearing Gossip
A full night's rest left Qianli Xun in no better spirits than yesterday.
How folk gossiped about her didn't matter; all that weighed on her was how she and Wenyuan could ever be together now.
This era she had crossed into was not an age of free love—without parents' consent, romance ran straight into a brick wall.
Why had the rumors hit both families on the exact day of the proposal, not a day earlier or later? Did Heaven really mean to keep her and Wenyuan apart? The more she brooded, the fiercer her hatred for those rumors burned—and the stronger her urge grew to go hear and see the gossipers with her own eyes.
After breakfast, with hours to spare before meeting Wenyuan, she decided to take a turn down the street.
"Kuqiao, go find me a set of men's clothes."
"What does Miss want men's clothes for?"
"Quit asking questions and just fetch some."
Kuqiao hesitated: "There isn't a single set of men's clothes in your wardrobe, Miss."
Qianli Xun thought of a quick fix: "Go ask one of the male servants outside for a set."
"How could Miss wear a servant's clothes?!"
"Do as I say and don't fuss."
Left no choice, Kuqiao went off to borrow a man's outfit from a servant.
Unbothered by the clothes request, Xiaguo was busy whisking morning tea, savoring the feeling that whisking tea made her look just like a refined young lady.
Bringing the finished bowl over to her mistress, Xiaguo asked with care, "Miss is meeting Young Master Qiao today—may I come along to wait on you?"
"You're my personal maid; of course you're coming." Raising the tea bowl to her lips, Qianli Xun lost the urge to drink and set it right back down.
Alarm flashed in Xiaguo's eyes: "Isn't Miss pleased with the tea I made?"
"No, don't overthink it—I just don't feel thirsty right now."
Her mistress had barely touched breakfast either; Xiaguo knew the wrecked marriage proposal had drained all appetite from her.
Kuqiao returned with a clean set of servant's clothes, helped her mistress change, removed her hairpins, and bound her hair into a man's topknot.
Checking the mirror, Qianli Xun nodded in approval. "I'm stepping out for a bit. Neither of you is to follow."
"Where is Miss going?" Xiaguo worried her mistress was sneaking off to see Young Master Qiao alone.
"Taking a turn down the street to hear what folks are saying about me." Leaving both maids behind, Qianli Xun walked out of Wangyue Pavilion alone.
Kuqiao sighed, "Poor Miss—will she have to dress like a man every time she steps outside now? Those gossipers out there are vile!"
Xiaguo couldn't fathom why her mistress would go hunt for insults: "Vile is right! Yesterday when I went shopping I heard folks spewing filth so awful I cursed them out on the spot. If Miss hears it with her own ears, she'll only hurt worse!"
"No kidding." Glancing at the untouched tea bowl on the table, Kuqiao sighed again: "Miss didn't even take one sip before leaving—she must be heartbroken."
Walking out onto the avenue, Qianli Xun meant only to stroll nearby, dressed as a man just to avoid being recognized.
Walking for a good half-hour through bustling crowds without hearing a single syllable about Zhu Shuzhen eased the knot in her chest.
Having skipped morning tea, thirst crept up after her long walk. Spotting a teahouse ahead, she stepped inside for a seat.
To draw customers, this teahouse featured live storytelling too.
The instant she crossed the threshold, she heard the storyteller reciting her own huaben story! Her spirits lifted at once; ordering a pot of tea, she sat down to sip and listen.
The storyteller's voice carried across the room:
Strange to tell, the very day the Hua family welcomed their daughter, flowers of every hue sprang up across their fields—a sign of heaven's favor, they believed. And auspicious it proved! From then on the Huas sold flowers for a living, prospering year by year until they became a wealthy country household.
When Hua Hongxiang turned fifteen, Wu Xuanying, son of the City Lord, was approaching his fifteenth birthday banquet and needed blossoms to deck the hall. His stewards searched high and low and found no flowers finer than the Huas'—and rumor said whenever the Hua girl stood nearby, blooms opened brighter and refused to wilt, so the banquet stewards placed a huge order with the Hua house...
Glancing around the packed tables, Qianli Xun saw nobody gossiping about Zhu Shuzhen—everyone sat hooked on her story!
She waved a waiter over.
The waiter stepped up. "What can I get you, sir?"
Pitching her voice low like a young man, Qianli Xun asked, "Your teahouse performs Qianli Xun's scripts too?"
The waiter beamed: "Looks like young sir loves storytelling—you know the brilliant Qianli Xun's name too! His tales are all the rage; every big teahouse and tavern in town is telling them."
"Is Qianli Xun really that famous now?"
"Quite the rising name!"
Delighted to hear "rising name," Qianli Xun pulled two bits of loose silver from her sleeve: "Here, for you."
The waiter grinned wide: "Thank you, young sir!"
Just then two men walked in, scanned the packed room for empty tables, and stepped up to Qianli Xun's table: "Mind if we share your table, little brother?"
Qianli Xun waved them over: "Go right ahead, make yourselves at home."
Sitting down, the two ordered tea, listened for a few minutes, then grew bored and began chatting in low voices.
"Heard this script three times already—know it by heart! Hua Hongxiang meets Wu Xuanying for the first time at the birthday banquet, yet with past memories erased, neither recognizes the other and misunderstandings fly."
"Wonder what happens next—we'll have to wait for Master Qianli Xun to write more."
Delight bubbled in Qianli Xun: not a whisper of rumor, only praise for her pen name! "You two know Qianli Xun as well?"
"Of course—everyone who loves storytelling knows him!"
Qianli Xun fished for more: "Pretty famous, is he?"
"Making a name, sure—though no fame beats Miss Zhu's right now! Haha!"
His laugh wiped the smile clean off Qianli Xun's face. Clamping her lips shut, she sipped her tea—ignoring the storyteller as every syllable from the two men poured straight into her ears.
"Tell me about it—everyone's talking! That Miss Zhu sure has nerve, not an ounce of noble-lady grace."
"My little sister used to adore her poetry and copied heaps of it from who knows where—hearing the street talk broke her heart."
Thinking someone out there had sense, Qianli Xun chimed in: "Your sister must know those street rumors are lies and feel furious for Miss Zhu!"
The man shook his head: "Not at all! My sister's heartbroken she wasted years admiring Youqi Jushi's verse—said if she'd known her morals were rotten, she'd never have hunted down so many poems!"
The other man asked, "Don't tell me she burned them all?"
"How'd you guess?!"
"Easy! Heard the Prefect torched all of Youqi Jushi's manuscripts—your sister did the same? Now that's a heroine to rival any man!"
Qianli Xun almost laughed out loud—burning Zhu Shuzhen's poems made a girl "a heroine to rival any man"? What twisted praise!
"Have you two ever considered those stories are just rumors, and Miss Zhu is nothing like what folk say?"
Both men stared at the strange youth and shook their heads together.
"Never thought about it. We don't know Youqi Jushi—how should we know what's true or false?"
Qianli Xun pressed: "Then you have even less business slandering her! Isn't wronging an innocent person a sin?"
"No smoke without fire! Besides, she's the Prefect's daughter—who'd dare slander her for nothing? She must be shameless to stink this far and wide; she has only herself to blame!"
That snapped Qianli Xun's last nerve. Lifting her tea bowl, she splashed the tea straight across the man's chest and roared, "Two grown men wagging tongues like fishwives—may your sons be born without assholes!"
Humiliated by a slip of a boy, the man jumped up cursing: "Little punk, dare splash tea on me? Watch your grandpa teach you manners!"
He swung a fist at Qianli Xun, his companion surging up to help.
Two grown men figured flattening one skinny lad would be a breeze—only for rapid left-right hooks to drop them both flat on the floor in two seconds, clutching bruised faces and howling, "Ow! Ouch!"
The brawl brought the teahouse to a standstill; the storyteller froze and every customer turned to gawk.
Qianli Xun barked, "Your old la—your grandpa isn't someone to push around! Mark my words: catch anyone else slandering Miss Zhu, and my fists won't go easy!"
Every bystander stuck out their tongue in silent relief—thank goodness they'd kept their voices low while gossiping earlier, or they'd be eating dust too!
Hurrying over to haul the two men up, the waiter pleaded, "Young sir, we run a tiny shop and can't survive a brawl—please don't hit anyone else!"
Qianli Xun glanced around. "Didn't break a single table or chair. Besides, I never hit innocents—only scum who ask for it by slandering Miss Zhu."
Pulling out the two bits of loose silver, the waiter held them back: "Take your silver back, sir! I gossiped too—I'm lucky you didn't beat me up; how could I dare keep your tip?!"
Qianli Xun waved the silver away: "Once given, I never take it back."
Pocketing it again, the waiter sighed, "Can't blame folks for talking, young sir—ask around, who in this room hasn't talked?"
One bruised man yelled back, "What's beating us up prove?! If you've got guts, go beat up the whole city! Heard peasants in the countryside are gossiping too—going to hike out there and punch them one by one?!"
His retort stopped Qianli Xun cold. Tough as her fists were, she couldn't beat up all of Lin'an. Common folk only gossiped for idle amusement; the real villain was whoever planted the rumors. Once she caught that rat, she'd chop him to pieces!
#CH 116
#TITLE: Meeting Wenyuan After the Tailor Shop
Leaving the teahouse with an even heavier heart, Qianli Xun saw why the Qiao family had canceled the proposal—with the city talking like this, what choice did she and Wenyuan have?
Walking on, she spotted a tailor shop. Her borrowed servant's outfit fit poorly; better have two proper sets of men's clothes tailored in case she needed them again.
Stepping inside, the shopkeeper greeted her: "Ready-made or custom-tailored, young sir?"
"Custom-tailored."
"Men's wear is on the left—right this way, sir."
Still a woman at heart, Qianli Xun glanced over to the right, where the women's ready-made robes looked lovely—and right there, browsing fabrics with a female friend, stood Miao Zhiyu!
Before they noticed her, Qianli Xun whipped her head away and ducked into the men's section.
An apprentice stepped up with a measuring tape: "Please raise your arms, young sir."
Qianli Xun lifted her arms obediently, keeping her back turned to the women's side so Miao Zhiyu wouldn't spot her.
Unaware of anyone on the men's side, Miao Zhiyu chatted with her confidante while sorting through silks.
"This style is lovely, Zhiyu—suits you down to the ground."
"Too plain. Let's look at others."
"Didn't you always prefer understated pieces? Why the change in taste?"
"Thought I'd try brighter colors—might suit me better."
Her friend added, "If Young Master Dongfang holds another poetry salon, you have to bring me along!"
"You don't even care for poetry—you'd be bored."
"I don't care about poetry; I just want to meet Young Master Dongfang!"
Miao Zhiyu let out a sigh. "I doubt Young Master Dongfang will hold any more salons."
"Why?"
"With everything hitting Youqi Jushi, how could he be in the mood?"
"Why would that stop him?"
"Without Youqi Jushi and Young Master Qiao, what's the point of holding a salon?"
"Oh, right. Speaking of Youqi Jushi—who knew she was that kind of woman? Never judge a book by its cover!"
Browsing robes, Miao Zhiyu spoke up: "Don't repeat wild street talk. Youqi Jushi is nothing like those rumors claim."
Warmth surged in Qianli Xun toward Miao Zhiyu—defending her while the whole city spat on her name showed real character.
Taking measurements, the tailor's apprentice puzzled in silence: clearly a young woman, yet dressed as a man ordering men's robes—how odd!
Finishing up, the apprentice said, "Got your measurements down, sir. Now pick out your styles."
Taking her time, Qianli Xun pretended to inspect patterns while keeping her back to Miao Zhiyu, ears pricked wide open.
The friend teased, "You talk like you know her inside out."
"Youqi Jushi and I aren't bosom friends, sure, yet we met at salon after salon, so I know her well. Calling her a tigress is outrageous! She used to show up to salons covered in bruises from Lin Jue's beatings. Later she grew tougher, yet she's always warm to friends. As for the rest of that filth, it's pure lies. Before her mutual divorce, she was scrupulous about proper distance around Young Master Dongfang and the others—never crossed a line once! Even when Young Master Qiao had feelings for her, she never..."
Miao Zhiyu stopped short, recalling the Zhu Manor banquet before the divorce when Youqi Jushi and Young Master Qiao had traded tender glances—sparking green jealousy inside her.
"Well? So Youqi Jushi isn't spotless after all?" her friend prodded at the pause.
Miao Zhiyu insisted, "She and Young Master Qiao only got together after her divorce. Point is, those street rumors are all slander."
Qianli Xun almost marched over to hug Miao Zhiyu in thanks, then remembered her men's disguise and held back, keeping her ears glued to their chat.
Her friend went on: "Zhiyu, you're probably the only soul left defending Youqi Jushi! Such a kind heart—and kindness brings good karma! Heard the Qiao family started bride selection for Young Master Qiao all over again—your chance has come!"
A smile curved Miao Zhiyu's lips before worry crept in: "I wonder how Young Master Qiao is holding up right now..."
Her friend urged, "Stop fretting here—find a chance to go see him!"
Miao Zhiyu nodded in agreement.
After browsing a bit longer, they settled on their robes.
The shopkeeper wrapped their purchases and saw both ladies out the door.
With no heart left for shopping, Qianli Xun picked two styles at random, paid the deposit, and walked out.
Having wandered long enough, it was time to head back and get ready to meet Wenyuan—only face to face would they know what path lay ahead.
...
Back at Wangyue Pavilion, Qianli Xun changed back into women's robes, took both maids, and rode out to meet Qiao Wenxian beneath the Divine Willow.
Xiaguo beamed at the thought of seeing Young Master Qiao again.
Kuqiao watched her mistress in quiet awe—facing such a storm with calm courage was no small thing. Seeing her mistress silent, Kuqiao stayed silent too, fretting over what lay ahead for Miss and Young Master Qiao.
Qiao Wenxian had arrived beneath the Divine Willow long before, staring down the path where Shuzhen was sure to appear, longing stamped across his face.
The willow's leaves had turned yellow; gusts of autumn wind shook withered leaves down onto Qiao Wenxian's shoulders, and Nan Shuang kept stepping up to brush them off into the grass.
Yellow willow leaves mingled with yellowing grass until neither could be told apart. All around them, nature drained of life—matching the bleak chill in Qiao Wenxian's chest.
He had lain awake all night searching for a future with Shuzhen and finding none, terrified above all that she might walk away from him.
At last the figure he longed for appeared, walking up the path at an unhurried pace, bringing a spark of light to his desolate face.
Unable to wait another second, he strode forward and pulled the woman he loved straight into his arms...
Kuqiao tugged Xiaguo over to join Nan Shuang.
Xiaguo dragged her feet, casting longing glances back over her shoulder at Young Master Qiao; seeing how haggard he looked made her heart ache.
Held tight in Wenyuan's arms, Qianli Xun wrapped her arms around him too, and tears spilled over at last: "Wenyuan, my name is dirt across all of Lin'an—don't you despise me?"
"I know you better than anyone. How could rumors ever sway me?"
Qianli Xun hugged him tighter. "Thank you for trusting me."
"Never say thank you to me again."
"All right, I won't." After a quiet beat, she asked the question she had turned over the whole way here: "What... what do we do from here?"
Qiao Wenxian did not answer at once. Letting her go, he gazed straight into her eyes with deep tenderness: "Wait for me. Trust me—I'll persuade my parents to propose to the Zhu family again."
Qianli Xun dropped her gaze. "Of course I trust you. Only Prince Yi told me your father would sooner give up his life than propose to Zhu Manor."
"If Father can stake his life, so can I—"
"No! Never say that!" Panic gripped Qianli Xun. "If it comes to that, I'd rather give up this marriage..."
Qiao Wenxian pressed a hand over her mouth to stop her words: "Don't talk like that. I promise I'll take care of myself—just believe in me and wait for me."
"All right, I'll wait. As long as you hold on, I'll hold on. If you ever give up—"
"I'll never give up."
"Neither will I."
Right as they drank in each other's vows, Prince Yi's voice sliced in: "What good do all your grand vows do?"
Qianli Xun rolled her eyes—talk about zero tact! Intruding right on her date with Wenyuan! Ready to snap back, she spotted Princess Jiahe walking beside him, swallowed her temper, and stepped up to bow: "Prince Yi, Princess Jiahe."
Qiao Mengrong hurried up to steady her: "Did Brother and I interrupt you two?"
"Need you ask? Huge interruption!" Qianli Xun raged inside, while smiling aloud: "Not at all. What brings Princess and Prince Yi out here?"
Shen Jianchen spoke up: "Nothing in particular. Felt like a walk and brought Mengrong along, and somehow our feet led us right here."
Qianli Xun cursed him in her mind: "On purpose, my foot! Banking on me not tearing your mask off in public so you can shamelessly wreck my date!"
She tugged Wenyuan's sleeve: "Let's go talk over there and leave this spot to Prince Yi and Princess Jiahe."
Qiao Wenxian nodded: "Prince Yi, Shuzhen and I will step over that way."
Shen Jianchen stepped in front of them: "Why bother? Since we ran into each other, let's all chat together! You two have been out here a while—must've said everything important already. Keep dwelling on sad stuff and you'll only feel worse. Let the four of us talk about something brighter to cheer you up!"
Qiao Wenxian saw sense in that: "Shuzhen, we've said our piece—why not join them?"
Before Qianli Xun could refuse, Princess Jiahe took her hand: "Youqi Jushi, I haven't seen you in ages and have so much to tell you! Why not stay the night at Youlin Retreat and talk all night with me?"
Glancing toward the horizon, Qianli Xun saw the sun sinking low; autumn days shrank fast, and night would fall soon. After all the storms lately, she did crave a good vent, and Princess Jiahe was the best confidante around.
"All right—then I'll stay the night at Youlin Retreat."
Hearing Shuzhen agree to stay cheered Qiao Wenxian up; with no rush now, he pulled Prince Yi aside.
Qianli Xun walked around the pond arm in arm with Princess Jiahe, chatting as they strolled.
Bai Feng stuck to Kuqiao like glue the second he saw her; ignored by Xiaguo, Nan Shuang wandered off to chat with Xilan; and Xiaguo stood alone, eyes fixed in anxious care on Young Master Qiao.
#CH 117
#TITLE: Xiaguo's Confession Rejected
Xiaguo kept her eyes locked on Young Master Qiao. Hearing her mistress agree to stay overnight had brought a spark of smiles back to his worn face as he drew Prince Yi beneath the willow tree to talk.
Qiao Wenxian had pulled Prince Yi aside for one reason only: to beg him for a plan.
"Neither Shuzhen nor I will give up, yet I have no idea what to do next. Can Prince Yi think of a way out for me?"
Facing the desperation on Wenyuan's haggard face, Shen Jianchen could not bear to crush him—yet remembering Qianli Xun's vow to hold on stabbed his own chest. "Is Youqi Jushi really refusing to give up? No matter how hard it gets?"
"She told me herself, and it filled me with hope! I have to do something worthy of her devotion—I just don't know what!" Gloom clouded Qiao Wenxian's brow.
Shen Jianchen tested gently, "Have you ever thought you and Youqi Jushi might simply not be fated to end up together?"
Before he even finished, Qiao Wenxian cut him off: "Impossible! We will be together—this is just a rough patch."
"In that case, I've got an idea." Qianli Xun loved Wenyuan and meant to stand by him; grinding his teeth, Shen Jianchen forced himself to help them.
Qiao Wenxian pressed: "What idea? Tell me!"
Wenyuan's pitiful look wrung the plan straight out of Shen Jianchen: "Take Youqi Jushi and leave Lin'an to build a life somewhere else. Zhu Manor already cast her out, so nobody will stop her. Once the rice is cooked and you two have a child, a few years down the road Grandfather will have no choice but to accept it—you're the Qiao family's only son and heir, so he'll have to bring you home. Happy ending, right?"
Qiao Wenxian shook his head at once: "No! How could I do that? Folk are already slandering Shuzhen—if I elope with her, her reputation will be ruined forever! Besides, a marriage without parents' orders and matchmakers' words earns no blessings. I won't let Shuzhen suffer with me. Isn't there another way?"
Hearing Wenyuan reject elopement sent a secret surge of relief through Shen Jianchen. He had half dreaded Wenyuan taking his advice and spirits-whisking Qianli Xun away—forcing him to chase after them until he couldn't even hide his feelings anymore. Truth be told, if not for hurting Qiao Mengrong and Wenyuan, Shen Jianchen would never have bothered hiding how he felt about Qianli Xun at all.
Shen Jianchen shook his head. "No better way."
"No, there has to be a better way—I need to think harder."
Crushed under heavy thoughts, Qiao Wenxian dragged his feet over to the boulder beneath the Divine Willow, sank down against the trunk, and stared up at the clear autumn sky—crisp and bright overhead, yet bleak inside his chest.
Gazing across the water, Shen Jianchen saw Qianli Xun and Qiao Mengrong reach the far bank and disappear into the woods toward Youlin Retreat.
"Let's head back, Wenyuan. Youqi Jushi and Mengrong already went into the woods."
Only then did Qiao Wenxian heave himself up and trudge off toward the far bank without waiting for Prince Yi.
Watching his heavy steps, Shen Jianchen thought, "Wenyuan's great in every way except he overthinks everything. If it were me, I'd drop it all and run off to the ends of the earth with the woman I love."
Seeing Young Master Qiao depart, Xiaguo trailed behind him and Prince Yi, with the rest following along.
At Youlin Retreat's gate, Shen Jianchen had Bai Feng drive Kuqiao back to Wangyue Pavilion to inform Matron Wei that Princess Jiahe and Youqi Jushi had hit it off so well the princess had invited her to stay the night and return in the morning.
Any excuse to spend time with Kuqiao delighted Bai Feng. Whipping up the carriage, he laughed and joked with Kuqiao all the way to Wangyue Pavilion.
After reporting her mistress's overnight stay to Matron Wei, Kuqiao rushed to add lest the matron keep her behind: "It's Miss's first time sleeping away from home—I'm worried and need to hurry back to wait on her."
Matron Wei hated the idea of her mistress staying out overnight, yet with Princess Jiahe hosting, she couldn't object. "Folks are wagging tongues everywhere lately; if word leaks that you stayed out overnight, they'll spin even worse filth! Be careful coming back tomorrow so nobody spots you."
Promising she would, Kuqiao rode back to Youlin Retreat with Bai Feng. After stealing a little more playtime with him, she hurried off to Princess Jiahe's courtyard to tend her mistress.
Tonight her mistress was sharing a bed with the princess—at Princess Jiahe's enthusiastic insistence, since she had never shared a bed with a close friend before and wanted to try it.
By the time Kuqiao arrived, Xiaguo and Xilan had already removed their mistresses' makeup and outer robes, leaving nothing left for Kuqiao to do.
Xilan settled Kuqiao and Xiaguo into the maids' quarters. With an easy conscience, Kuqiao drifted off in minutes.
Xiaguo, however, tossed wide awake—not worrying over her mistress, but over Young Master Qiao. Never had she slept so close to him, and she longed to draw closer still.
Slipping out of bed, she dressed in the dark, tidied her hair, and tiptoed out of the maids' quarters.
Out in the yard, she heard her mistress and the princess chatting indoors. Uninterested, Xiaguo crept out through the courtyard gate.
On entering Youlin Retreat earlier, she had memorized the layout and the exact route from Princess Jiahe's yard to Young Master Qiao's quarters.
Finding his courtyard took no effort. Outside the gate, she spared a quick glance at the wooden viewing platform, then peered over the low wall—warm lamplight still glowed inside.
Stepping up to the gate, she found it unlatched and nudged it open with a soft creak.
Nan Shuang's voice rang out: "Who's there?"
Xiaguo froze, answering in a timid voice: "It's me."
