Stepping outside, Nan Shuang recognized Xiaguo by moonlight and lit up, thinking she had come for him: "What brings you here?"
Xiaguo replied, "I have something to tell Young Master Qiao."
Nan Shuang's excitement fizzled on the spot.
From indoors came Qiao Wenxian's voice: "Who's outside, Nan Shuang?"
In a flat, disappointed tone, Nan Shuang called back, "Young Master, it's Youqi Jushi's maid, Xiaguo."
Thinking Shuzhen had sent word, Qiao Wenxian answered, "Let her in."
"Hear that? Young Master says go in."
Nan Shuang watched Xiaguo walk straight past him through the door and trailed right in behind her.
Unable to sleep, Qiao Wenxian had been poring over books at his desk in hopes of finding a way out, turning up nothing. Xiaguo coming by meant maybe Shuzhen had hit on an idea!
"What message did your mistress send?"
Her mistress hadn't sent any message—realizing Young Master Qiao didn't even see her stabbed Xiaguo's heart.
"Why do you look upset?" Dread pricked Qiao Wenxian—could Shuzhen be thinking of giving up on them, despite their vows earlier today?
Gathering her courage, Xiaguo said, "What I have to tell Young Master Qiao can't have anyone else listening."
Qiao Wenxian told Nan Shuang, "Step outside for a minute and shut the door."
Scowling, Nan Shuang tramped outside and pulled the door shut, muttering, "Big deal! So you're a little pretty—what's pretty get you? Still a maid's fate! Think a pretty face is going to marry you to a gentleman like my master?!"
Inside, Qiao Wenxian pressed: "What word did your mistress send?"
Xiaguo dropped to her knees. "Miss didn't send any message. I came because I wanted to see you, Young Master. Watching you grieve over the ruined proposal broke my heart, so I came to check on you..."
Disappointment crashed over Qiao Wenxian, mixed with annoyance: "If that's all, go back."
"Hear me out, Young Master—then I'll leave."
"No need. Leave."
Refusing to waste her one shot to confess, Xiaguo blurted out, "I want Young Master to know that besides Miss, I hold you in my heart too! I don't ask you to love me the way you love Miss—just let me stay by your side and wait on you once you two are together, and I'll be content! And if things with Miss don't work out, please take me in—I'll wait on Young Master for the rest of my life..."
"Shut up! Get out! Get out!" Rage and impatience flared in Qiao Wenxian as he shouted, "Nan Shuang!"
Hearing his master's shout, Nan Shuang pushed the door open to find Xiaguo kneeling on the floor and his master furious, baffled at what had happened.
"Nan Shuang, escort Xiaguo out!"
Rejected, Xiaguo felt crushed yet unrepentant—at least she had spoken her heart. From tonight on, every time Young Master Qiao laid eyes on her, he would remember what she had confessed.
Rising to leave, she froze when Qiao Wenxian called out, "Hold on!"
Hope flared as she spun back—had Young Master Qiao changed his mind?!
Instead, his voice fell cold as stone: "I'll pretend tonight never happened. You forget it too."
Without another word, Xiaguo turned and walked out.
#CH 118
#TITLE: Pillow Talk Into the Night
With Princess Jiahe's warm invitation to share her bed, Qianli Xun neither wanted nor had the heart to refuse.
Too much had piled up over the past two days; she needed a sympathetic ear to vent to, and Princess Jiahe was the ideal confidante.
Not finished talking before bed, the two girls kept right on chatting beneath the quilts like long-lost sisters catching up—with romance, of course, taking center stage.
"Shuzhen..." Dropping the formal "Youqi Jushi" for her given name, Qiao Mengrong asked, "Brother told me the Qiao family called off the proposal. Have you thought about what you and Young Master Qiao will do next?"
"Wenyuan said he won't give up, and neither will I."
"What's your plan?"
"No idea." Aside from primitive technology, Qianli Xun decided the worst flaw of this era was zero freedom in love and marriage. "If there were no parents' orders and matchmakers' words, Wenyuan's and my troubles would vanish in a snap."
"Hard to picture a world where parents stay out of their children's marriages and nobody needs a matchmaker—what would that even look like?"
Qianli Xun rolled over to face Qiao Mengrong. "Young people fall in love freely while parents stay hands-off, only stepping in when it's time to talk wedding plans—and even then respecting what their kids want. Plenty of folks don't even want to date or marry, driving their parents so crazy they'd drag any stranger off the street to get them hitched!"
"Sounds so wild—you talk like you've lived through it!"
"I dream of an age like that, and I believe one day it'll come."
Qianli Xun pictured bringing Wenyuan back to the modern world to taste the freedom to love. Funny how human beings worked: when you had freedom, you took it for granted; only after losing it did you realize how precious that modern life had been.
Thoughts drifting to Prince Yi, Qiao Mengrong asked, "Shuzhen, you've known Brother and me a while now—do you think Brother loves me?"
Prince Yi's flirtatious glances flashed across Qianli Xun's mind. How could he be devoted to Princess Jiahe? Yet she couldn't bear telling her the blunt truth: "You're with Prince Yi every day—can't you feel whether he loves you?"
Qiao Mengrong sighed. "Back when Brother was sick, I knew for a fact he had room only for me. Since he recovered, he can't remember the past—he even forgot our love. Now I'm not so sure how he feels..."
"Wait—Prince Yi had amnesia?!" A strange spark of hope leaped in Qianli Xun: could Prince Yi have some tie to Shen Jianchen?
"Yes. Brother lost his memory and remembers nothing from before."
"How did he lose his memory?"
"Brother was at death's door and breathed his last breath. While I was sobbing my heart out, he miraculously came back to life, remembering nothing. Matron Zou said Brother wandered to the Gates of Hell, King Yama sent him back, and because he'd drunk Mengpo's Soup, his past memories were wiped clean."
"So Prince Yi came back from the dead." Qianli Xun murmured under her breath. After her own wild crossing-over, tales of the Gates of Hell and Mengpo's Soup sounded believable enough.
She dropped the notion that Prince Yi could be Shen Jianchen—and at last understood why an engaged prince kept flirting with her: he had forgotten his old love.
Still, forgetting or not, his engagement to Princess Jiahe stood solid as rock, giving him zero right to flirt with other women.
"Brother still treats me well, just without the warmth he used to have. What should I do?"
Qiao Mengrong's question plunged Qianli Xun into silence. Every life held its hidden thorns: to outsiders, Princess Jiahe's imperial betrothal to Prince Yi looked flawless, yet only the one inside tasted its bittersweet ache.
She and Wenyuan had no betrothal and faced walls on every side, yet their hearts beat as one. Qianli Xun prayed their shared devotion would smash through every barrier.
"Shuzhen, are you asleep?"
"Oh, no. Just thinking—you and Prince Yi have an imperial betrothal from His Majesty, so he's guaranteed to marry you. Compared to most women, you're already lucky, marrying the man you love. Don't dwell on how he changed—remember the advice I gave you before?"
Qiao Mengrong had already forgotten. "What advice?"
"I'll repeat it, so listen close! Once you marry Prince Yi, you're the primary wife. Guard your authority as primary wife tight, and don't sweat him taking concubines—let him take whoever he wants, and your graciousness will win his favor. Don't invest too much heart in him either; the heavier your heart leans on him, the worse off you'll be. Keep a cool head—got it?"
This time the words sank in: "You're right—I can't put too much of my heart on Brother; I have to stay levelheaded. Still, I don't think Brother's the type to take concubines."
Qianli Xun itched to tell her how Prince Yi kept tossing sparks her own way, yet held back lest it poison their friendship. Instead she invented a tale: "Listen, I saw Prince Yi staring at another girl with my own eyes."
Qiao Mengrong froze, choking out after a long beat: "W-where... did you see that?"
Racking her memory for a scene, Qianli Xun landed on Qixi Festival—crowds of pretty girls everywhere!
"On Qixi, when we went strolling. You and Prince Yi walked ahead, and I caught him eyeing a passing girl up and down—creepy look in his eyes, total lecher vibes!"
Thinking back to that day, Qiao Mengrong burst into relieved laughter through her tears: "Oh, that! You misunderstood Brother! He did look at a girl, because I told him to—the Mohouluo clay doll in her hand looked just like Brother when he was little, so he looked twice!"
Trying to spin a caution only to get her serve smashed straight back in her face, Qianli Xun muttered, "Guess I saw wrong, then. Stay sharp anyway, so after you marry him you won't... *yawn*... end up disappointed."
Yawns rolled over Qianli Xun as sleep pulled at her lids.
"Nobody's ever talked to me like this—thank you for caring about me."
"Of course I... care about you... want you to have a happy home... Don't worry, I'll never... come between... the two of..." Murmuring broken phrases, Qianli Xun drifted fast asleep.
Still wide awake, Qiao Mengrong missed her friend's mumbled last words. With her confidante asleep, she turned onto her side and slipped off to sleep too.
...
Having talked into the wee hours, both girls slept right past dawn.
Tactful, the maids left them to sleep undisturbed.
Right as they slept soundest, Qiao Shouren stormed into Youlin Retreat with a crew of brawny servants carrying clubs and ropes—plainly here to haul someone away in bonds.
Butler Li hurried out: "Master!"
Qiao Shouren barked, "Where is Wenyuan's courtyard?"
Sensing disaster with zero chance to tip off his young master, Butler Li bowed: "This way, Master."
Reaching Qiao Wenxian's yard, Qiao Shouren kicked the gate wide open and roared into the courtyard: "Wenyuan! Get out here!"
Having lain awake most of the night and risen at dawn, Qiao Wenxian sat flipping books in vain frustration when his father's shout shook the yard. Opening the door, he stepped out.
One glance at the clubs and ropes told him Father meant to haul him back to Qiao Manor in bonds. "Didn't Father order me to stay at the retreat? Why bring this mob?"
"Ask yourself why! I left you at the retreat to keep Prince Yi company and calm your mind—and here you go letting a woman who has no business here sleep overnight! Today I'm tying you up and hauling you back to Qiao Manor to stay put!"
A spy among the retreat's staff must have run straight to Qiao Manor last night! No surprise—Father had picked every servant here. Qiao Wenxian kicked himself for getting careless.
Before he could utter another word, the servants swarmed in with raised clubs. Knowing no martial arts, Qiao Wenxian couldn't fight back; several big men pinned his arms and legs and trussed him tight with rope.
Struggling in vain, Qiao Wenxian shouted, "Father! Think tying me up will change my heart?! Listen well—I still love Shuzhen, and I'll marry her or nobody!"
Qiao Shouren barked, "Carry Young Master onto the carriage!"
Nan Shuang threw himself to his knees: "Have mercy on Young Master, Master—he's miserable enough!"
Qiao Shouren snapped, "You little rascal—don't think I don't know you've been running messages between Wenyuan and Miss Zhu! Tie him up too and lock them both in Qiao Manor!"
At the command, the servants trussed Nan Shuang up as well.
Just as Qiao Shouren moved to haul his bound son out, Prince Yi's voice cut across the yard: "Why treat Wenyuan like this, Grandfather?"
After leading Master to Young Master's courtyard and seeing trouble brewing, Butler Li had bolted straight to warn Prince Yi.
Up at dawn coaching Bai Feng in martial arts, Shen Jianchen heard Butler Li's panicked report and rushed straight over with Bai Feng.
Bai Feng had itched to jump in and clobber the men tying Young Master Qiao, only for Prince Yi to hold him back: "No need to use the fists I taught you on them."
With that, Shen Jianchen had called out to Qiao Shouren.
#CH 119
#TITLE: Waiting Outside the Gate for His Beloved
Expecting Prince Yi to intervene, Qiao Shouren came prepared, stepping up with a deferential bow: "Your Highness Prince Yi, I'm here to bring Wenyuan back to Qiao Manor. Please do not stand in my way."
Shen Jianchen reasoned, "Of course I won't stop Grandfather from taking Wenyuan home, yet tying him up makes no sense. Wenyuan isn't some wild dandy like Young Master Chen—why treat him like a criminal? Untie him."
Qiao Shouren stood ironclad: "Prince Yi speaks sense, yet without ropes he won't come home quiet. I'm taking him back to lock him up until he willingly lets go of Miss Zhu."
Qiao Wenxian shouted, "Save me, Prince Yi! I don't want Father locking me up!"
Shen Jianchen tried again: "Grandfather can lock up his body, not his heart."
Qiao Shouren countered, "The heart sits inside the body—lock up the body long enough, and the heart gives in too."
Unable to watch Wenyuan suffer, Shen Jianchen pushed: "And what if I stop you by force?"
Seeing Prince Yi wouldn't back down, Qiao Shouren blamed every ounce of this family turmoil on Miss Zhu—if he didn't cut it off now, Wenyuan would sink beyond saving.
Drawing a dagger from his sleeve, Qiao Shouren pressed the blade to his own throat: "If Prince Yi stops me by force, today becomes the day I die!"
Never expecting Qiao Shouren to pull that stunt, Shen Jianchen felt equal parts alarm for his grandfather's life, pity for Wenyuan, and—truth be told—a handy excuse to step back.
"Why resort to something so extreme, Grandfather? I won't interfere."
Torn by anguish, Qiao Wenxian cried, "Don't push Prince Yi, Father—I'll go home with you."
Sheathing his dagger, Qiao Shouren bowed with full respect once more: "Forgive this old man, Prince Yi. I'll take Wenyuan away at once. As of today, I gift Youlin Retreat to Your Highness—Wenyuan won't set foot here again."
With that, Qiao Shouren led his men away, hauling Wenyuan off in haste.
Left standing in the empty yard, Shen Jianchen felt this tucked-away retreat grow lonelier than ever with Wenyuan gone—and the lonelier it felt, the stronger his longing burned to be beside the woman he loved.
Seeing His Highness stride toward Princess Jiahe's courtyard, Bai Feng trailed behind in silence, daring not say a word.
At the gate of her small courtyard, the wooden doors still stood bolted shut.
Standing on tiptoe to peer over the wall, Bai Feng saw the chamber door closed too, with not a maid in sight.
"Princess must still be asleep, Your Highness. Want to head back first?"
"No need. I'll wait outside a bit." Shen Jianchen figured Qianli Xun and Mengrong had stayed up talking past midnight and were sleeping late into the morning.
His mind drifted back before crossing over, to the morning he had hunted down Qianli Xun's apartment in the city, bought a bouquet of roses at dawn, drove his luxury car to her high-rise, and rode the elevator up to the twenty-second floor.
Finding her door, he knocked: *Knock, knock, knock!*
Dead silence inside. Puzzled—his intel swore she was home today—why wasn't anyone answering?
He knocked again—*knock, knock, knock!*—still nothing. Four or five rounds later, Qianli Xun's groggy, sleep-thick voice finally yelled through the door: "Who the hell is hammering like a ghost at crack of dawn?!"
Hearing her voice, Shen Jianchen called out in high spirits: "It's me!"
"What deity on earth disturbs my beauty sleep this early?!"
"Me! Shen Jianchen!"
"Scram! I was writing until dawn and I'm dead tired—quit bugging me!"
Then dead silence again.
Knowing she was home put him at ease. Setting the roses on the floor, he slumped down against the hallway wall, pulled out his phone, and played games while waiting for her to wake up.
He had cleared his whole work schedule today for chasing Qianli Xun—no leaving until he saw her in person.
Around noon, the faint shuffle of slippers sounded inside. Tucking his phone away, he scooped up the roses, smoothed his suit, cleared his throat, and knocked again: *Knock, knock, knock!*
The door swung open to reveal Qianli Xun with wild bedhead, glaring out of wide eyes crusted with eye boogers: "You're still here?!"
"Well? Pretty, right?" Shen Jianchen thrust the bouquet under her nose.
"So tacky! All you ever bring are roses!"
"Tacky? I don't think so. After waiting outside for hours, can't you reward my persistence and let me in instead of blocking the door?"
Qianli Xun swung the door wider. "Come in, put the flowers on the table, and never bring me flowers again—I hate cheesy stuff like that!"
Setting the bouquet down, Shen Jianchen glanced around the apartment: "Way too bare in here. One of these days I'll hire designers to renovate it for you."
"Are you nuts?! Renovating takes time, money, and weeks airing out formaldehyde—when am I supposed to live in it?!"
"Fine, I'll buy you a luxury turnkey condo—just bring your suitcase. Deal?"
"Think having a little cash makes you king, Shen Jianchen? I'm not broke—who asked you to buy me a place?!"
Shen Jianchen realized Qianli Xun was no ordinary girl money could win over.
"Go freshen up—I'm taking you out for a fancy feast."
"Fancy feast, my foot! Acting like I've never eaten decent food! Hate to break it to you, I despise fancy restaurants—save that routine!"
Facing the toughest challenge of his life, Shen Jianchen yielded: "Whatever you want to eat—your call."
Pleased with shutting him down and amused at his helpless look, Qianli Xun cracked a grin: "Haha, I don't feel like going out today. Since you suffered waiting outside so long, I'll cook you a bowl of instant noodles with a poached egg."
"Instant noodles? My specialty! Sit tight—I'll cook." Making her instant noodles with his own hands beat a five-star restaurant by a mile.
Qianli Xun agreed at once and went off to wash up in the bathroom, leaving him the kitchen.
Opening her fridge to find two cartons of milk, a few packs of instant noodles, greens, and eggs, he muttered, "What a Spartan life—get with me and I'll pack your fridge to bursting."
Grabbing the ingredients, he got cooking. By the time Qianli Xun came out fresh and tidy, two steaming bowls of noodles topped with poached eggs sat ready on the table.
Slurping away, Qianli Xun praised his noodles as even better than hers.
Watching the girl he loved eat with such relish, Shen Jianchen seized the moment to confess: "I'd love to cook instant noodles for you for the rest of our lives."
Qianli Xun laughed right back: "Haha, trying to make me die of boredom eating noodles?!"
Remembering that day curved Shen Jianchen's lips into a warm smile—the longest, happiest afternoon he had ever spent with Qianli Xun.
Watching his master beam at thin air baffled Bai Feng, though he dared not ask.
Tiptoeing to peek over the wall again, Bai Feng spotted Kuqiao stepping out of the maids' quarters and waved frantic hands in a stage whisper: "Kuqiao! Kuqiao! Over here! Look here!"
Catching his whisper, Kuqiao glanced around until she spotted Bai Feng's head bobbing above the wall.
Walking over, she unbolted the courtyard gate—finding Prince Yi standing right outside.
Kuqiao dropped into a bow at once: "Prince Yi! Princess and my mistress are still asleep—I'll go wake them."
"No need. I'm fine waiting right here." Shen Jianchen savored the wait; it brought back those bright days from before.
"Let me bring Prince Yi a chair—"
"No need, I'd rather stand. Go play with Bai Feng and leave me be."
"Thank you, Your Highness!" Needing no second invitation, Bai Feng grabbed Kuqiao's hand and vanished around the corner.
Shutting the gate himself, Shen Jianchen stood guard outside with a willing heart, hoping he would be the very first man Qianli Xun saw when she woke.
Stepping out of her own room, Xilan squinted up at the high sun—almost noon! Time to wake the princess.
With the princess and Youqi Jushi sharing one bed, waking one meant waking both; Xilan only cared about tending her own mistress anyway. Pushing open the maids' quarters door, she saw no Kuqiao, only Xiaguo dead to the world, snoring loud.
Thrilled yet stung after confessing last night, Xiaguo had lain awake half the night before crashing hard toward dawn.
Xilan called out, "Xiaguo, get up! Come help me wake the mistresses!"
Zero response—Xiaguo kept snoring away.
Reaching under the quilt, Xilan gave Xiaguo's arm a sharp pinch.
Jolting awake with a yelp, Xiaguo was about to curse when her blurry eyes made out Xilan—the princess's personal maid outranked her, so she swallowed her anger and sat up: "If Sister Xilan wants to wake me, no need to pinch—that hurt!"
"Called you five times and you didn't budge, and Kuqiao's nowhere to be found! Works like magic, doesn't it? Drag yourself out of bed—it's almost noon!"
Letting out a huge yawn, Xiaguo crawled out of bed and threw on her clothes with zero time to tidy up, hurrying off to serve her mistress—resenting her lot in life more than ever. When would she shed this maid's skin and become a mistress waited on by others?
#CH 120
#TITLE: Awkwardness of Misplaced Hearts
Reaching the bedchamber door, Xilan called inside, "Princess, the sun is high—time to rise! Sleep any longer and His Highness might arrive to catch you still in bed."
Xilan knew her mistress inside out: at the mention of Prince Yi visiting, Qiao Mengrong snapped wide awake and scrambled up.
"How did I sleep so late, forgetting Brother might drop by? If he catches me still in bed, how embarrassing! Wake up too, Shuzhen—don't you want to see Young Master Qiao?"
Qiao Wenxian's name worked like instant adrenaline on Qianli Xun, who sat bolt upright: "Right! Barely got two words in with Wenyuan yesterday before you and Prince Yi showed up, and then I talked with you all night—today I'm going to have a proper chat with him."
"Come in, you two!" calling to the maids outside, Qiao Mengrong added with a smile, "Holding a grudge that Brother and I interrupted you yesterday?"
"Not a chance! You two weren't interrupting—you were icing on the cake! Talking with you all night lifted a ton off my chest and pumped me full of positive energy. Even if folk yelled insults in my face on the street right now, I wouldn't flinch—having a royal princess as my bosom friend is armor enough against every rumor in town!"
Entering the room, the maids helped each mistress dress and wash up.
Qianli Xun asked, "Where's Kuqiao? Haven't seen her."
Helping her mistress into her robe, Xiaguo said, "With Bai Feng around, when does Sister Kuqiao ever sit still?"
Already dressed and seated at the vanity, Qiao Mengrong froze at the mention of Bai Feng. If Kuqiao was gone, Bai Feng had come—and wherever Bai Feng went, Prince Yi wasn't far behind! Could he be here right now?
Unable to sit still, she started to rise, caught her untidy hair in the mirror, and urged, "Hurry and do my hair, Xilan—Brother might be right outside the gate!"
Qianli Xun laughed: "Since when do you have clairvoyant eyes, Princess, seeing straight through walls?"
"I can just feel Brother standing right outside." Even Qiao Mengrong couldn't explain where that sudden certainty came from.
Xilan pinned up her hair in record time.
Jumping up, Qiao Mengrong hurried across the yard to the gate, whispering a silent prayer: "If Brother really is standing outside, it proves he has me in his heart."
Waving Xilan back from the latch, she meant to test her prayer with her own hands.
Heart pounding, she eased the wooden gate ajar, squeezing her eyes shut for fear of finding the lane empty...
Xilan's voice rang out: "Your Highness, you really are waiting right outside!"
Qiao Mengrong's eyes flew open to find his tall, sturdy figure standing right before her, real as daylight—he really did hold her in his heart!
Scolding herself for ever doubting him—why else would he wait outside her gate?—she rushed forward and threw her arms around him once more.
"How long has Brother been waiting out here?"
Embraced by the wrong woman, emptiness sank into Shen Jianchen's chest. Mengrong was getting way too fond of hugging him lately; he'd have to keep his distance better.
"Not... not long. Why are you the only one up? Is Youqi Jushi still asleep?"
"Shuzhen's up, just finishing her hair. Somehow I sensed Brother waiting outside, hurried out, and here you are! Doesn't that mean our hearts beat as one?"
Without answering, Shen Jianchen looked past her into the courtyard, longing for the figure he had waited all morning to see.
A moment later, she came walking out—bringing a smile to Shen Jianchen's lips and a pang of sorrow to his heart.
Reaching the gate, Qianli Xun saw Princess Jiahe clinging tight to Prince Yi while his arms hung limp at his sides—and the instant she approached, his eyes locked onto her so hard they looked ready to pop from his sockets.
Qianli Xun scoffed inside: "Blind to the blessing in his own arms! Holding a beauty and still ogling me like that!"
Stepping through the gate past them, she bowed with polite distance: "I'll leave you two—off to see Wenyuan."
With that, she turned to go.
"Wait, Youqi Jushi!" Shen Jianchen called out at once, easing Qiao Mengrong off his chest. "Mengrong, I have words for Youqi Jushi."
Floating on air, Qiao Mengrong agreed: "Go ahead, Brother—I'll step aside."
"No need to step aside. It isn't a secret."
His openness warmed Qiao Mengrong's heart even more, and she nodded.
What a busybody—calling her back right in front of his fiancée! Since he didn't bother hiding, Qianli Xun saw no reason to either; maybe this was her chance to expose his true colors to the princess!
"What does Prince Yi want?"
Stepping closer, Prince Yi fixed her with that same deep, ardent gaze.
Glancing at Princess Jiahe behind him, pity surged in Qianli Xun. She made up her mind to blow his cover right here and now—her mouth opened to say, "Prince Yi, how dare you flirt with me right in front of Mengrong?!"
Before a syllable came out, Prince Yi spoke: "I'm afraid Youqi Jushi won't be seeing Wenyuan anytime soon."
"What do you mean?" His words knocked her accusation straight back down her throat.
"At crack of dawn while you were sound asleep, Grandfather showed up with men, trussed Wenyuan up like a bundle, and hauled him back to Qiao Manor."
"What?! How could that happen?! Aren't you a prince—why didn't you stop him?! Why didn't you send someone to wake me? I would've stopped him myself!"
For the first time, Shen Jianchen saw Qianli Xun cut raw by love—nothing like the woman he knew before crossing over. Would she ever hurt like this over him?
"I tried stopping him. Grandfather pressed a dagger to his own neck—he'd die before budging, so my hands were tied. Besides, what good would waking you do? Did Youqi Jushi mean to throw away her pride and beg the Qiao family to marry her?"
Shen Jianchen spoke harsh on purpose, hoping to snap her out of illusions about Wenyuan—only if she let Wenyuan go might a spark ever open for him someday.
Wincing at his harsh tone, Qiao Mengrong chided him soft: "How can Brother talk to Shuzhen like that?"
Heartache gripped Xiaguo too—poor Young Master Qiao trussed up in ropes! If Miss's hopes died, hers grew dimmer too.
Watching her mistress swallow her grief and keep her voice steady, Xiaguo admired her nerve: "Much as I love Wenyuan, I'd never throw away my pride begging the Qiaos to take me. I just can't fathom it—am I really that awful, that the Qiao family had to tie Wenyuan up just to tear us apart?"
Qiao Mengrong stepped up beside her: "Nothing's wrong with you, Shuzhen—it's just cruel fate."
"Thank you, Mengrong. I'd better head home." Grateful for her friend's kindness, Qianli Xun left Prince Yi a pointed parting line: "You and Mengrong are lucky to hold a betrothal in your hands, Prince Yi—treasure the one right in front of you."
Leading Xiaguo toward the retreat's gate, Qianli Xun kept her face rigid, eyes brimming with tears one blink away from spilling. "Are Wenyuan and I really finished?" she whispered inside.
Then yesterday beneath the Divine Willow flashed back—their matching vows never to give up. Her jaw set: until Wenyuan himself sent word he was giving up, she would never quit.
Watching Qianli Xun walk away in pain tore at Shen Jianchen: "Mengrong, I have to step out."
"Where is Brother going?"
"I'm worried about Wenyuan hauled back in ropes—I'm going to Qiao Manor to try persuading Grandfather again."
"It's lunchtime already—eat before you go."
Sick with worry for Qianli Xun, how could he swallow food? "Missing one meal won't hurt."
Taking a step away, guilt tugged him back toward Mengrong. Turning around, he wrapped his arms around her: "Rest easy, Mengrong—I will marry you and give you a wedding to make you proud."
His very first embrace since waking from amnesia—and another promise to wed her! A windfall of joy swept every lingering cloud from her heart, leaving pure sunshine.
"That's my Brother!"
Releasing her after a beat, Shen Jianchen hurried off after Qianli Xun.
Still floating in bliss, Qiao Mengrong sighed, "Did you see that, Xilan? Brother hugged me on his own at last—his arms are so strong and warm!"
Xilan beamed for her mistress: "Saw every bit of it, Princess! His Highness even promised you a wedding to make you proud!"
"Mid-Autumn is the day after tomorrow—a whole year left until next Mid-Autumn! Time crawls so slow; I wish I could marry Brother tomorrow!"
With no patience to hunt down Kuqiao, Qianli Xun boarded a Youlin Retreat carriage with Xiaguo and asked the driver to take her home.
Striding up to the front gate, Shen Jianchen caught Bai Feng strolling back with Kuqiao and snapped, "Youqi Jushi already left, and here you are keeping her maid—outrageous!"
Bai Feng protested: "Your Highness told me to take Kuqiao off to play, and now you blame me!"
Hearing her mistress had left, Kuqiao cried, "I can't play anymore—I have to catch up to Miss!" and started out the gate.
"Hold on, I'll give you a ride." Stopping Kuqiao, Shen Jianchen barked at Bai Feng, "What are you standing around for? Go hitch a carriage and drive!"
Hurrying out, Bai Feng hitched a carriage in no time, driving Prince Yi and Kuqiao after Youqi Jushi—wondering the whole way whether His Highness was chasing Youqi Jushi for himself or helping Kuqiao.
Chasing Youqi Jushi made zero sense; helping a maid made slightly more, yet who ever saw a royal prince running tiny errands for a maid?
Before long Bai Feng shrugged it off—Prince Yi was unlike any other prince alive; impossible things were just Tuesday for him.
#CH 121
#TITLE: Wenyuan Locked Away Without Freedom
Having trussed his son up tight, Qiao Shouren sat opposite him in silence all the way back to Qiao Manor.
Fury simmered in Qiao Wenxian. Father had gone way too far—he was a respected scholar of Lin'an, yet Father hadn't spared him a shred of dignity, tying him up like a thief and even threatening suicide in front of Prince Yi! Never in all his years had he seen Father like this.
Worse, facing iron stubborness like this made winning his parents' blessing to wed Shuzhen look hopeless.
Remembering Prince Yi's suggestion to elope, Qiao Wenxian kicked himself for turning it down yesterday! Had he agreed and carried Shuzhen off yesterday, none of this would have happened.
Watching his son squirm in ropes hurt Qiao Shouren's heart too, yet he had no choice.
Last night, when his trusted servant at the retreat rushed over reporting Young Master had kept Miss Zhu overnight, panic seized Qiao Shouren lest his son do something reckless.
If the two crossed a line together, there would be no undoing it—even cast out by Zhu Manor, the Zhus would raise hell and force the Qiaos into marriage!
Worse still, what if they eloped? He knew his own innocent son, yet who knew what Miss Zhu was capable of? With a reputation that dark, what wouldn't she try—sleeping overnight at another family's house with two unmarried men under the roof!
How on earth had the Prefect raised such a shameless daughter?! Having been married once already, she knew the ways of the world and had nothing left to lose, whereas pure-hearted Wenyuan stood no chance against her charms.
Lying awake all night in dread, Qiao Shouren had rallied his men at dawn prepared for anything—determined to drag his son home in ropes and lock him up for ten days or half a month until he came to his senses and cut ties with Miss Zhu.
"Quit glaring at me like that. Hate me today if you want; one day you'll thank me."
Qiao Wenxian refused to give his father a single word.
"Forget Miss Zhu. Lin'an is overflowing with fine girls—why cling to her? If you ask me, those street rumors didn't lie one bit! She's no decent girl!"
Hearing Father smear Shuzhen broke Qiao Wenxian's silence: "Scold me all you want, Father, just don't slander Shuzhen! In my eyes, she's the finest woman alive."
"Finest? Does a decent girl stay overnight at a man's house on a whim?"
"Princess Jiahe invited her! She shared a bed with Princess Jiahe and even sent her maid back to tell her matron!"
"So what? What respectable daughter sleeps under someone else's roof overnight?"
"Save your breath, Father—to me, Shuzhen is the best woman in this world."
"Believe that if you like—then sit tight at Qiao Manor! The day you forget Miss Zhu is the day I let you out."
Qiao Wenxian clamped his jaw shut.
Back at Qiao Manor, Qiao Shouren ordered servants to haul his son into his usual bedchamber, nail every window shut, and leave only one small square hatch just wide enough to slide meal trays through.
Inspecting the room to confirm no escape route existed, Qiao Shouren untied his son himself, then had Nan Shuang shoved inside too.
"You stay locked in here with Young Master—wait on him and talk some sense into him. When he stops pining for Miss Zhu, you both come out."
Stepping outside, Qiao Shouren pulled the door shut, and Nan Shuang heard the heavy padlock click home.
"Young Master, Master really locked us in!"
Eerily calm, Qiao Wenxian flopped back onto his bed: "Let him lock us in. Eat when food comes, sleep when tired—Father can lock up my body, never my heart! I'll never give up Shuzhen—see what he can do about it! Think he'll lock me up till I die?"
Nan Shuang grinned: "Great attitude, Young Master! With all this free time, why not teach me chess?"
"Deal!" Qiao Wenxian sounded light, yet agony twisted his chest.
Hearing Master had hauled their son home in ropes, Madam Qian and Concubine Mi hurried to his courtyard, equal parts relieved and worried, calling through the hatch.
Peering through the square opening, Concubine Mi called, "Son, Sister and I came to see you."
Hearing their voices, Nan Shuang stepped out from the inner chamber and spoke through the hatch: "Madam, Concubine Mi, Young Master is lying down inside—please persuade Master to let him out!"
Madam Qian scolded him: "What do you know, boy? Go fetch Young Master—I have words for him."
Nan Shuang ducked back inside: "Young Master, Madam and Concubine Mi want to see you."
Rising from bed, Qiao Wenxian walked to the window, hoping to recruit them to soften his father.
Thoughtful as ever, Nan Shuang pulled over a chair: "Sit while you talk, Young Master."
Sitting down, Qiao Wenxian spotted Concubine Mi's face framed in the hatch: "Mother, Mama, what's the point of visiting? Father locked me up like a prisoner."
Madam Qian pleaded, "Be good, Wenyuan—forget Miss Zhu. She isn't right for you."
"Save your words, Mother. Lock me up for life and I still won't give up Shuzhen."
Concubine Mi fretted: "Why are you so stubborn?! You're giving her your whole heart, yet who knows if she feels the same! Her name outside is mud—yesterday I even heard folk claiming she and Young Master Dongfang carry on behind closed doors..."
"Rubbish! Nobody knows Shuzhen and Brother Dongfang better than me!"
"How could you know? Were you glued to them day and night?" Madam Qian chimed in. "Yesterday Intendant An's wife invited me for tea—her son goes to your salons too. Word is Young Master Dongfang runs those salons just to praise Miss Zhu's verse and collects her poems to spread everywhere! Tell me, if there's nothing going on, why would he do all that?"
Qiao Wenxian barked a bitter laugh: "Give a dog a bad name and hang him! Nothing could be cleaner than Shuzhen and Brother Dongfang—just because he admires her poetry, folk twist it into filth! This world can't tell black from white!"
"Son," Concubine Mi added, "I heard Young Master Dongfang got locked up at home too—Master Dongfang forbade him from holding salons or seeing Miss Zhu ever again."
Qiao Wenxian let out a hollow sigh: "Looks like our six-person poetry circle is done for."
Madam Qian asked, "What did you mutter, Wenyuan? Speak up—Mother can't hear."
Raising his voice, Qiao Wenxian called, "I said instead of wasting breath on me, go tell Father to ignore rumors and go propose to the Zhu family!"
With that, he stood, walked back into the inner room, and collapsed onto bed.
Calling over and over to dead silence, Madam Qian and Concubine Mi gave up and went off to confer with Qiao Shouren.
Out in the courtyard, Qiao Shouren stood watering his flowers at a leisurely pace, calm as could be.
Madam Qian marched up, plucked the ladle from his hand, and passed it to a servant: "With servants all around, why water flowers yourself? Figure out what to do about Wenyuan!"
Taking the ladle right back and scooping more water from the wooden bucket, Qiao Shouren said, "Tending flowers calms the mind—let servants do it and where's the calm?"
Madam Qian protested, "Calm your mind all you like, yet keeping Wenyuan locked up solves nothing."
Watering on, Qiao Shouren chided them: "You two women have zero patience. Locked under our roof, at least we know he can't sneak off to see Miss Zhu. Out of sight, out of mind—give it time and his infatuation will cool."
Concubine Mi worried: "Makes sense, Master, yet Wenyuan won't let go overnight."
Qiao Shouren's jaw set hard: "If a month isn't enough, lock him up a month! Half a year isn't enough, lock him up half a year! A year isn't enough, lock him up a whole year!"
Tears sprang to Madam Qian's eyes: "What if locking him up drives him out of his mind?! He's the Qiao family's only son!"
Concubine Mi nodded fast: "I've really heard of people going mad after being locked up too long! No lie, Master—it's true!"
With both women nagging his ears off, Qiao Shouren lost all mood for gardening, tossed the ladle to a servant, and headed indoors.
Both women trailed right after him: "Master, we can't just leave Wenyuan locked up!"
Halting at the doorway, Qiao Shouren turned around: "Relax—I won't keep him locked up long, a month at most. Use this time to pick a fine girl and nail down Wenyuan's betrothal fast! Once a marriage is set, what does it matter who sits in his heart?"
Madam Qian brightened: "Brilliant as ever, Master! Sister and I will get right on it!"
The two women hurried off one after the other, praying to settle a bride fast so Wenyuan could walk free again.
#CH 122
#TITLE: Paying Another Call on Wangyue Pavilion
Sharing a carriage with a royal prince made Kuqiao stiff as a board, too tense to speak a word out of turn.
Yet being escorted home by a prince himself also filled her with pride—convincing her more than ever that Prince Yi was a fine man.
Mind tangled around Qianli Xun, Shen Jianchen spared the maid no thought; her sole value right now was giving him a rock-solid excuse to approach Qianli Xun.
He had lied to Mengrong about going to see Wenyuan when he was actually heading straight to Wangyue Pavilion. Now he understood: sometimes lies came from sheer helplessness.
He had tried to forget Qianli Xun and give Mengrong his whole heart, and failed flat. Knowing full well he had to wed Mengrong and could promise Qianli Xun nothing, he still couldn't stop gravitating toward her—even if she hated him for it.
Noon was approaching; waking late, Qianli Xun had to be starving. Better bring her food—Juxiang Tower came straight to mind.
"Bai Feng, swing by Juxiang Tower first."
"You got it!" At the name Juxiang Tower, Bai Feng beamed, assuming His Highness meant to treat him and Kuqiao to lunch.
When His Highness didn't take a table and ordered everything packed to go instead, Bai Feng's face fell.
Catching his droop, Shen Jianchen chuckled: "Drop me at Wangyue Pavilion, take some silver, and bring Kuqiao right back to Juxiang Tower for a feast."
Bai Feng lit up like a lantern: "Young Master thinks of everything!"
Kuqiao grew even fonder of Prince Yi—a man of such lofty rank without an ounce of arrogance was rare indeed.
Juxiang Tower's kitchen moved fast; a waiter packed the steaming dishes into food carriers, loaded them onto the carriage, and the three rolled on toward Wangyue Pavilion.
Dropped off at Wangyue Pavilion by the Youlin Retreat carriage, Qianli Xun thanked the driver and walked toward her bedchamber with heavy steps, Xiaguo trailing behind in just as grim a mood.
Shutting her bedchamber door behind her, Qianli Xun left even Xiaguo outside. Xiaguo retreated to the maids' quarters to nurse her own bruise—though one bright spark remained: at least Young Master Qiao knew her feelings now.
Instead of collapsing onto bed, Qianli Xun sat at her desk staring into space. The heavier her chest felt, the wilder her thoughts tangled as she searched for a thread through the maze.
Crossing over alone and stuck in a loveless marriage had left her hungry for real love. Meeting Wenyuan, she had thought she found it—even wondered if crossing over had been fated just to bring her to him—only for reality to slam a fist in her face.
What now? Only two roads lay open: give up, or hold on. So far she had no reason to give up—especially after promising Wenyuan just yesterday not to quit, their vows still ringing in her ears.
When life hit a wall, nobody could save you but yourself. Those four words floated back: "Right Thought, Right Mindfulness."
Was quitting right, or holding on? Too many things in life refused neat labels of right and wrong—maybe because she stood too deep inside the forest of love. Two lines of verse came to mind: "Why can't I tell the true shape of Lu-shan? Because I myself am in the mountain" [TN: Translated by Burton Watson]. That described her right this second.
Master Jingguang had said whether she became Zhu Shuzhen or Qianli Xun came down to actions, and actions sprang from mindset. Zhu Xiyan burning her manuscripts proved Qianli Xun's mindset and choices had already steered her onto Qianli Xun's own path.
Then maybe this trial with Wenyuan was a hurdle Qianli Xun had to cross. If so, she would face it head-on.
Before crossing over, she had always kept a cool head in romance—even Shen Jianchen's relentless pursuit had never rattled her. Crossing over and meeting Wenyuan had made her lose her footing in pure emotion, and reality had just dealt her a hard lesson.
She made up her mind to honor her heart while taking back her reason: "I'll wait one year for Wenyuan. If the Qiao family still won't budge after a year, I'll... let go."
"Young Lady, I heard you got back—have you eaten lunch?" Matron Wei's voice sounded outside.
Qianli Xun rose, opened the door, and returned to her desk.
Stepping in, Matron Wei scanned the empty room: "Where are those two maids? Getting lazier by the day!"
"Kuqiao hasn't returned from Youlin Retreat yet, and I sent Xiaguo to the maids' quarters—wanted some quiet."
"Did you eat lunch at the Qiao retreat?"
"No. Not hungry."
"That won't do! Assuming you ate there, the kitchen didn't cook for you—I'll go have them whip something up right now."
"No need, Matron. Sit and talk with me awhile—your words nourish me better than food."
Matron Wei smiled: "Not afraid I'll disturb your quiet?"
"You've seen the world and see straight through things, Matron—talking with you clears the fog in my head."
Taking a chair, Matron Wei asked, "More trouble weighing on you, Young Lady?"
Qianli Xun nodded. "Word of me staying at the retreat somehow reached Master Qiao. He showed up at dawn with men, tied Wenyuan up, and hauled him back—doubt they'll let him out to see me again."
"Forgive an old woman speaking blunt, Young Lady, and don't take offense: sleeping overnight at the Qiao retreat last night was a mistake. It only gave the Qiao elders more reason to look down on you."
"I didn't overthink it—Princess Jiahe pressed me so warm to stay and talk heart-to-heart, and nothing improper happened."
"Of course I know your character, Young Lady, yet outsiders—above all the Qiaos—won't see it that way. What's done is done; no use stewing, just be more careful going forward."
"Right you are, Matron. I'll watch every word and step from now on."
"The Qiaos are imperial relatives, and Young Master Qiao is the cream of Lin'an's youth—half the noble daughters in town dream of marrying in. Still, this mess showed me the Qiaos' true size: lofty gate, shallow sight, spooked by street chatter and blind to the rare jade right before their eyes! Hmph—better off not marrying into that house! A finer match awaits you."
Matron Wei's fierce loyalty brought a faint smile to Qianli Xun's lips: "You're family, Matron—of course you think I'm wonderful. Outsiders don't."
"Pay street folk's chatter no mind! They just parrot hearsay without knowing a thing about you—chasing gossip after dinner until they get bored and hunt a new thrill, tossing old rumors straight into the dust."
"Listening to you clears the clouds right out of my sky, Matron! I'll stay put inside Wangyue Pavilion until the gossip dies out."
"If you ask me, don't let folk box you in—go out when you feel like it! One lecture I will give you, though: stop putting every private thing into poetry."
"Fair criticism, Matron. From now on, what should be written gets written, and what shouldn't won't see a drop of ink."
"Now you're talking."
Eager to hear Matron Wei's take, Qianli Xun asked, "With the Qiao family acting like this, Matron, what should Wenyuan and I do?"
Matron Wei thought a beat: "I don't doubt Young Master Qiao loves you with all his heart, or that you love him right back. Pity—true love alone counts for nothing in this world. Born into this age, you have to learn to bend with it."
"So I should give up on true love?" Shadow returned to Qianli Xun's face.
"What else? Trying to slam your bare heart against the whole world is like an egg striking stone."
Grief silenced Qianli Xun.
Matron Wei softened her voice: "Since you can't bear throwing away a true heart, set yourself a deadline. If nothing changes by then, walk away clean—that way you'll never look back in regret."
Only then did Qianli Xun say, "Great minds think alike, Matron—I just gave myself a one-year deadline."
Matron Wei beamed: "Just called you a rare jade, and sure enough you already have a head on your shoulders! The Qiaos are fools to miss out—they'll live to regret it! Still, a year drags too long. How about half a year?"
After a quiet beat, Qianli Xun made up her mind: "Deal. Half a year."
Matron Wei stood up: "Rest a minute while I have the kitchen make lunch—hungry or not, you need food in your stomach."
Just as she turned for the door, Kuqiao stepped in: "Miss, Matron—Prince Yi escorted me back and even bought hot dishes from Juxiang Tower! He's waiting outside right now."
Matron Wei blinked in astonishment: "Prince Yi escorted a maid home?! Did my ears deceive me?"
"Not at all, Matron! Prince Yi was heading to see Young Master Qiao anyway, saw Miss had left without me and I had no carriage, so he kindly gave me a lift—and said since Youlin Retreat hadn't served Miss lunch, he brought dishes over."
Matron Wei mused aloud: "Prince Yi sure has a warm heart. Since we moved to Wangyue Pavilion, nobody from Zhu Manor has visited once, yet Prince Yi has called twice."
Knowing Matron Wei's razor-sharp eyes, Qianli Xun feared she might catch on and snapped, "Kuqiao, go tell Prince Yi I'm under the weather and already in bed—send him off."
Kuqiao said, "Prince Yi guessed you'd say that! He told me to tell Miss: if you want news of Young Master Qiao, you'd better let him in."
Qianli Xun fumed inside: "Shameless snake! Fresh out of Mengrong's hug and already swooping in while I'm down!"
Matron Wei urged, "Young Lady, he's Prince Yi—who dares slam the door on a royal prince? Invite him in! And since he brought lunch, it saves me bothering the kitchen."
Needing news of Wenyuan, Prince Yi was her only lifeline right now. Rising with heavy reluctance, Qianli Xun led Matron Wei and Kuqiao out to welcome the man she least wanted—yet had—to see.
#CH 123
#TITLE: Bringing a Feast to Wangyue Pavilion
Leading the household out to Wangyue Pavilion's gate, Qianli Xun found Prince Yi waiting with Bai Feng, who held two food carriers.
Stepping forward, Qianli Xun bowed: "Thank you for escorting Kuqiao home yourself, Prince Yi."
He had told her time and again to skip bowing, and she had agreed—yet every time they met she bowed all the same, unable to forget his lofty rank.
On the tip of his tongue to tell her again not to bow, Shen Jianchen saw the cool reserve on her face—clear notice she meant to hold him at arm's length—and swallowed his words.
"If Youqi Jushi wants to thank me, invite me to share lunch at Wangyue Pavilion. I brought the food."
Before Qianli Xun could waver, Matron Wei spoke fast lest they offend royalty: "Having Prince Yi honor Wangyue Pavilion is our privilege! Forgive me for speaking for my mistress—please come right in!"
With Matron Wei extending the invite, Qianli Xun could not object and stepped aside to let Prince Yi pass.
Delighted, Shen Jianchen strode inside, Bai Feng trailing with the food carriers.
In the dining hall, Kuqiao and Bai Feng set out dish after dish of Juxiang Tower's finest specialties.
Helping himself to a seat, Shen Jianchen said, "Too much food for one man—won't Youqi Jushi join me?"
Qianli Xun turned him down flat: "No appetite, and I'm tired—going to rest. Let Kuqiao and Bai Feng keep Prince Yi company, or Matron Wei."
"Too bad—I already gave Bai Feng silver to take Kuqiao to Juxiang Tower for lunch. As for Matron Wei, I'm sure she has more pressing tasks, don't you, Matron?" Shen Jianchen felt pretty smug about his airtight setup.
Unable to cross a prince, Matron Wei bowed: "Enjoy your meal, Prince Yi, Young Lady. I have chores to attend to and take my leave."
Forced to let Matron Wei withdraw, Qianli Xun still wasn't out of options—Kuqiao wasn't the only maid in Wangyue Pavilion!
"If Prince Yi hates dining alone, I'll summon Xiaguo and a few more maids to wait on you..."
"No need. I have words for Youqi Jushi and don't want onlookers. Bai Feng, take Kuqiao to Juxiang Tower right now!"
At the prince's order, Bai Feng grabbed Kuqiao's hand and bolted.
Qianli Xun grumbled at Kuqiao in her mind: "Bought off that easy?! Wait till you get back—I'll give you an earful!"
Alone at last, Shen Jianchen smiled, stood up, and pulled out a chair beside the table: "Have a seat, Youqi Jushi."
That gesture hit Qianli Xun with a flash of deja vu. Back in her own era, whenever she ate out with Shen Jianchen, he pulled her chair out like that every single time. Why would Prince Yi do the exact same thing? Could...
Catching the flicker of wonder and hope in her eyes, Shen Jianchen knew she had just remembered their past.
He had tried telling her he was Shen Jianchen more than once and failed every time; now he didn't even want to say it. What good would it do when his betrothal to Mengrong still chained him down?
As long as that betrothal stood, what difference did it make whether he was Zhao Pu or Shen Jianchen? Better to approach her as Zhao Pu—that way, every rejection fell on Zhao Pu, not Shen Jianchen.
"Daydreaming? Sit down already."
Drawn toward the pulled-out chair by an odd pull, Qianli Xun sat down: "Why did Prince Yi pull my chair out? A royal prince waited on everywhere he goes—where did you pick up a habit like that?"
"Does a tiny gesture like that need lessons? I'm not an idiot." Sitting back down, Shen Jianchen shrugged: "High-ranking prince I may be, yet I'm down-to-earth with zero airs. Pulling out a chair is nothing—I do it for Mengrong all the time. Force of habit."
"Oh, I see." Clarity snapped back into Qianli Xun's mind. How could he ever be Shen Jianchen? Just a textbook playboy prince.
She had thought heartache had killed her appetite, yet sitting up close to the fragrant, gleaming dishes woke her stomach right back up.
Lifting her chopsticks, she tasted a bite and nodded: "Prince Yi sure knows how to order—looks, smells, and tastes great." Digging in with gusto, she pushed her grief aside for a spell.
Shen Jianchen watched in quiet pleasure. Her hearty appetite hadn't changed one bit—hardly ladylike, yet he adored it. He couldn't stand women who nibbled like sparrows and fussed over appearances at the dinner table.
"Aren't you eating, Prince Yi?"
"Of course—can't waste fine food." Picking up his chopsticks, he joined in.
Neither spoke as they ate, Shen Jianchen drinking in the rare peace of sharing a quiet table with Qianli Xun alone.
Mid-bite, Qianli Xun caught Prince Yi watching her with a look of sheer bliss. Annoyance flared, and she set her chopsticks down: "I'm full, Prince Yi. Enjoy the rest at your leisure."
"Stay until I say my piece."
"What piece?"
"From now on, I'll pass messages between you and Wenyuan." All Shen Jianchen really wanted was a steady excuse to keep seeing her.
Torn between gratitude and suspicion, Qianli Xun asked, "Why is Prince Yi helping me?"
"You never asked why back when you begged favors from me—why ask now?" Shen Jianchen set his chopsticks down too; eating alone while she sat there felt rude.
"That was different. When I asked for help, I only cared if you'd say yes. Now I didn't ask, yet you're volunteering—which means you want something in return, and I'm afraid I can't give it."
"I do want something: I just want Youqi Jushi to know I love one woman with all my heart—loved her the very first time I saw her. I don't ask to possess her; just knowing she knows someone loves her in silence—and maybe thinks of me once in a blue moon—is enough."
After a cold pause, Qianli Xun let out a sharp laugh: "Hmph! Just how many women sit in Prince Yi's heart? Fall in love with every single one you meet?"
"Believe what you want. Only one woman has ever held my heart, and her name is Qianli Xun..."
His declaration sounded absurd to Qianli Xun: "Hmph! Didn't Prince Yi swear to guard my secret? Why keep throwing that name around at every turn? Watch out I don't brand you a liar!"
"I will guard your secret. I only say it because nobody else is here—and because 'Qianli Xun' is a secret belonging to just the two of us, whereas anyone on the street can call you Youqi Jushi or Zhu Shuzhen..."
Qianli Xun smirked back: "Haha, hate to burst your bubble—Kuqiao and Kujue already know! I even told the Lin elders I was Qianli Xun; they just didn't believe me..."
"Why do you always shoot me down like that? Why can't all my sincerity move you even an inch?"
"Save your breath, Prince Yi, and treat Princess Jiahe right. I have lines I don't cross, and I know right from wrong clear as day. Even if Wenyuan and I part ways, your so-called sincerity will never move me—to me, your sincerity isn't worth a single copper coin."
Her words drove a knife straight into Shen Jianchen's chest: "Not worth a copper coin? Hmph... not even one copper coin?"
Qianli Xun stood up: "Never say things like that to me again, Prince Yi—I hate hearing them. Princess Jiahe treats me like her closest friend; how would she feel if she heard you today? Go home and think about repaying her devotion when she nursed you at your sickbed! Don't whisper sweet nothings when you need her, then turn into a total playboy the second you recover!"
Spinning on her heel, she started out—only for Prince Yi to call after her.
"Hold on!"
Stopping to glance back: "What else does Prince Yi want?"
Shen Jianchen stepped right up to her: "Rest easy, Youqi Jushi—I will repay Mengrong's kindness, and I will marry her..."
Qianli Xun scoffed: "Then that settles it! Why keep pestering me?!"
"Has Youqi Jushi never heard of a heart that cannot help itself?"
"Heard it—so what? I don't want to hear another word like this from Prince Yi. Please don't come to Wangyue Pavilion again."
Throwing royal etiquette to the wind, Qianli Xun left him standing there and marched out.
Shen Jianchen called after her into the empty room: "Rest easy—I won't come to Wangyue Pavilion again! I'll send Bai Feng with Wenyuan's news!"
With her gone, the whole table of delicacies turned tasteless as sawdust. Letting out a heavy sigh, he walked out with dragging feet toward the front gate.
Halfway across the yard, he met Matron Wei, who stepped up with a bow: "Did Prince Yi enjoy his meal?"
"Passable." Feeling a nudge to speak, Shen Jianchen said, "Your mistress needs to work on that temper of hers. Lucky for her I'm an easygoing, reasonable prince—pull that attitude on anyone else and she'd be kneeling in punishment for hours."
Matron Wei flattered smooth: "Everyone in Lin'an knows Prince Yi cares for the common folk and has zero airs—we all pray for more princes like you! Our Young Lady's temper does flare now and then; please bear with her. Between the whole city's gossip and the Qiao family's mess, anyone would be on edge."
"Forget it—I won't hold it against her."
Matron Wei paused, then added, "Actually, I waited here on purpose to catch Prince Yi."
"Oh? You have words for me, Matron?"
Matron Wei bowed low once more: "With ugly rumors about our Young Lady still fresh across Lin'an, for the sake of her reputation, I beg Prince Yi not to visit Wangyue Pavilion again..."
Warned away a second time—polite as Matron Wei's tone was, cold loneliness washed over Shen Jianchen: "Very well. I won't come to Wangyue Pavilion again."
Turning with firm yet reluctant steps, he walked out toward the gate...
Matron Wei bowed once more to his retreating back: "My thanks to Prince Yi!"
#CH 124
#TITLE: Family Warmth on Mid-Autumn Night
On Mid-Autumn Festival, wealthy households decked out their pavilions and towers, and every major tavern in Lin'an had been booked full long ago for evening drinking and moon-viewing.
Once night fell, music of strings and reed pipes drifted from every tavern onto the street; crowds thronged beneath dazzling lanterns, flutes and pipes echoed from home after home, and children chased each other laughing down the lanes.
Wangyue Pavilion, by contrast, felt quiet and forlorn.
Even though the kitchen had baked mooncakes, an altar to the Moon Deity stood set in the courtyard with decorations all around, and the maids and page boys laughed together admiring the full moon overhead, Qianli Xun lay sprawled across a bamboo chair, unable to summon a shred of cheer—her gloom draining the color from every corner of the house.
Mid-Autumn meant family reunion, yet she had no home to return to. Wangyue Pavilion didn't feel like home—where was home, anyway? That twenty-second-floor apartment before crossing over? Qianli Fist Manor? Or Zhu Manor? Every one had been home, yet none was home now.
With no answer in sight and the Qiao family blocking her and Wenyuan, sorrow weighed on her all the heavier amid the festival cheer outside.
A drift of osmanthus scent floated into her chamber, reminding her several osmanthus trees grew in Wangyue Pavilion's garden. That rich fragrance lifted her spirits a fraction.
"Fresh out of the oven, baked by the kitchen on Matron Wei's orders—still piping hot!" Carrying in a plate of mooncakes, Kuqiao urged, "Have one, Miss."
"Set it down. I want to walk by the osmanthus trees." With zero appetite for mooncakes, Qianli Xun rose from the bamboo chair and stepped outside.
Setting the plate down, Kuqiao grabbed a cape and followed her mistress out.
Out in the yard, Xiaguo knelt with the other maids and page boys bowing to the Moon Deity, whispering a silent prayer for a chance to be with Young Master Qiao.
Rising from her bow to see Miss and Kuqiao walk past, she scrambled up to follow: "Where is Miss headed?"
Qianli Xun stayed silent.
Kuqiao answered for her: "For a walk by the osmanthus trees."
Seeing her mistress mute in gloom, Xiaguo dared ask no more and trailed behind in silence.
Three osmanthus trees stood in Wangyue Pavilion's garden, branches crowded tight with golden blossoms pouring out rich perfume—sweeter with every step closer.
Qianli Xun circled the three trees, hoping the thick scent might wash away her low spirits.
She had never been the sentimental type before; why had crossing over turned her into such a softy? No answer came.
Completing a lap around the trees, she spotted a pavilion beside them and stepped inside. From the pavilion she could take in all three trees at a glance while waves of osmanthus fragrance drifted straight across her face.
Since moving in, it wasn't that she hadn't had time to explore the garden—she just hadn't had the mood. Without that osmanthus scent, she wouldn't have left her room tonight.
Standing in the pavilion, she lifted her eyes to the night sky. What a huge, round moon! Only now did she realize this pavilion was the prime moon-viewing perch—and why the house bore the name Wangyue Pavilion, the Pavilion for Gazing at the Moon.
"Put out the lanterns."
Exchanging a puzzled look, the two maids asked no questions and snuffed the pavilion's lamps.
With the glare gone, moonlight spilled twice as bright across the garden. Heaven had blessed this festival night with clear skies. What were the folks at Zhu Manor doing right now? And was Wenyuan still locked up?
Just then Matron Wei hurried up calling, "Been hunting everywhere for you, Young Lady! A maid said you headed this way, and here you are—come to the front courtyard! Guests are here to see you!"
Guests? Who? Not Prince Yi—he had promised never to come back. Could it be her family?
Qianli Xun hurried down the pavilion steps: "Who came?"
Matron Wei kept the surprise: "You'll see in a minute, Young Lady!"
Quickening her steps out of the garden, Qianli Xun tried guessing who waited ahead...
Entering the reception hall in the front courtyard, she saw Madam Xiang and Chen Wenxin sitting there—and joyful tears sprang straight to her eyes as she bowed: "Mother, Sister-in-law, thank you for coming to see me..."
Allergic to tearjerkers, Chen Wenxin teased, "Why act like strangers—bowing and thanking us as if Mother and I aren't family! If we weren't family, why on earth would we trek over here at night?!"
Laughing through her tears, Qianli Xun rushed up to hold Madam Xiang's hands: "I missed you so much, Mother! Haven't been out long, yet it feels like ages since I saw you."
Tears shimmered in Madam Xiang's smiling eyes: "You don't blame Mother for taking this long to visit, Shuzhen?"
"How could I? A daughter ought to go home to see her mother—I'm the unfilial one making Mother trek all the way out here."
Madam Xiang stood and looked her over: "You've gotten thinner! Eat more—with Mother not beside you, take good care of yourself."
"Don't fret over me, Mother—I eat great and sleep great. Look at you, though, more silver hairs at your temples! Quit worrying so much."
Chen Wenxin chuckled: "Mother, Shuzhen, quit standing around—sit down so we can have a proper chat!"
Everyone took seats.
Matron Wei signaled a maid to bring out the fresh mooncakes.
Madam Xiang beckoned: "Sit with us too, Matron—it's Mid-Autumn, merrier with the whole family together."
Taking a seat, Matron Wei said, "Try these mooncakes, Madam and Young Madam, and bring our Young Lady good fortune! Living out here alone on Mid-Autumn, I knew she felt lonely, yet baking mooncakes was all I could do. While baking, I wished that whoever visited tonight would eat a mooncake so our Young Lady's life ahead turns round and complete!"
Without a second thought, Madam Xiang and Chen Wenxin each picked up a mooncake and ate every bite with heart, silently blessing Shuzhen's future with fullness and peace.
Watching her two loved ones eat those mooncakes thawed Qianli Xun's chest, making all her earlier gloom look silly. Where was home? She had her answer now: wherever family stood, there was home.
Finishing first, Chen Wenxin dabbed her lips with a silk handkerchief: "Shuzhen, guess what I was thinking while eating that mooncake?"
"How could I guess Sister-in-law's thoughts?"
"I was thinking maybe the Qiao match falling through is a blessing in disguise—a better match is waiting for you! The Qiaos aren't the only fine family in Lin'an; our Chen house isn't shabby either!"
"Sister-in-law wants me to marry into the Chen family?"
"Why not?!"
"Quit joking, Sister-in-law—a guy like Chen Haoyu couldn't pass my inspection in a million years!"
"Haoyu isn't the only Chen boy! My third uncle's son Haofei is a gem—hates business, loves books, well-read as can be. Worth considering!"
"Sounds like Sister-in-law is bored stiff, playing matchmaker!"
Chen Wenxin appealed to Madam Xiang: "What do you think, Mother?"
Finishing her mooncake, Madam Xiang laughed while chiding her: "I'm taking it as a joke—your third uncle's boy is five whole years younger than Shuzhen! Haha!"
Dropping the match, Chen Wenxin grinned: "Just trying to make Shuzhen laugh!"
"Leave it to Sister-in-law to joke about something like that—aren't you afraid I'll take you up on it?"
"Even better if you did!"
Bad match aside, her sister-in-law's warm intent cheered Qianli Xun up more: "Coming over here tonight, Mother and Sister-in-law—won't Father be mad?"
Madam Xiang smiled: "Silly child, if your father objected, could we have walked out the gate? Acting stern and sending you out was all a show for outsiders—he worries about you every single hour."
Chen Wenxin chimed in: "True! Cut Father-in-law some slack—he's the Prefect of Lin'an and head of the Zhu house. With a scandal this loud, he had to put on a stern stance to answer to outsiders."
Qianli Xun had long stopped blaming Zhu Xiyan, though she put on a mock pout: "Father only cares about answering to outsiders—doesn't he owe his own daughter an answer too? Throwing me out just gave folks more to laugh at."
Hearing no real grudge left in her voice, Matron Wei beamed: "As the ancients say, fortune and misfortune lean on each other. Maybe this storm is Heaven's exam for you, Young Lady! Facing a whole city's gossip without flinching shows true grit; handling the Qiao mess with a cool head instead of weeping and threatening suicide shows real wisdom. If I were the examiner, I'd give you top honors! Haha!"
Half joke, half wisdom—Matron Wei's words struck home. Qianli Xun vowed inside to tackle every test life threw at her with clear judgment. Right now, the best answer for Wenyuan was patience.
Bringing up the Qiao family with care, Madam Xiang asked, "What are your thoughts on Young Master Qiao, Shuzhen?"
"I'm giving myself half a year. If nothing moves in half a year, I'll... let go."
Madam Xiang nodded: "Sensible—just hard on you."
"I'm glad you're keeping a cool head, Shuzhen. A gem like you—the Qiaos are the losers for backing out! Lucky whichever boy wins you next." Chen Wenxin silently sighed: if only her own brother had half a spine she'd pitch him right now, yet with Haoyu's wild streak and the way those two clashed, she couldn't even open her mouth.
"Sister-in-law's family—of course you praise me."
Wangyue Pavilion no longer felt cold or empty; family warmth filled every corner.
Kuqiao beamed too—Miss wasn't alone, the Zhus still loved her.
Only Xiaguo couldn't raise a smile. Half a year—what if nothing happened in half a year and Miss gave up Young Master Qiao? What on earth would Xiaguo do then?!
#CH 125
#TITLE: Huayu Comes for Poems and Eats Roast Chicken
They chatted a while longer until Madam Xiang realized they shouldn't leave Zhu Xiyan and Zhu Jingyuan sitting alone on Mid-Autumn night, and rose to go.
"Now that Mother knows the way, I'll come visit again soon."
Qianli Xun and Matron Wei saw them out to the gate and into their carriage.
Just as the driver flicked the reins, Qianli Xun called out, "Wait!"
When the carriage halted, she stepped up and asked, "Sister-in-law, there's a house right nearby called Yongning Residence—does it belong to the Chen family?"
Poking her head out the window, Chen Wenxin said, "The Chen clan owns so many houses I can't keep track—why ask?"
"Oh, nothing. My maid happened to see Chen Haoyu walk into Yongning Residence, so I got curious."
"If Haoyu went in, it's bound to be a Chen house. Having estates everywhere is why that rascal never sleeps at home! Lately he's turned over a new leaf, though—moved back to Chen Manor swearing he'll behave and stay home! Sun's rising in the west, haha..."
"Thanks for telling me, Sister-in-law." Hearing Chen Haoyu had holed up at Chen Manor eased Qianli Xun's mind; they probably wouldn't bump into each other anymore.
Madam Xiang dared not look back at her daughter lest her tears show and dampen Shuzhen's mood.
The Zhu carriage rolled off, wheels rumbling down the lane until it vanished into the night.
Just as the clatter faded and Qianli Xun turned to head inside, a voice hailed her: "Wait, Youqi Jushi!"
Stepping out of the Mid-Autumn moonlight came a familiar boy—Dongfang Chenze's attendant, Huayu.
"How did you track me down here?"
"My master swore Youqi Jushi would compose poems on Mid-Autumn night! Old Master locked him indoors with no freedom to come out himself, so he sent me to beg a poem. Didn't know where you moved, so I ran to Youlin Retreat to ask Young Master Qiao—didn't find him, got your new address instead, and rushed over to try my luck!"
"Why did your master get locked up?"
Huayu told the plain truth: "With everyone gossiping about Youqi Jushi, Old Master worried my master would get dragged in, so he locked him up."
So Dongfang Chenze got grounded over her! "Come inside—I'll write two poems for you to take back."
Huayu beamed—his trek hadn't gone to waste!
Unwilling to stay up late, Matron Wei turned in early. Kuqiao seated Huayu in the reception hall while Xiaguo followed her mistress to the bedchamber to tend ink and brush.
Walking back, Qianli Xun already knew which two Mid-Autumn poems of Zhu Shuzhen's to write.
Grinding the ink fresh and soaking the brush tip, Xiaguo presented it with both hands.
Taking the brush, Qianli Xun wrote down two Mid-Autumn poems:
Since autumn came I've lingered sick in bed, / No easy thing to reach Mid-Autumn's night. / To gaze upon the moon that shines tonight, / I climb the tower of last night once more.
Thick dew dims shadows of the wutong tree, / Fine breezes float the sweet osmanthus scent. / Do not regard it with a casual eye: / Even Chang'e knows what sorrow means. —"Mid-Autumn" [TN: Translator's rendering]
It claims the fullest splendor of the fall; / Heaven's hand does show favor, after all. / Its pure light reaches out a thousand li, / A bright white orb rounded in perfection.
Through chill the jade hare works its pestle still; / Grown old, the moon toad stands yet stronger still. / I fear I cannot drink my fill of it, / For once it passes, a whole year must turn. —"Enjoying the Moon at Mid-Autumn" [TN: Translator's rendering]
Watching her mistress write, Xiaguo recognized every character yet missed the deeper layers. She vowed to keep studying hard until she could grasp every poem and even offer critiques—worthy of Young Master Qiao someday.
Once her mistress laid down the brush, Xiaguo blew the ink dry, folded the sheets neat, and carried them to the reception hall.
Inside, Huayu was munching mooncakes while chatting with Kuqiao.
"Sister Kuqiao, heard you and Bai Feng are an item..."
Kuqiao flushed red: "You little kid—quit talking nonsense!"
"Bai Feng told me himself!"
"Why does that blabbermouth tell everyone?! Wait till I see him next—I'll teach him a lesson!"
"Judging by your face, Sister Kuqiao, it's true!" Huayu grinned through a mouthful of mooncake.
"What does a kid know? Eat your mooncake!"
"I'm no kid! Same age as Nan Shuang—if he can crush on Xiaguo, why can't I tease Sister Kuqiao and Bai Feng?"
Walking in just in time with a smile on her face, Xiaguo snapped cold at the mention of Nan Shuang: "Keep my name out of Nan Shuang's mouth! I wouldn't look twice at him—tell him to drop it!"
Swallowing his last bite, Huayu hopped up and sidled over with a cheeky grin: "What does Sister Xiaguo think of me, then?"
"Say one more silly word and you don't get Miss's poems!"
Huayu backed off at once: "Hand them over, Sister Xiaguo! My master's locked indoors moping—he's counting on Youqi Jushi's poems to cheer him up!"
"No more nonsense from you!"
"Never again, I swear!"
Xiaguo handed over the folded sheets: "Here. And if you see Nan Shuang, tell him to stop acting like a toad dreaming of swan meat."
Taking the papers, Huayu winced: "Can't deliver that message, Sister Xiaguo! How is Nan Shuang a toad? Bai Feng told me poor Nan Shuang is locked up in Qiao Manor right alongside his master—haven't even laid eyes on him."
"Enough chatter! Take Miss's poems and go—and don't you dare gossip about Miss to outsiders!"
"Not my style—besides, my master would skin me if he heard!" Huayu called back as he headed out.
After seeing Huayu off, both maids returned to help their mistress get ready for bed.
Warm family love, loyal friends sending for her poems even while the city jeered—Qianli Xun felt lucky all over again, refusing to stew in gloom beneath a full Mid-Autumn moon.
Only one ache lingered: her future with Wenyuan. Yet worrying changed nothing—she couldn't storm Qiao Manor and bust him out! Tempting as it sounded, fists couldn't solve everything; she had to wait on fate.
Once the maids tucked her in, they turned in too.
Lying in bed pondering destiny, Qianli Xun felt sure she was walking Qianli Xun's own path now. Even if fate denied her Wenyuan, she would summon the grit to accept an ending she never wanted.
Tucking the two poems inside his robe, Huayu strolled home on foot. Mid-Autumn night markets blazed all night every year—why not enjoy the bustle while he was out?
Mid-stroll, someone called out: "Huayu!"
Spinning around, he spotted An Mingxuan and Cao Ban each munching a candied hawthorn skewer.
"Greetings, Young Masters!"
An Mingxuan said, "Thought that was you! If you're out, where's Brother Dongfang?"
"Still locked up at home, unlike you two free birds! I don't get it—you two love Youqi Jushi's verse just as much, so why didn't your parents lock you up too?"
Popping the last candied hawthorn into his mouth, Cao Ban barked, "Watch it! Putting a curse on us?!"
Huayu bowed fast: "Wouldn't dare!"
An Mingxuan chided him: "Your master's locked up miserable, and here you are roaming the night market instead of waiting on him—isn't that awful?"
Huayu defended himself: "I'm not out playing! My master gave me a mission—swore Youqi Jushi would write verse on Mid-Autumn night and sent me to get some!"
Both spoke in unison: "Did you get any?"
Huayu puffed out his chest: "When I'm on the case, nothing fails! Scored two whole poems!"
"Two?!" An Mingxuan gasped. "Show us right now!"
Huayu named his price: "Sure, you can look—treat me to sour plum drink and roast chicken first!"
Cao Ban waved a hand: "Easy! Come on, let's get your drink and chicken!"
At a nearby stall, Cao Ban bought Huayu two bowls of sour plum drink, which Huayu downed like a man crossing a desert. Finishing their skewers, the two young masters then walked him over to pick out a roast chicken.
Chicken in hand, they ducked into a teahouse. Once Huayu pulled out the poems, each scholar grabbed a sheet and read like starving men, then paid the waiter for brush and paper to copy both regulated five-character poems down.
Chomping roast chicken as he watched them, Huayu shook his head: rich boys didn't know real life, fussing over useless poems when hot roast chicken beat verse by a mile!
Done copying, the two scholars returned the originals, told Huayu to send Brother Dongfang their regards, and dashed off in a rush.
Tucking the poems back in his robe, Huayu reached for his chicken—only for the waiter to step over: "Pot of tea, guest?"
"Nah, I've got roast chicken."
"You didn't buy that chicken here! Taking up a table without ordering tea—isn't that bad form?"
"Those two young masters didn't order tea either!"
"They paid for brush and ink—what did you pay? We're running a business, not a charity!"
Wrapping the rest of his chicken, Huayu huffed off muttering, "Money-grubbing snob—careful you don't fall down a coin hole and get stuck!"
Clutching Youqi Jushi's brand-new poems, the two young masters beamed—now they had a ticket to the Mid-Autumn Poetry Banquet!
With Brother Qiao and Brother Dongfang both locked up and Youqi Jushi under fire across town, they had planned to skip the banquet and just wander the streets; landing two fresh gems like these, skipping the banquet would be a crime!
Halfway there, Cao Ban hesitated: "A circle of literati chipped in to host this Mid-Autumn Poetry Banquet—we didn't pitch in a coin. Won't they kick us out?"
An Mingxuan waved it off: "Forgot we paid last year? They'll give us face—especially with new poems in hand!"
"True, yet what if folk turn up their noses at Youqi Jushi's poems with all the rumors flying?"
"Do you believe street gossip?"
Cao Ban shook his head: "Not a word."
"If you don't buy it, literati above our level certainly won't turn away masterpieces over market chatter!"
Convinced, both strode straight toward the tavern hosting the Mid-Autumn Poetry Banquet.
#CH 126
#TITLE: Fame of the Storyteller Scholar Spreads
Just as An Mingxuan predicted, the literati at the Mid-Autumn Poetry Banquet refused to let street gossip cloud their judgment—pouring praise on Youqi Jushi's poems instead.
One scholar marveled that no storm of rumors could break Youqi Jushi's spirit, letting her pen a line as transcendent as "Do not regard it with a casual eye: even Chang'e knows what sorrow means."
Another praised how, while market folk wagged tongues, Youqi Jushi met the world with a smile and enjoyed the Mid-Autumn moon—a breadth of spirit to envy.
Above all, the couplet "Through chill the jade hare works its pestle still; grown old, the moon toad stands yet stronger still" drew raves: "chill" and "old" mirrored harsh reality, yet the jade hare pounded its elixir undaunted by autumn frost, and the moon toad grew firmer with age—radiating an indomitable grace.
Both young masters showed off their critical flair too, reveling until the small hours before exhaustion sent them home to bed, while other scholars kept drinking, gazing at the moon, trading verse, and debating until dawn.
Stepping out of the tavern, they heard Lin Zixiao hail them: "Hold on, you two young masters!"
Recognizing him, both bowed: "Greetings, Second Uncle Lin. What brings you to stop us?"
Lin Zixiao spoke straight: "Have either of you seen Youqi Jushi?"
Both shook their heads: "Not in person."
"Then where did you get her poems?"
Cao Ban smiled: "No need for Second Uncle to worry over that."
"I'm not prying—just want to ask you to pass a message. She lived under the Lin roof once, so we were family. Hearing how folk smear her, how the Qiao match fell through, and how the Prefect cast her out—life's hard for a young woman on her own. Reading her poems tonight showed me Youqi Jushi isn't someone hardship can break. I admire her."
Moved by Lin Zixiao's words, An Mingxuan said, "Tell us whatever you'd like to pass along, Second Uncle Lin, and we'll deliver it."
"Tell Youqi Jushi this: no matter what outsiders say, neither I nor anyone in the Lin family will ever breathe a single bad word behind her back. Tell her too that rumors are like a gust of wind—here one moment, blown clean away the next time the wind shifts."
