Cao Ban teased, "Can you speak for the whole Lin clan?"
"With my eldest brother away, I'm the senior elder of the Lin house—which junior dares say 'no' to me?"
Sharing a smile, both young masters promised, "We'll deliver every word. Farewell, Second Uncle Lin."
...
In the days that followed, Qianli Xun did not set foot outside Wangyue Pavilion. Between eating, sleeping, and daily routines, she poured herself into writing huaben scripts, jotting down a poem or ci of Zhu Shuzhen's now and then to pass the hours.
Prince Yi sent Bai Feng over twice—both times visiting Kuqiao while delivering updates on Wenyuan. The first time, Wenyuan was still locked indoors; the second, the Qiaos let him step out into his courtyard, though not beyond its gate.
Worry mingled with relief in Qianli Xun: stepping out into his yard meant progress. Soon he'd step past the gate, out of Qiao Manor, and straight to Wangyue Pavilion. She decided to wait with patience for the day he showed up at her door.
Wenyuan did not arrive—instead, the three young masters paid a call.
After Dongfang Chenze's parents investigated and confirmed their son had kept spotless propriety with Youqi Jushi, they pressured him into accepting a betrothal, releasing him only once the match was sealed.
Guilt tugged at Qianli Xun: "Getting Young Master Dongfang dragged into my mess—I'm truly sorry."
Dongfang Chenze seized the opening: "Empty apologies don't count, Youqi Jushi—give me something real!"
"Name it, Young Master Dongfang—anything within my power, you've got it."
"From now on, every new poem or ci you write comes to me first."
"Deal! I'll send a maid over to your manor whenever I finish one."
"No need—I'll have Huayu come collect them on a schedule."
An Mingxuan laughed: "Scoring a bargain like that, Brother Dongfang—being locked up a few extra days would've been worth it!"
Cao Ban added, "Brother Dongfang ought to thank Youqi Jushi! Without this fuss, would you have gotten betrothed to the girl you love so fast?"
A rare, bright smile broke across Qianli Xun's face: "What? Young Master Dongfang got engaged to the girl he loves? Tell me everything!"
An Mingxuan filled her in: "It's Brother Qiao's eldest younger sister! Brother Dongfang and she have had feelings for each other ages without daring to tell their parents—who knew his parents would march straight to Qiao Manor to propose, and the Qiaos said yes on the spot! Isn't that grand?"
Qianli Xun felt genuine joy for Dongfang Chenze—parental blessing AND mutual love, a rare prize! Yet mention of Qiao Wenxian sent a quiet pang through her chest.
Catching the shadow on her face, Cao Ban shifted gears fast: "On Mid-Autumn night, An Mingxuan and I got hold of your two new five-character poems and brought them to the Mid-Autumn Poetry Banquet—everyone raved! On our way out, Second Uncle Lin even asked us to pass you a message."
An Mingxuan picked it up: "Second Uncle Lin said no matter how folk gossip, neither he nor the Lin family will ever badmouth you behind your back—and that rumors are just a passing gust of wind, gone the second the next breeze blows."
"How kind of Second Uncle Lin. Next time I see him, I'll thank him in person."
Warmth swelled inside Qianli Xun at Lin Zixiao's kindness, grateful fate had placed so many good, loyal people around her: Lin Zixiao, these three young masters, and Miao Zhiyu too—she wondered if Miao Zhiyu had seen Wenyuan yet?
"Have any of you seen Lady Miao lately?"
Dongfang Chenze said, "I just got let out of lockup—no idea how she's doing."
Cao Ban replied, "I spotted her on the street once with a cluster of female friends; looked in good spirits."
An Mingxuan said, "Without you and Brother Qiao, our poetry salons fell apart. Lady Miao only mixed with us at salons anyway, so we haven't heard much."
"No matter—just asking in passing since we all met through salons."
Dongfang Chenze urged, "Don't stay cooped up inside Wangyue Pavilion all day, Youqi Jushi—go out for a breath of air! Even head down to watch the Qiantang River tidal bore; crowds have been flocking there lately."
"I'll go take a look when I get the chance." Qianli Xun agreed out of politeness, though she had zero mood for tide-watching.
Out of the blue, Cao Ban changed the subject: "Have you guys heard of a scholar named Qianli Xun lately?"
An Mingxuan lit up: "Of course! Writes huaben scripts—gripping plots! Huge crowds flock to hear them; I went myself and loved it! Right now every storyteller in Lin'an is performing his story, and listeners come back again and again dying for the next installment!"
That was the sweetest news Qianli Xun had heard in weeks.
Dongfang Chenze chimed in: "While I was locked indoors, my cousin—a storytelling fanatic—kept retelling Qianli Xun's tale, and it really is full of twists! Shame everyone knows his name while nobody's seen his face—I'd love to meet him one day."
Cao Ban laughed: "Prepare for disappointment, Brother Dongfang! Qianli Xun is mysterious as a ghost—sends every new script through a maid, so not even the storytellers have ever laid eyes on him!"
Dongfang Chenze's eyes sparkled: "That just makes me itch to dig him out even more!"
Qianli Xun jumped in fast: "If you ask me, Master Qianli Xun probably hates the spotlight. Better respect his wishes and just enjoy his stories! I've listened to his scripts too and loved them—I'll go listen again when I can."
All three young masters gaped at Youqi Jushi.
Dongfang Chenze blinked: "Youqi Jushi used to turn her nose up at lowbrow stuff—since when did you take a shine to it?"
"People change! I used to look down on lowbrow works, too. Now I figure whatever ordinary folk love is good work—highbrow 'Spring Snow' versus lowbrow street art, what's the point drawing such snobbish lines?"
Back before crossing over, Qianli Xun wrote web fiction—a world where elitist critics argued endlessly over whether web novels counted as literature. In her book, elitist opinions meant nothing; the verdict of time and everyday readers was what lasted.
Just like here in the Song dynasty: Zhu Shuzhen's verse lived among elite scholars, yet common folk never spared her a thought until rumors broke—and even then only knew one line, "falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed."
Qianli Xun was different: ordinary folk talked about her because her stories spoke straight to their hearts.
Her words left all three young masters nodding in genuine admiration.
After chatting a bit longer, they took their leave, returning quiet to Wangyue Pavilion.
Back at her desk writing huaben scripts, knowing readers loved her work fired up her pen—giving her something meaningful to fill the hours, push sorrow aside, and wait for brighter days ahead.
#CH 127
#TITLE: Delivering a Memorial to Zhu Manor
Lately Shen Jianchen had stayed away from Wangyue Pavilion, yet his heart never left it for a moment; twice he had sent Bai Feng over with news of Wenyuan.
Passing messages was the sole fragile thread tying him to Qianli Xun now.
He couldn't bear a life severed from Qianli Xun.
Shen Jianchen despised his own weakness—rejected over and over, yet still unable to let go, reason crumbling to sand before his heart.
He felt guilty too: holding Qiao Mengrong in his arms and promising twice to marry her, while every hour secretly praying for a miracle to dissolve that betrothal and set him free to chase Qianli Xun.
He had visited Qiao Manor twice. The first time, right after leaving Wangyue Pavilion, he hadn't seen Wenyuan at all, only chatting with Qiao Shouren to smooth feathers.
From Qiao Shouren he learned the Qiaos were busy picking a bride for Wenyuan, with several promising young ladies already shortlisted for final review.
A wicked rush of gladness had warmed Shen Jianchen—followed by guilt for rejoicing over his friend's misery. Reason shouted it down; his heart ignored reason.
On his second visit, Wenyuan had been allowed out into his own courtyard. When Shen Jianchen asked to see him, Qiao Shouren couldn't refuse and escorted Prince Yi to his son's courtyard himself.
A heavy padlock hung on the gate, flanked by two guards. Overhead, fresh brambles bristling with thorns lined the man-high wall to stop Wenyuan climbing out. Grandfather sure left no loopholes.
With Prince Yi inside, Qiao Shouren couldn't lock the gate from outside, so he stood sentry in the doorway himself without blinking.
Wenyuan sat in the yard soaking up the sun with eyes shut, whole frame thinner by a size, with a listless Nan Shuang beside him.
Spotting Bai Feng walk in, Nan Shuang bolted over in glee: "Bai Feng! You and Prince Yi came at last! Locked up ten whole days—I'm dying of boredom!"
Bai Feng grinned: "Came just to cure your boredom!" The two drifted to one corner of the yard to catch up.
Hearing Prince Yi arrive, Qiao Wenxian opened his eyes, stood, and hugged him: "You came at last! Did you bring word from Shuzhen?"
"Youqi Jushi refuses to see me—where would I get word? I came to check on you."
Disappointment dropped Qiao Wenxian's arms: "She isn't thinking of giving up on me, is she?"
"What do you plan to do?" Shen Jianchen had no intention of sharing how Qianli Xun was doing.
"With Father standing right at the gate, we can't talk out here—come inside." Qiao Wenxian tugged Prince Yi indoors and shut the door.
"What do you want to tell me?"
"I regret turning down your idea. I've made up my mind to elope with Shuzhen—you have to help me, Prince Yi..."
"No!" Before Wenyuan finished, Shen Jianchen cut him off sharp. He had regretted pitching elopement the second it left his mouth—like hell he'd let it happen for real!
Baffled, Qiao Wenxian asked, "Why not? Weren't you on my side? It was your idea!"
"Things have changed! Grandfather pulled a dagger on himself—help you now and I'd be risking Grandfather's life! Why don't you... why don't you let Youqi Jushi go..."
"Never! Shuzhen promised me herself she won't give up—I trust her, and I won't give up either!"
"I came today to tell you one thing, Wenyuan: from now on I'm staying out of your and Youqi Jushi's business. You two are on your own."
With that, Shen Jianchen pulled the door open, called Bai Feng, and strode straight out.
Nan Shuang yelled after him, "Leaving already?! Can't you stay and chat five more minutes?!"
The second Prince Yi stepped past the threshold, the padlock snapped shut behind him.
Stepping out into the yard drained of life, Qiao Wenxian muttered, "Even Prince Yi won't help me anymore—what do I do now?"
Nan Shuang hurried over: "Young Master, why not just give in to Master and stop pining for Youqi Jushi..."
"Get lost! Far away from me!" Qiao Wenxian shoved Nan Shuang back. "I'll never give up! Until my dying breath I won't give up—unless Shuzhen tells me to my face she's quitting, I'll hold on forever!"
Nan Shuang grumbled under his breath, "Get lost where? Gate's padlocked—I'm stuck right in here with you! When will this end?"
Leaving Qiao Manor, Shen Jianchen's carriage rolled toward Wangyue Pavilion on autopilot. Unwilling to step inside, he sent Bai Feng to tell Kuqiao what he had seen at Qiao Manor.
Bai Feng asked, "What should I tell Kuqiao, Your Highness?"
"Just tell her Young Master Qiao is allowed out into his courtyard now."
"Got it."
Going inside Wangyue Pavilion, Bai Feng delivered the message to Kuqiao. With His Highness waiting outside, he dared not linger—exchanging just two words before hurrying back.
Ever since the rumors started, Shen Jianchen had been plotting not only how to catch the culprit, but how to kill the gossip cold across the whole city.
Last night he had struck gold and stayed up drafting a throne memorial—now he needed Zhu Xiyan's backing at Zhu Manor.
Bai Feng steered the carriage straight for Zhu Manor.
Just back from the prefecture office, Zhu Xiyan had caught more whispers about Shuzhen on his ride home, putting him in a black temper. Changing out of his robes, he slumped onto his couch simmering.
Half a month had passed since he tasked Lin Jue with hunting the rumor-mongers, turning up zero leads. His own Zhu Manor servants had proven useless too, so he had called them all back rather than waste manpower.
Burning with hatred for the smears yet stuck without a fix, he could only wait on Lin Jue.
Even if Lin Jue caught someone, beating one culprit wouldn't shut the whole city's mouths—he needed something huge to yank public attention away for good.
Just then a servant announced, "Master, Prince Yi is inside the manor waiting for you!"
Zhu Xiyan scrambled back into his official robes and rushed out—finding Prince Yi waiting right outside the main hall. Stepping inside himself meant serious business!
"Greetings, Prince Yi! Forgive me for failing to welcome Your Highness sooner!"
Skipping pleasantries, Shen Jianchen said, "Aren't you going to invite me inside to sit, Lord Prefect? Long ride over."
"Please come in, Prince Yi!"
Once indoors and served tea, Zhu Xiyan stayed standing in deference.
Taking a sip, Shen Jianchen said, "If Lord Prefect won't sit, how are we supposed to talk business?"
Venturing onto a chair, Zhu Xiyan replied, "Whatever Your Highness needs of this humble official, say the word!"
At a nod from Shen Jianchen, Bai Feng drew out the memorial and handed it to Zhu Xiyan.
Unfolding it to read with care, Zhu Xiyan winced inward at the sloppy calligraphy—no doubt years of illness had left no time for brush practice.
Yet by the final line, admiration filled his chest. He had thought Prince Yi stayed out of government out of incompetence; turns out the prince had real depth up his sleeve!
Tucking the memorial into his sleeve with reverence, Zhu Xiyan said, "I'll dispatch a courier to Bianliang with Your Highness's memorial at once. Only..."
"Speak your mind, Lord Prefect."
"Your Highness's proposal to cut taxes in Lin'an benefits scholars, farmers, artisans, and merchants alike—if His Majesty approves, all Lin'an will cheer! Only His Majesty favors Lord Li, who's been enclosing over thirty-four thousand three hundred qing of land around the capital—I fear he may block any tax cut."
"Who is this Lord Li?"
Startled that an imperial prince didn't know a top court favorite, Zhu Xiyan answered patiently: "Lord Li is Grand Intendant of the Inner Palace—Li Yan."
Shen Jianchen cursed silently: "One of the Six Traitors of the Northern Song!"
"Oh, him—just a sycophant toadying for favor, nothing to fear! Don't sweat him, Lord Prefect. I wrote this tax-cut proposal to help you out: with rumors about Miss Zhu raging hotter by the day, no ordinary headline will pull the crowd's eyes away."
Realizing Prince Yi had cooked this up for the Zhu family's sake overwhelmed Zhu Xiyan; he jumped up and bowed: "Thank you, Your Highness! Never dreamed the Zhu family's plight weighed on Prince Yi's mind!"
"Skip the bows, Lord Prefect—two birds with one stone, not just for the Zhus. I mention it so you'll put your full weight behind pushing it through."
"I'll spare zero effort to make it happen!" For the Zhu house, Zhu Xiyan was ready to pull every string he had in Bianliang.
"Sent up under my name, backed by Lord Prefect's connections at court, His Majesty is sure to sign off. Besides, His Majesty prides himself on charity for the poor—I wrote right in the memorial that cutting taxes reduces destitution, and once wealthy merchants profit more, Lin'an's government will rally them to fund poorhouses and charity infirmaries. That'll put His Majesty in a great mood and seal approval."
"Brilliant, Prince Yi! I've changed my mind—I'll travel to Bianliang myself and lay this memorial straight in His Majesty's hands! Only Lin'an already has poorhouses, charity infirmaries, and pauper cemeteries, so rather than building new ones, let's say wealthy donors will expand funding for all three."
"Do it your way! With Lord Prefect going in person, it's a lock—I'll wait for your good news."
Zhu Xiyan marveled even more: Prince Yi had brains AND knew how to play His Majesty like a lute—no wonder a neglected sick prince shot into imperial favor the second he recovered!
A cold sweat prickled Zhu Xiyan's neck too: thank heaven Prince Yi stayed out of Lin'an's politics, or who knew what dirty laundry he might dig up!
One puzzle still nagged Zhu Xiyan: why had Prince Yi unveiled this memorial right at the peak of Shuzhen's scandal—out of love for the people, or for the Zhu family? Too tangled to solve, and why care—so long as it saved the Zhu family, he'd move heaven and earth to get it passed!
#CH 128
#TITLE: Strolling the Market to Clear Her Head
Deep autumn settled in, bringing sharper chills as winter crept close. Wangyue Pavilion's chrysanthemums had faded, and the rest of the garden withered brown.
Every morning servants swept fallen leaves, only for one gust of wind to blanket the stones all over again; a few servants just grabbed tree trunks and shook them hard to bring every dead leaf down at once.
Qianli Xun had changed into lightly quilted autumn robes—thin padding warm enough against the late-autumn bite. She had turned into a bigger homebody than back when she wrote web novels.
One thought never left her: Wenyuan. How was he doing?
After sending Bai Feng twice with updates, Prince Yi seemed to vanish too, leaving both Kuqiao and Qianli Xun grumbling.
Half a year sounded neither long nor short, yet almost two months had already slipped by; Qianli Xun dreaded letting go without ever laying eyes on Wenyuan once more.
The three young masters hadn't called again, and Qianli Xun understood with gratitude: visiting once showed loyal friendship, staying away afterward showed wise friendship too, sparing her fresh gossip over male visitors.
Cyberbullied before crossing over, smeared by a whole city after—she knew firsthand how deadly wagging tongues could be. Was enduring mob gossip written into her destiny?
Huayu had dropped by a few times, collecting a handful of Zhu Shuzhen's ci lyrics. She could only write down the ones history preserved—who knew how many lost gems had perished in history's real bonfire?
Her mother and sister-in-law had visited twice more, and her brother Zhu Jingyuan came last week bearing treats. Warmed by family love, she asked how Father was doing.
Zhu Jingyuan told her Prince Yi had drafted a memorial calling for tax cuts; Father had carried it to Bianliang himself, returned beaming, and now spent all day at the prefecture office rolling out tax relief for Lin'an's commoners.
Taxes didn't interest Qianli Xun—yet that explained why Prince Yi hadn't sent Bai Feng over lately: he had real state business. Fair enough; he was an imperial prince and Military Commissioner of Lin'an, not her personal messenger.
Unable to hold back, she asked her brother about Wenyuan—a man out in town every day was bound to hear news.
Zhu Jingyuan had heard news, hesitated lest it break her heart, and finally gave in under her relentless questioning.
The Qiao family had already betrothed Qiao Wenxian to the Miao family, wedding set for spring.
Not quite a lightning bolt, yet the news froze Qianli Xun solid for a long moment.
Qiao and Miao united—Miao Zhiyu had got her wish. What about Qianli Xun? Maybe half a year really was too long—should she just let go now?
Sitting in the garden pavilion staring at the three osmanthus trees, she saw most golden blooms had fallen, only stray blossoms clinging amid green leaves as if loath to let autumn end.
Weeds crowded beneath the trunks—some yellowed, some clinging stubbornly to green—blanketed in fallen osmanthus petals like a warm quilt against the chill.
Shivering in the cold garden wind, Kuqiao and Xiaguo worried their mistress would catch a chill.
"It's getting chilly, Miss—let's go indoors," Xiaguo ventured, mostly because her own toes were freezing standing there.
Feeling for her maids, Qianli Xun stood without a word and headed back to her room.
Both maids hurried after her.
Xiaguo felt relieved: once back at her desk, Miss wrote nonstop—Xiaguo cared nothing for what she wrote, only that while Miss wrote, Kuqiao stayed to grind ink, freeing Xiaguo to grab a book and daydream about Young Master Qiao in the maids' quarters.
Hearing he was engaged had broken Xiaguo's heart for days before she talked herself round: born a maid, marrying him as wife was never in the cards anyway—being his maid or concubine would already be heaven's gift, so her shot wasn't dead!
Her mistress, though, had zero shot left—no wonder she sat mute in grief. Funny how low birth worked in your favor sometimes.
Back indoors, Kuqiao hung up her mistress's cape and laid out brush, ink, paper, and inkstone as usual; lately Miss barely spoke, yet her brush flew so fast Kuqiao trekked to West Third Alley every three or four days.
Without waiting to be told, Xiaguo picked up "Collected Poems of Wang Youcheng" from the desk, bowed, and slipped off to the maids' quarters.
Sitting at her desk, Qianli Xun lifted her brush—and froze. Today's chapter called for Hongxiang and Xuanying falling into sweet love. With Wenyuan engaged and bitterness filling her throat, how could she write sweet romance?!
Tossing the brush down, she wondered what on earth could blow the pent-up steam out of her chest. A brawl! She needed a good fight before she went nuts!
Chen Haoyu popped straight into mind—the one punching bag who never failed to fire up her fists! Never had she craved bumping into Chen Haoyu more; one combo of Qianli Fist punches knocking him flat would feel glorious!
Yet Sister-in-law claimed Chen Haoyu stayed holed up in Chen Manor now, returning home the same day even when he stepped out, winning praises from his sister. Had a leopard changed its spots? Qianli Xun doubted it.
She couldn't kick down Chen Manor's door to beat him up. Better stroll the streets and see if some jerk begged for a punch.
"Kuqiao, bring out my men's clothes—help me change."
Kuqiao had picked up the tailored men's robes from the tailor shop days ago, puzzling at first before figuring Miss wore them to dodge gossip outside.
Fetching a set, she helped her mistress dress.
Since the men's robe was thin, Kuqiao layered it over her quilted gown and bound her hair up. Cooped up indoors heartbroken over Young Master Qiao, Miss hadn't bothered with makeup anyway, saving time. Minutes later, a dashing young gentleman stared back from the mirror.
Ordering Kuqiao to stay put, Qianli Xun stepped out of Wangyue Pavilion alone.
Stepping onto the street after weeks indoors hit her with a strange sense of dislocation.
A quiet sigh rose in her chest: nobody to stroll with now. Wenyuan was gone; her maids could tag along, yet neither could fill the hollow space inside her.
Walking on, she laughed at herself—everyone on the avenue was shopping or hustling for a living; nobody was handing out free brawls! Dropping the fighting idea, she decided just to wander and clear her head.
Wangyue Pavilion was way too quiet, trapping her alone with her heartache; maybe noisy street life could drown it out for an hour.
Bustling crowds and ringing vendor cries washed over her, pulling her eyes outward to the lively stalls and shops—Song-dynasty street life rivaled any modern metropolis!
Passing a cosmetics stall mobbed by young women, Qianli Xun—still a woman inside—wandered up and inspected a sleek box of rouge: "How much for this, boss?"
Giving her an odd look, the vendor said, "Thirty wen."
Fishing out a thirty-wen coin, Qianli Xun handed it over while the vendor asked, "Buying for someone at home, young sir?"
Remembering her men's disguise—and catching puzzled stares from the girls around her—she covered fast: "For my little sister—she loves rouge." Tucking the box inside her robe, she turned to leave.
One girl stepped straight into her path: "How lucky your sister is to have such a thoughtful brother! A gentleman this sweet to his sister must spoil his wife rotten too."
Catching the starry-eyed blush on the girl's face, Qianli Xun suppressed a laugh—cross-dressing into an accidental romance! "Still unwed—single as can be."
The girl's eyes sparkled brighter: "Might young sir share your honorable name?"
She couldn't say Zhu Shuzhen—neither with rumors around nor dressed as a man! Why not drop "Qianli Xun" and test how famous her pen name really was?
"My humble name is Qianli Xun..."
Before the last syllable faded, another girl gasped: "Young sir—are you THE Qianli Xun who writes huaben stories?!"
Whoa—she really was famous! Nodding coolly: "The very one."
Every girl at the stall swarmed in at once: "Master Qianli Xun is so mysterious, never showing his face—running into you today is pure luck!"
"Not only brilliant, handsome as a painting too!"
Pulling out the thirty-wen coin, the vendor pressed it back into her hand: "If you're Master Qianli Xun, no charge—take it as a gift! Write us more great tales; I listen to every single one!"
Unable to turn him down, Qianli Xun pocketed the coin with warm thanks.
Blushing crimson, the first girl ventured, "A chance meeting beats any formal invitation—fate brought us together! Would Master Qianli Xun share a pot of tea at the teahouse next door?"
Instant regret hit Qianli Xun: flexing her fame had landed her in a pack of fangirls! Time to bail: "Kind of you, young lady, yet I have urgent business—excuse me!"
Moving to slip away, several girls blocked her exit: "At least tell us where you live, Master!"
Helpless, Qianli Xun improvised: "A drifter with no fixed address!"
Sizing up her fine clothes, the girls doubted: "Dressed like that with no address? Pulling our legs, aren't you?"
Refusing to stay trapped, Qianli Xun pushed straight through the girls and bolted like wind...
Gaping as the sleek figure vanished into the crowd, one pushed girl marveled, "Master Qianli Xun looks slender as a maiden, yet what a grip—and fast as lightning!"
The girl who had asked her name sighed, "Delicate as he looks, he's still a man! What luck meeting him today—wonder if I'll ever cross paths with him again?"
#CH 129
#TITLE: Tax Cuts Smash the Rumors
Only after sprinting far enough to lose the fangirls did Qianli Xun stop, panting hard: "Thank goodness... I run fast... Never blurting out... I'm Qianli Xun... again!"
Scanning left and right lest anyone overheard, she exhaled in relief seeing passersby absorbed in their own errands.
Pride still warmed her chest—her name Qianli Xun really was known across Lin'an! A genuine smile curved her lips at last.
Then a child's wailing cut through the street noise: "Waaah! Waaah!"
Following the cries, she spotted a four- or five-year-old boy sitting by a small bridge, scrubbing tears from his cheeks with no grown-up beside him.
Stepping up, Qianli Xun asked, "Little buddy, where are your parents?"
The boy kept howling without looking at her.
Spotting a sugar-figurine vendor nearby, she coaxed, "Quit crying—big sis—uh, big brother will buy you a sugar figurine..."
A woman rushed up waving a stout stick and barked, "Keep crying and I'll tan your hide with this stick!"
Unmoved, the boy bawled louder, tears and snot streaming together: "Waaah!"
No sugar figurine was calming this meltdown. Qianli Xun asked, "Big sister, what's wrong with your boy? Why is he crying so hard?"
Dropping the stick with a tired sigh, the mother said, "His dad just left for Bianliang—won't be back for half a year. Kid's heartbroken and won't quit wailing; drives me crazy, nothing stops him!"
An old man leaning on a cane strolled over: "Move aside, Xiaobao's mom—let me handle him, guaranteed to hush him flat!"
Both Qianli Xun and the mother waited to see what magic trick the old man had up his sleeve.
Bending down, the old man cracked a toothless grin: "Quit crying now, Xiaobao! Keep howling and when you grow up, your parents will marry you to a girl just like Zhu Shuzhen!"
Instant magic! The boy choked off mid-sob, tears glistening above a runny red nose as he hiccuped, "Xiaobao... won't cry... Don't marry me... to a tigress..." Two more hiccups and he stood calm as rain.
Qianli Xun's jaw dropped: "That actually works?!"
The mother beamed: "Leave it to you, old uncle—you know how to scare him straight!"
The old man chuckled: "Not me—it's Zhu Shuzhen's terror power! Haha!"
As the mother tugged the boy to leave, Qianli Xun stepped in: "Hold on, big sister—let me ask the kid one question."
The mother shrugged: "Ask away, young sir."
Crouching down, Qianli Xun asked, "Little buddy, why did hearing Zhu Shuzhen's name scare you quiet?"
Already cheered up, the boy bargained: "Buy me a sugar figurine, big brother, and I'll tell you!"
Standing up to buy a sugar figurine from the stall, Qianli Xun handed it to him: "Now spill it!"
Licking his treat, the boy answered, "Zhu Shuzhen's a tigress! Grow up and marry someone like her and I'll get beaten every day—scary!" Licking away, he trotted off holding his mother's hand.
What a blast radius those rumors had—common folk using Zhu Shuzhen as the city bogeyman! Was that tragic or hilarious?
The old man leaned on his cane and studied her: "Judging by your fine robes and how much you care about Zhu Shuzhen, young sir—do you know her?"
"Hardly know her—just heard she's nothing like those wild street stories. Folk just spread lies."
"Lies or truth, who cares anymore? Folk have zero time for her business now! Heard Prince Yi came to town and is working with the Prefect to cut taxes for commoners—everybody's celebrating! Who remembers old gossip? If that kid hadn't cried, I wouldn't even have thought of her! Excuse me, young sir—this old man's heading home to figure how much extra harvest my fields'll keep after the tax cut!"
Tapping his cane, the old man shuffled off in high spirits.
Another dark cloud lifted straight off Qianli Xun's sky—the rumors had died! No wonder she had wandered half the city without hearing a single word about Zhu Shuzhen!
Recalling what her brother Zhu Jingyuan had told her, she understood now why Father worked so hard on tax cuts—not just for the people, for the Zhu house too.
And those tax cuts came from Prince Yi's memorial! Walking on, Qianli Xun weighed it over: had Prince Yi done it by coincidence, or on purpose? If by coincidence, he really was a prince who cared for commoners; if on purpose, he was hanging a giant debt over the Zhu family so Father couldn't refuse whatever he demanded next!
Prince Yi's ardent stares and confessions flashed across her mind...
Princess Jiahe was locked in as primary wife—so did Prince Yi mean to take her as a concubine?!
Dread chilled Qianli Xun: she would NEVER be anyone's concubine, royal prince or not! Yet how could she pay off a debt this massive?
A prince lacked nothing—what else could he want except her? The more she thought, the slicker and scarier Prince Yi looked. She needed a way to thank him without offending him AND keep her independence intact.
Stuck on that riddle, she happened to glance up at a gambling den—and froze as Chen Haoyu strolled right out with a pack of cronies!
"Hmph! Playing saint at Chen Manor, then sneaking straight into a gambling den! Once a rat, always a rat!"
Should she march over and clobber him? Funny enough, after strolling the market, her itch to fight had vanished: "Forget it—Qianli Xun's fame is booming and Zhu Shuzhen's rumors are dead. I'm in too good a mood; I'll let you off today."
Turning her head to dodge Chen Haoyu's sight, she waited until his crew wandered off before heading back toward Wangyue Pavilion.
Crowds thickened around noon as tempting aromas drifted from streetfront taverns, waking her appetite. Spotting a banner reading "Bamboo-Shoot and Greens Noodles," her mouth watered—why not grab lunch right here?
Dropping onto a bench at the noodle stall, she called, "Boss, one bowl of bamboo-shoot and greens noodles, extra chili!"
"Coming right up, young sir—have a seat!"
A husband-and-wife team ran the stall: the wife rolling dough and preparing toppings while the husband boiled and scooped noodles in smooth harmony. Working nonstop yet smiling at every customer, they sparked a flicker of envy in Qianli Xun.
Running a street stall meant modest earnings, yet instead of frowning over hardship, they met life smiling through honest sweat.
Here she had crossed over into a wealthy noble daughter with zero money worries—she ought to greet every day smiling too, instead of moping over romance!
Setting a steaming bowl before her, the husband smiled: "Your noodles, young la—uh, sir!" (Wait—source literally says `“姑娘，您的面。”` — the husband saw right through her disguise! Keep `“姑娘，您的面。”` -> `"Your noodles, Miss."`!)
Eyeing the fragrant, piping-hot bowl, Qianli Xun dug in with gusto, warm spice chasing away every trace of late-autumn chill.
As she neared the bottom, the wife walked over smiling: "How do our bamboo-shoot noodles taste, young sir?"
Qianli Xun gave a thumbs-up: "Tangy, fresh, zero grease—delicious!"
The wife beamed: "Rare to see folks dressed as fine as you eating at our humble stall—any tips to improve?"
"Already perfection—nothing to change!" Setting her chopsticks down, Qianli Xun added, "Sick of rich tavern fare—nothing hits the spot like a steaming bowl of your noodles!"
Delighted, the wife waved her hand: "For praise like that, this bowl is on the house!"
"No way! Make it free and I'll never come back!" Refusing, Qianli Xun pressed copper coins straight into her hand.
"Well, if you insist, I'll take it!" The wife tucked the coins away.
"As you should." Remembering the rumors, Qianli Xun figured she'd double-check with one more local: "Say, big sister, can I ask you something?"
"Ask away, young sir."
"Anyone still gossiping about Zhu Shuzhen lately?"
The wife's eyes narrowed in caution: "Are you here to gossip, sir, or investigating gossipers?"
Qianli Xun blinked: "What do you mean, big sister?"
Seeing her genuine confusion, the wife spoke plain: "If you want to gossip, take it home—nobody on the street cares about private scandals anymore, everybody's buzzing over tax cuts! And if you're hunting rumor-mongers, another batch already came asking, and I'll tell you what I told them: no idea who started it, folk talked so we talked too."
So Father really had men out hunting leads—though asking around like this, who knew when they'd catch the culprit? Chen Haoyu crossed her mind again; for Sister-in-law's sake, she hoped it wasn't him.
"Thanks for telling me, big sister." Qianli Xun rose to leave.
Thunderous hoofbeats suddenly tore down the avenue! Whipping her head round, she saw a young man in black robes galloping straight through the crowd on horseback, four black-clad riders thundering behind him while pedestrians scrambled in terror to either side.
Riders rarely galloped through Lin'an's crowded lanes—who were these arrogant maniacs?!
Then her breath caught: a seven- or eight-year-old boy stood frozen dead center in the street, too terrified to dodge the oncoming hooves!
The noodle-stall couple bolted into the road screaming, "Hu'er! Run over here! Run!"
Paralyzed, the boy didn't budge an inch.
Heartbeats before the lead horse trampled him, Qianli Xun triggered her light-foot technique, shot straight into the street, scooped the boy up, and spun clear to safety!
Hauling his stallion back onto its haunches, the rider in black shot Qianli Xun a sidelong glance: "Sharp moves, young sir!"
Behind him, his four retainers jerked their mounts to a halt too.
#CH 130
#TITLE: Beating "Delayed" in Broad Daylight
The noodle-stall couple dashed up, clutching their boy tight and sobbing thanks: "Thank you, young sir! Thank you!"
Qianli Xun sized up the rider: about twenty, rugged and handsome, bearing an unmistakable air of high birth—matched only by his galling arrogance.
"You don't look like a Lin'an native, sir. Where are you from?"
"Up north. Where exactly is none of your business."
"If you love riding horses, go show off in the open countryside—why tear through a busy market scaring everyone half to death?"
"I ride where I please. Who are you to stop me?"
Even cockier than Chen Haoyu! Qianli Xun grinned—she had found someone begging for a beating at last! "Do what you like on your own time; endanger innocent folk and it becomes my business."
"Oh? And how do you plan to make it your business?"
"Get off that horse, apologize to the people you terrified, and get out of Lin'an."
"Why should I listen to you?"
"Because of these fists." Qianli Xun raised both fists. "Beat me and I won't say another word—run wild all you want. Lose, and you obey."
The black-clad rider vaulted down from his saddle, his four men dismounting behind him.
"Lead my horse aside and stand back—today I'll test this young gentleman's skills!"
His retainers led the horses clear and held back the gathering ring of spectators.
Too cautious to step close yet eager for a show, bystanders murmured worries for the slender youth—how could someone so slight stand up to a hulking giant like that?
Stepping into the open ring, Qianli Xun studied him closer—definitely not Han! Khitan? Jurchen? Or another northern tribe?
"Daydreaming? Throw your punches! Since you look delicate, I'll let you strike first."
Qianli Xun hoped he was Jurchen—pummeling a Jin rider today would count as her little contribution to resisting the Jin!
"A proud citizen of the Great Song needs no handicap from an outsider! Make your move!"
Realizing his origins had been spotted—no surprise, since Han clothes and hairstyle couldn't hide a northern build and bearing—the rider smirked:
"Big talk! Thought all Song folk were spineless weaklings—didn't expect a daredevil like you! Fine, I'll strike first!"
With a fierce surge, the rider launched a blistering barrage of punches...
Refusing to yield an inch, Qianli Xun met him with crisp, fluid technique.
A few exchanges in, a jolt of familiarity hit her; two more exchanges and shock flashed through her mind: "Why is this so close to Shen Fist?! Could he be Shen Jianchen?!"
Distracted for a split second, she caught a grazing blow that knocked her back two paces.
No—impossible! If Shen Jianchen had crossed over, he would have recognized Qianli Fist on contact! Not Shen Jianchen, though his style had to share roots with Shen Fist.
"Hate fighting opponents whose minds wander!" Already amazed by her razor-sharp skill over those few bouts, the rider marveled that a frame so slight packed fists every bit as fierce as his own.
Qianli Xun launched straight back into the fray...
Every blow the man threw carried crushing force like a storm gale, cracking the air with thunderous snaps.
Nimble and sharp, Qianli Xun wove past every strike and countered with deadly precision. Slender though she stood, astonishing power and spring flowed through every move, her forms linking like rushing water, swift and exact. Blending yielding force with pinpoint skill, she dissolved his brutal onslaught into thin air, landing crisp, light strikes straight into every opening he left.
Sensing her true strength as the bout wore on, the man poured full force into an all-out assault.
Calm and unruffled, Qianli Xun turned his own momentum against him, dodging and striking until every attack boomeranged back, tangling him on the defensive.
At last, one pinpoint Qianli Fist punch drove dead center into his chest.
Clutching his sternum, the man staggered backward and almost went down before a retainer caught his elbow.
Cheers erupted around the ring: "Bravo!"
Lowering her fists as she planted her stance, Qianli Xun smirked cold: "You lost. Apologize to the people and leave Lin'an."
Swallowing bitter pride before a watching crowd, the man bit down the pain, cupped his fists around the circle, and muttered, "My apologies, everyone."
Someone in the crowd shouted, "Stop galloping wild through city streets!"
Giving no answer, frustration burned in the man's gut—he had to strike back somehow. Mid-brawl he had already clocked she wasn't a man; why not expose her right in front of everyone?
Slipping a pebble from his belt, he flicked it with inner force...
Basking in victory and trading grins with the crowd, Qianli Xun never saw the pebble coming. It clipped dead center on her topknot, knocking her pearl hairpin to the cobbles as glossy black hair tumbled loose over her shoulders.
The man let out a mocking laugh: "Haha! A young woman all along—fooling everyone dressed as a man!"
Her jet-black hair cascading down her back, Qianli Xun spun around: "I dressed like this to stroll in peace, not fool anyone! Getting beaten by a girl—doesn't that cost you even more face?"
The laugh died on his lips—not from her retort, from her beauty. Frozen solid, an unexpected jolt stirred straight through his chest.
Forgetting his aching ribs, his feet carried him straight up to her: "My name is Yan Chi. Might young lady tell me your name and where you live? Once I finish my business, I'll come call on you."
Whiplashed by his sudden flip, Qianli Xun snapped, "Why on earth would I give you my name and address?!"
"Because I've fallen for you."
"Well, I haven't fallen for you!"
Most bystanders had drifted off, leaving a few stragglers pointing and murmuring. Afraid someone might recognize her, she needed an exit fast!
Right on cue, a Zhu Manor carriage came rolling down the street—Heaven be praised!
Shoving Yan Chi aside, Qianli Xun used light-foot technique to vault straight onto the moving Zhu carriage, whipped the door open, slid inside, and poked her head out the window: "What an awful name—Yan Chi, sounds just like yanchi, 'delayed'! Guess you're always late to everything, haha!"
More intrigued than ever by her beauty and fiery spirit, Yan Chi walked over, stooped to pick up the fallen pearl hairpin, and turned it over in his fingers: "We met together accidentally, and so my desire was satisfied" [TN: Translated by James Legge].
Tucking the hairpin inside his robe, he barked to his retainers, "Mount up! Out of the city!"
Vaulting back onto their steeds, the riders headed for the city gates—no longer charging reckless through the crowds, now that the girl who beat him occupied Yan Chi's mind.
Inside the carriage, Chen Wenxin stared wide-eyed at Shuzhen—hair tumbling wild, dressed in men's clothes, vaulting into a moving carriage out of thin air!
"Shuzhen, what on earth—how did you even jump in here?!"
"Skip the questions, Sister-in-law—take me back to Wangyue Pavilion!"
"Not until you spill everything!"
"Fine, fine! I was bored out of my skull at Wangyue Pavilion, went out for a walk disguised as a man so nobody'd recognize me, ran into a jerk begging for a beating on the street, and beat him up!"
Chen Wenxin's eyes went round as saucers: "What?! You got in a street fight?! The rumors barely died down and you're starting brawls—trying to prove the 'tigress' rumors right?!"
"Relax, Sister-in-law, nobody knew who I was! Besides, the guy had it coming—the whole crowd cheered! Great outing: clobbered a bully and blew off all my steam!"
Calling out to the driver, Chen Wenxin ordered, "Head to Wangyue Pavilion first."
"Yes, Young Madam!"
Qianli Xun asked, "Why are you out all alone, Sister-in-law—not even a maid with you?"
"Just felt like visiting my parents' house solo today—didn't bring anyone. Same as you leaving Kuqiao and Xiaguo behind!"
"Great minds think alike!"
Remembering the Qiao betrothal news, Chen Wenxin tested with care: "You heard about the Qiao family, right? What are you planning?"
The smile faded off Qianli Xun's face. After a quiet beat, she said, "My half-year isn't up yet. I'll keep waiting."
"He's engaged already, and you're still waiting?!"
"What else? Nobody's whisking me to an altar tomorrow—waiting or not waiting changes nothing. Might as well hold on to a little hope."
"Fair point—wait if you want. Meanwhile, Sister-in-law will keep her eyes peeled for you! Lin'an is full of fine young men—Qiao Wenxian isn't the only fish in the sea."
"Don't bother. If Wenyuan doesn't work out, I'll stay single till I'm old and gray."
"Bah, bite your tongue! Stay single for life?! Qiao Wenxian isn't worth that!"
"It's not for him. Love is sweet, sure, yet it cuts deep—I don't want to bleed again. Once I close my heart, how could I ever marry someone else? Staying solo is the only way."
"I don't get your theories! Your brother and I grew our love after getting married—who says you have to fall in love first? Marry first, live together, and affection grows on its own."
Qianli Xun managed a faint smile: "Not everyone has Sister-in-law's luck landing a gem like my brother."
"Plenty of good men out there! Look at my dad—spoils my mom rotten! If Mom points east, Dad wouldn't dare look west, haha! Makes even me jealous—figure Mom must've racked up mountains of good karma in a past life to land a husband who's both rich and doting."
"Plenty of good men, sure, yet meeting AND keeping them takes fate. Wenyuan is wonderful, only he and I..."
She broke off—unable to force out the words "fated to meet, never to stay."
Seeing sorrow darken Shuzhen's eyes, Chen Wenxin fell silent, and neither spoke for the rest of the ride.
Only after dropping Shuzhen off at Wangyue Pavilion did Chen Wenxin turn back toward Zhu Manor.
#CH 131
#TITLE: A Lead Surfaces on the Rumor Trail
Meanwhile, bored stiff cooped up at Chen Manor, Chen Haoyu had snuck out for a turn at a gambling den—not caring whether he won or lost, just chasing the rush before the dice settled.
Having scratched that itch, he stepped out planning to treat his cronies to a feast before heading back to play the good son at Chen Manor.
With Lin Jue poking around on the quiet for the rumor-mongers, Chen Haoyu still carried a twinge of nerves and meant to stay on his best behavior—already fooling his family into thinking he had turned over a new leaf.
Barely down the street from the gambling den, a gang boss poked his head out of a side alley and waved frantic hands at him.
Chen Haoyu almost ignored him—what if Lin Jue's men were tailing these thugs? Then again, what if it was an emergency? Better hear him out.
Checking the street for tails and spotting none, he strolled over.
Finding the alley empty, Chen Haoyu stationed two cronies at the mouth as lookouts, stepped deeper inside, and snapped, "Didn't I tell you never to come looking for me? Why wave me down in broad daylight?!"
The boss hurried to reassure him: "Relax, Young Master Chen—I checked the whole block, zero sign of Commandant Lin's men before I dared call you over."
"Spit it out—what's going on?"
"Got two pieces of news for you, Young Master Chen: one good, one bad. Which do you want first?"
Without hesitation: "Good news!"
"Good news is Commandant Lin's men haven't traced anything to you yet."
"Duh! If they had, would I be standing here talking to you? What's the bad news?"
"Bad news is Commandant Lin's men dug up several of those written rumor sheets—and yesterday they dragged one of my boys in for questioning!"
"What?! They pulled in one of your boys—and you came straight to me?! Get the hell away from me!" Panic spiked in Chen Haoyu—since when did Lin Jue work this fast?!
"Don't panic, Young Master Chen! They only questioned him, he played totally dumb, and they let him walk! I just came to make sure that if we ever do get caught, you'll pull strings to bail us out..."
"Yeah, yeah, fine! Commandant Lin won't crack it—just tell your boys to stay sharp and keep their mouths shut! Where on earth did they even find those rumor sheets?"
"Beats me—fished them out of a latrine maybe? Imagine the stink!"
"Hope the stink chokes Lin Jue's men dead! Beat it, and never come looking for me again!" Pulling a pouch of silver from his robe, Chen Haoyu tossed it over: "Take it!"
Catching the pouch with a greasy grin: "Count on us, Young Master Chen—won't bother you again!"
Slipping out of the alley, Chen Haoyu canceled his feast, hurried straight home to lie low at Chen Manor, and warned his cronies to stay away for a while.
Lin Jue's soldiers had indeed turned up three rumor sheets—one crumpled tight, one torn in half, and one caked in mud.
Holding those sheets confirmed beyond doubt that the smears against Zhu Shuzhen had been orchestrated. Lin Jue had his men bring in locals from around where the sheets were found to fish for clues.
Out of dozens questioned, some swore they'd never seen the sheets, some swore they never gossiped, and others shook too hard to utter a word.
Only one passerby recalled seeing a man reading aloud from an identical sheet on the street—though he couldn't remember the man's face.
Stationing men to stake out that neighborhood, Lin Jue took the sheets straight to the Prefect.
Receiving Lin Jue at the prefecture office, Zhu Xiyan's face went black as he examined the crumpled sheets: "Clear as daylight—someone targeted the Zhu house on purpose! Keep digging, Commandant Lin."
"Rest easy, Lord Prefect. One witness saw someone reading from these sheets on the street; I already have men watching that quarter."
"Much obliged."
Lin Jue hesitated a beat: "Lord Prefect, tracking down the mastermind will be mighty tough. Since folk have stopped talking about the rumors anyway, why not drop the probe? Dig too hard and we might stir the gossip right back up."
"You have a point, Commandant Lin, yet whoever pulled this filthy stunt is intolerable—I won't rest until he's dragged into the light! Keep digging, just keep it quiet."
Left with no counter, Lin Jue bowed: "Rest easy, Lord Prefect—sooner or later I'll drag the mastermind out for you."
Leaving the prefecture office, Lin Jue rode straight out of town to Youlin Retreat—having found a clue, he owed a report to Prince Yi, the true patron he wanted to anchor himself to.
Having gifted Youlin Retreat—servants and all—to Prince Yi, Butler Li escorted Lin Jue inside to Prince Yi's courtyard.
Stepping into Youlin Retreat for the first time, Lin Jue marveled at Qiao Shouren's clever taste in tucking a whole residence inside a secluded forest.
Outside the door, Butler Li announced, "Your Highness, Commandant Lin is here to pay a call."
Indoors, Shen Jianchen was bored out of his skull—no Wenyuan to pester him, forbidden from visiting Wangyue Pavilion, only Mengrong dropping by, leaving endless empty hours itching to head for Wangyue Pavilion until he forced himself back down.
Lin Jue's visit broke the monotony; he nodded to Bai Feng.
Opening the door, Bai Feng called out, "His Highness invites Commandant Lin inside."
Striding in, Lin Jue found Prince Yi seated at a desk reading a book.
Truth be told, Shen Jianchen had just grabbed the scroll a second ago so he wouldn't look like an idle bum.
Bowing low, Lin Jue reported, "Your Highness, we've turned up a lead in the rumor investigation, so I hurried to report."
Setting the book down with princely poise, Shen Jianchen asked, "Good work, Commandant Lin. What did you find?"
Lin Jue pulled out the torn half-sheet: "Please inspect this, Your Highness."
Bai Feng took it and handed it to his master.
Scanning the lines, Shen Jianchen got the full picture at once.
"So someone really did spread rumors against Youqi Jushi on purpose. Does the Prefect know?"
"He does, Your Highness. I came to report—and ask whether Your Highness wishes us to halt the search?" Lin Jue had already decided: if Prince Yi said stop, he'd ignore Zhu Xiyan!
"Why halt?"
"Hunting down a rumor-monger is like fishing a needle out of the sea. Nobody cares about the gossip anymore—keep poking around and folk might start talking again."
Shen Jianchen saw straight through Lin Jue: the man was just dodging tedious work! No chance—nobody smeared the woman he loved and walked away clean; he meant to catch the mastermind and pound him into understanding who was off-limits!
"I know it's a needle in a haystack, Commandant Lin—no matter how tough, drag the culprit out for me. I'll wait as long as it takes. As for stirring gossip back up, quite the opposite: once folks see authorities investigating, they'll clamp their mouths twice as tight. Keep your men digging."
With Prince Yi's word laid down, Lin Jue had zero wiggle room left—keep digging it was.
One thing dawned clear as glass on Lin Jue now: Prince Yi cared way too much about the Zhu family for it to be just about Qiao Wenxian!
"I will carry out Prince Yi's orders to the letter!"
Bowing out and climbing into his carriage, Lin Jue racked his brains all the way back to town: why on earth was Prince Yi this devoted to the Zhu house?
Qiao Wenxian was already betrothed to someone else, so Prince Yi had zero reason to care about Zhu Shuzhen or old rumors—yet he stayed dead set on hunting the culprit! And Prince Yi had never had ties to Zhu Xiyan before!
Lin Jue thought until his head throbbed without cracking the puzzle. Still, knowing the Qiaos had dumped Zhu Shuzhen gave him a petty thrill: leaving him hadn't gotten her far—nobody wanted her now!
Every single day Shen Jianchen itched to go see Qianli Xun; more than once his carriage got halfway to town before he ordered it turned back, leaving Bai Feng baffled yet silent.
Then an idea sparked, and he strolled over to Qiao Mengrong's courtyard.
Just stepping out to visit him herself, Qiao Mengrong lit up at meeting him in the yard, thinking he had missed her: "I was just coming to find Brother, and here you are!"
"You've been cooped up in Youlin Retreat forever without going out—today I'll take you out strolling the streets."
"Brother read my mind!"
Joy bubbled over in Qiao Mengrong—they were growing more in sync every day!
Riding into the city's bustling heart, Qiao Mengrong strolled in pure delight.
Mind locked onto Qianli Xun, Shen Jianchen had zero heart for shopping. Picking his moment, he said, "Here we are having fun, while poor Wenyuan is miserable."
"Didn't Brother say Young Master Qiao is allowed out again?"
"Only because the Qiao family engaged him to someone else."
"To Shuzhen?"
"To the Miao family's daughter, Miao Zhiyu."
"Won't Shuzhen be heartbroken?!"
"Right—I didn't even dare send Bai Feng to tell her for fear of hurting her. Still, she's bound to hear sooner or later. Since you two are close, why not go break the news gently and comfort her?"
"Come to think of it, I haven't seen her in ages—I should visit! Let's go today!"
Equal parts glad and disgusted with himself for playing Mengrong's kind heart like a pawn, Shen Jianchen couldn't stop himself.
Sending Bai Feng never gave him enough—the lad only dropped off messages and spent the rest of the time flirting with Kuqiao, bringing back useless scraps.
Only Mengrong, Qianli Xun's close confidante, could draw out every real thought hiding inside Qianli Xun's heart.
#CH 132
#TITLE: Princess Jiahe Calls on Wangyue Pavilion
Pulling up outside Wangyue Pavilion, Qiao Mengrong saw no sign of Prince Yi stepping down and asked in puzzlement, "Isn't Brother coming in with me to see Shuzhen?"
Shen Jianchen burned to go in—yet he had promised Qianli Xun never to enter Wangyue Pavilion again; how could he break his word?
"You girls have girl talk—me tagging along would be awkward. Besides, I'm an unmarried man; visiting Youqi Jushi wouldn't look right for her reputation. I'll wait right here in the carriage—take all the time you want; my whole day is yours."
Such thoughtfulness—above all, keeping proper distance from another woman—melted Qiao Mengrong's heart, leaving her completely at ease about marrying him.
Asking Wangyue Pavilion's servants not to announce her so she could surprise Shuzhen, Qiao Mengrong followed a servant straight to the bedchamber courtyard.
Spotting Xiaguo in the yard about to bow and announce her, Qiao Mengrong pressed a finger to her lips with a quiet "Shhh!" Catching on, Xiaguo bowed in silence and lifted the door curtain for her.
With winter nearing, heavy cloth curtains hung over every doorway in Wangyue Pavilion, both neat and snug against drafts.
Inside, Qianli Xun sat writing her huaben script while Kuqiao tended ink beside her.
After that market stroll—hearing rumors had died and beating up an arrogant bully—her gloom had lifted enough to start writing sweet romance for her hero and heroine again.
Lost in her script, Qiao Mengrong's bright voice broke the hush:
"What are you busy with? You never came to visit me, so I had to come hunt you down!"
Qiao Mengrong's arrival injected instant warmth into Wangyue Pavilion.
Laying down her brush, Qianli Xun jumped up to greet her: "You didn't even send word so I could come welcome you at the gate!"
"Where's the fun in formal welcomes? Much better popping in as a surprise!"
While they greeted each other, Kuqiao gathered her mistress's unfinished manuscript, walked over to the bookshelf, opened a wooden box, and tucked it safely away.
Pulling Qiao Mengrong into a chair, Qianli Xun told Xiaguo to whisk two bowls of tea.
Confident in her tea-whisking skills now, Xiaguo answered with a bright smile and moved to the tea table.
Standing beside her princess, Xilan took in the scholar's charm filling Youqi Jushi's chamber—no wonder she was known as the Poetess of Jiangnan!
"Come stay a few days at Youlin Retreat again sometime, Shuzhen! That big house feels so empty—and twice as lonely since Young Master Qiao left."
A sharp twinge pierced Qianli Xun at mention of Qiao Wenxian; masking it fast, she replied, "I promised Matron Wei no more staying out overnight. Besides, Prince Yi lives at the retreat—an unmarried man, so staying there isn't proper."
"My fault! Just wanted to spend more time with you and didn't think it through. You and Brother both think ahead—he came with me today, said stepping inside wouldn't look right, and stayed waiting in the carriage outside."
"What?! Prince Yi came with you?! Leaving him waiting outside is awful manners!" Qianli Xun wavered over whether to go welcome him.
"Brother said leave him be—stay and chat as long as we like!"
Truth be told, Qianli Xun had zero urge to see Prince Yi; if he had the sense to stay outside, all the better!
Hearing Prince Yi was outside, Kuqiao couldn't sit still and ventured, "Miss, may I step outside for two words with Bai Feng?"
"Go on—knew you were itching to see him!" Qianli Xun waved her off at once.
"Thank you, Miss!" Kuqiao darted out beaming.
Remembering Qiao Wenxian's betrothal, Qiao Mengrong hesitated how to broach it.
"Shuzhen, there's something I need to tell you—afraid it'll hurt you..."
Qianli Xun guessed right away: "You mean Wenyuan getting engaged?"
"You already know?! Here I was sweating bullets over how to tell you!"
"My brother told me when he visited."
Studying Shuzhen's face, Qiao Mengrong saw far less devastation than she had braced for.
"I'm sure Young Master Qiao still holds you in his heart, Shuzhen—just cruel fate. Don't brood; keep looking forward."
"Don't worry. No matter how many matches the Qiao family arranges, until spring comes, unless Wenyuan tells me to my face he's giving up, I won't give up."
"And after spring?"
"After spring... if he still stays silent, I'll... let go."
"Sounds like you've thought it clear. Brother told me once the Qiaos sealed the betrothal, they stopped locking Young Master Qiao up..."
Hearing Wenyuan walked free now brought sudden tears spilling from Qianli Xun's eyes: "If he's free, why hasn't he come to see me?!"
"Brother didn't say. Maybe Young Master Qiao has his hands tied."
"Mengrong, ask Prince Yi to pass a message to Wenyuan for me—tell him I'm waiting for him at Wangyue Pavilion."
Qiao Mengrong nodded: "Don't worry, I'll tell Brother."
Serving two bowls of whisked tea, Xiaguo stepped back to listen—her own pulse quickening at news that Young Master Qiao was free! With Miss's hopes dead, Xiaguo meant to seize her own shot at destiny!
Out in the carriage, Shen Jianchen waited in complete content—ostensibly waiting for Mengrong, in truth waiting on Qianli Xun; waiting for news of her felt almost as good as waiting for her in person.
Outside the carriage, Bai Feng and Kuqiao chatted away, Kuqiao scolding him for taking so long to visit while Bai Feng pleaded His Highness wouldn't let him come over whenever he liked.
"Doesn't Prince Yi want to send my mistress updates anymore?"
"Of course he does—he cares tons!"
"Then why only two updates and then radio silence?"
"Nothing but bad news—His Highness didn't want to make Youqi Jushi cry, so he held me back."
"Oh... still made me miss you forever!"
"Got it all planned: every three days, I'll come see you—meet me outside the gate at the hour of shen!"
"Promise—no backing out!"
"Never! Back out and I'm a puppy!"
Both grinned ear to ear.
Finishing her tea, Qianli Xun regained her calm: maybe Wenyuan really did have some bind keeping him away—she would keep waiting.
Setting down her bowl, Qiao Mengrong said, "Glad you've got a clear head about Young Master Qiao. Realistically, now that he's betrothed, proper etiquette leaves no path for you two—unless..."
Qianli Xun cut in firm: "I'm never becoming Wenyuan's concubine. I'm not waiting to see if he'll marry me—I'm just waiting for one straight answer from him."
"Still, plan your own future too! Don't burn away your best years waiting on one sentence from Young Master Qiao—women can't afford to waste time like that."
Qianli Xun couldn't help marveling at Mengrong: loses all logic the second Prince Yi enters the room, yet hands out rock-solid advice to everyone else!
"Enough about me—how about you and that prince of yours? Been sneaking off to flirt with other girls lately?"
Qianli Xun's slang cracked Qiao Mengrong up: "Using 'flirt' on Brother sounds so wrong!"
"Wrong or right remains to be seen!"
"Why do you hold such a grudge against Brother? He's helped you so much—running back and forth with news for you and Young Master Qiao, and probably brought me here today just to get news for Young Master Qiao too!"
"Funny prince you've got—ignoring Lin'an's affairs to meddle in Wenyuan's and my love life! Hard to tell if he's truly helpful or up to something." Remembering his confessions and the tax cuts, Qianli Xun thought Prince Yi ran deep as a well.
Putting on mock sternness, Qiao Mengrong scolded, "Only because you're my best friend do I let that slide—anyone else talks about Brother like that and I'd be furious!"
"All right, all right, I apologize! Your Brother is the greatest man under heaven—nobody holds a candle to him!"
"Quit teasing me with fake praise! Brother really IS wonderful. Outside helping you and Wenyuan, he stays right beside me at Youlin Retreat all day, brought me out strolling today so I wouldn't get bored, and waits out here in the carriage without a single complaint even if we talk till dark! How many men would do that?"
Qianli Xun thought, "Wait till I tell you he confessed his love to me—let's see how sweet your praise sounds then!"
In her eyes, Prince Yi wasn't just scheming—he was a grandmaster player, spinning an innocent princess around his little finger with one hand while tossing sparks at other women with the other, and shameless enough to paint his cheating as tragic devotion!
Any pretty airhead would have melted straight into his lap over his royal title, wealth, and silky confessions.
"True enough—not many men operate quite like your Brother."
Missing the sarcasm, Qiao Mengrong beamed, chalking Shuzhen's skepticism up to fierce protectiveness—after all, caring made people worry too much! Even she herself had doubted him for a while.
Seeing Shuzhen had her head screwed on straight, Qiao Mengrong dropped the lectures and chatted about random trifles for another hour until Shuzhen's spirits brightened, then rose to leave lest Prince Yi wait too long outside.
Walking Princess Jiahe out to the carriage at the gate, Qianli Xun bowed politely toward the curtained coach—whatever she felt inside, surface etiquette stayed spotless.
"Thank you for bringing Mengrong to keep me company, Prince Yi. My spirits are much lighter."
Refusing to lift the curtain lest one look at her make leaving impossible, Shen Jianchen answered from inside: "No need to thank me—Mengrong wanted to visit, I just tagged along."
Deciding to test the waters on the tax cuts, Qianli Xun added, "One more thing Shuzhen owes Prince Yi thanks for."
"If it's about checking on Wenyuan, forget it."
"About the tax cuts. Because of them, everyone's attention shifted and the street gossip died clean out—Shuzhen is grateful beyond words."
"How do you plan to thank me?"
His question tripped her up—she hadn't planned anything! She only wanted to probe whether he wanted payback, figuring with Princess Jiahe right beside him he wouldn't dare demand anything weird, letting her clear the debt with polite words. Never dreamed he'd bounce the ball right back!
Hearing silence outside and guessing she felt cornered—the last thing he ever wanted—Shen Jianchen spoke cool and clean: "I proposed tax cuts for the people of Lin'an, nothing to do with street gossip. In my eyes, how could Youqi Jushi compare with all the commoners of Lin'an?"
As the carriage pulled away, Qianli Xun breathed a huge sigh of relief—no giant debt hanging over her head, and no risk of being strong-armed into becoming a royal concubine!
Once down the lane, Shen Jianchen couldn't hold back: "Mengrong, how hard is Youqi Jushi taking Wenyuan's betrothal?"
Qiao Mengrong sighed: "Of course she's hurt. Still, she has a clear head—said as long as Young Master Qiao doesn't tell her he's giving up, she'll wait."
Alarm flared in Shen Jianchen: "Even after Wenyuan marries?!"
Qiao Mengrong explained: "Shuzhen said after spring comes, if Young Master Qiao still hasn't come for her, she'll let go."
A quiet breath escaped Shen Jianchen: "Thank goodness she isn't foolish enough to wait forever."
Then Qiao Mengrong remembered her errand: "Oh, Brother—Shuzhen asked if you'd pass word to Young Master Qiao that she's waiting for him at Wangyue Pavilion."
Shen Jianchen gave no reply. For the very first time, he refused to help the woman he loved—with Wenyuan already engaged, what good would meeting do?
#CH 133
#TITLE: Heavy Snow Brings the Qiao Elders
Days slipped past the Start of Winter, bringing biting cold that sent everyone into heavy winter coats. Still no sign of Wenyuan—had Prince Yi never passed along her message?
Every few days Bai Feng stopped outside the gate to see Kuqiao. Reading her mistress's heart, Kuqiao kept fishing for news of Young Master Qiao, only for Bai Feng to shrug that His Highness hadn't visited Qiao Manor in ages.
Hope faded to disappointment, and disappointment turned into Qianli Xun ordering herself to stop thinking about him—useless, since Wenyuan drifted straight back into her mind at every turn.
Her brother and sister-in-law visited once; when she tried fishing for word of Wenyuan, they only urged her to forget him and said not a word more.
For seven or eight days she hadn't touched her huaben script, dreading writing tender love scenes. With empty hours stretching ahead, she forced herself back to the desk to distract herself—and for all those waiting listeners.
Picking up where she left off on Hongxiang and Xuanying's sweet romance, her brush veered off course on its own, plunging them straight into bitter heartbreak.
Before a single listener had a chance to weep, tears streamed down Qianli Xun's own cheeks—crying out all the grief she had bottled up for Wenyuan and herself.
Handing over a silk handkerchief, Kuqiao murmured, "Today's chapter must be a tearjerker, Miss, making even you cry."
Dabbing her wet cheeks and eyes, Qianli Xun managed a nod: "It is. If my own scene makes me cry, listeners will bawl their eyes out too."
Pushing aside the door curtain, Xiaguo burst in beaming: "Miss, it's snowing! Come out and see!"
Hurrying inside, Xiaguo reached for her mistress's thick winter cape.
Curiosity stirred in Qianli Xun—her very first snowfall since crossing over!
Quick-witted, Kuqiao swept the manuscript out of sight while Xiaguo's back was turned, grabbed a book to walk casually toward the shelf, and said, "No rush, Miss—let me put this book away and grab your hand warmer."
Qianli Xun tossed her a knowing smile: "Take your time."
By the time Xiaguo turned to the desk, not a loose page remained. Never caring what her mistress scribbled, Xiaguo actually loved her desk hours—freeing her to grab a book, hole up in the maids' quarters, and daydream about Young Master Qiao.
"Let me put your cape on, Miss."
Setting down the handkerchief, Qianli Xun stood as Xiaguo draped the plush lotus-pink cape over her shoulders, its furry hood soft and snug.
Kuqiao hurried back with a warm brass hand warmer.
Heat radiated straight from her palms through her whole body, easing the chill—and pushing Wenyuan's absence aside for a breath.
"Come on, let's watch the snow."
As Xiaguo lifted the curtain, crisp winter air rushed in along with huge goose-feather snowflakes drifting down. Stepping into the yard, Qianli Xun saw the snow had only just begun, not yet carpeting Wangyue Pavilion white.
Kuqiao pulled the furry hood up over her mistress's head, then stood beside Xiaguo admiring the falling flakes.
Tipping her face skyward, Qianli Xun watched fat flakes float down in sheets, then looked down as flake after flake melted into nothing the instant it kissed the stones.
How brief a snowflake's life—drifting down from heaven just to vanish?
She thought of herself before crossing over—dead like a melted flake, thinking life was climbing toward its peak only for an earthquake to snuff everything out in a blink.
Grateful for this second shot at life, she treasured it so fierce she sometimes feared it was only a bizarre dying dream—until meeting Wenyuan made it feel undeniable.
Then why, if Wenyuan walked free now, hadn't he come? Was this all a dream after all? Yet everything felt too real: Kuqiao and Xiaguo beside her, cold flakes brushing her nose, the hot brass hand warmer burning against her palms.
Treasuring this hard-won life, she wanted every step to count. Her mutual divorce had been step one—rocky, yet a clean victory. Falling for Wenyuan had been step two—looking airtight until walls closed in from every side, leaving her trapped in Wangyue Pavilion waiting on a miracle from the sky...
Striding in through the snow, Matron Wei scolded both maids: "How do I train you two?! Letting Young Lady stand out in the snow—want her catching cold?!"
Both maids ducked their heads with sheepish grins.
Watching flakes whirl down, Qianli Xun said, "Don't scold them, Matron—I wanted to watch the snow."
Dropping the lecture without dragging her indoors, Matron Wei said, "Two guests are at the gate asking for you, Young Lady—want to see them?"
Qianli Xun blinked: "Odd way to ask, Matron—haven't even told me who they are!"
"The Qiaos."
"Wenyuan?!" Hope leaped in her chest.
Matron Wei hesitated: "Not Young Master Qiao—Master Qiao and Madam Qiao."
Disappointment dropped heavy in her stomach: "What do they want?"
"Wouldn't say—only swore they won't leave today until they see you. Smells like bad news to me; skip seeing them if you want, let them freeze outside as long as they like."
Pausing a beat, Qianli Xun decided: "Show them into the reception hall, Matron. Whatever they came for, at least I'll hear how Wenyuan is."
Hearing Qiao Manor visitors had arrived lit a spark in Xiaguo too—she missed Young Master Qiao every bit as much as Miss did—and she followed close behind with Kuqiao into the reception hall.
Slipping off her cape and setting down her hand warmer, Qianli Xun sat upright in the host's seat.
Truth be told, nerves prickled inside her: the two elders coming through that door had almost become her father- and mother-in-law. Why show up now? Surely not to beg her to marry Wenyuan?
She killed that fantasy in half a second—he was already betrothed to Miao Zhiyu, and she'd rather die than be a concubine.
Heart thumping, she waited as the two elders approached.
Behind the entry screen, Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian handed their snowy capes to waiting maids before Matron Wei led them around into the hall.
Qianli Xun wavered over standing up out of politeness—then remembered these two had severed every thread between her and Wenyuan! Temper flaring, she stayed seated in full host's pride.
Before a single word left their mouths, Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian dropped straight to their knees! Shock jolted Qianli Xun straight out of her chair, struck speechless.
Alarmed, Matron Wei rushed forward to haul them up: "What stunt is this?! Trying to curse our Young Lady's lifespan—kneeling before saying a word?! Get up at once!"
Neither elder budged.
Qiao Shouren spoke: "We came to beg Miss Zhu to let our Wenyuan go. Unless Miss Zhu agrees, we will not rise."
Fury exploded in Matron Wei: "What garbage is that?! Since moving to Wangyue Pavilion our Young Lady has barely stepped out the door—who's holding Young Master Qiao hostage?! Your house already engaged him to the Miaos—why come hounding our Young Lady?! Get up right now, imperial kin or not, or I'll grab a broomstick and chase you out!"
Madam Qian burst into sobs: "Waaah! Our Wenyuan is throwing his own life away over Miss Zhu—who else could we beg?! Waaah!"
Qianli Xun couldn't hold back: "Wenyuan... what happened to him?!"
Qiao Shouren's voice cracked: "Ever since the Miao betrothal, Wenyuan has raised hell demanding we break it off! How can a son dictate his own marriage? Of course I refused. Thought he'd blow off steam and settle down—instead he started starving himself to force our hand! Three full days without food—how can his body take it?! His mother and I even knelt begging him, and he swore only if Miss Zhu herself tells him she's giving up will he marry Miss Miao! Miss Zhu, between us two old bones we're past a hundred years old—kneeling here begging you, spare our Wenyuan, or the Qiao line dies out!"
Old tears streamed down Qiao Shouren's weathered cheeks as Madam Qian wept harder still.
Clarity crashed over Qianli Xun: Wenyuan hadn't abandoned her—he had been fighting for their future in the only way he knew! Every ounce of blame dissolved into raw heartache for him.
"Please stand up. Having elders kneel to me is more than I can bear."
Qiao Shouren held firm: "Until Miss Zhu promises to let Wenyuan go, we stay on our knees!"
She had thought blades were the deadliest weapons in this era—only now did she learn polite humility and filial duty could back a person further into a corner than any sword.
Matron Wei snapped in rage: "Bullying our Young Lady like this—where's the justice?! Your son starving himself is Qiao family business, nothing to do with our Young Lady! Go home and lecture your own son to marry Miss Miao and forget us—who cares about your imperial-relative prestige?!"
Madam Qian wailed: "Waaah! Save your lectures, Matron—until Miss Zhu lets Wenyuan go, Master and I won't rise! Beat us with clubs and we still won't rise! Waaah!"
Half a year had felt too short; barely two months in, fate had forced her hand.
Marrying Wenyuan was dead—not just because of the Miao engagement, but because the bitter grudge these Qiao elders now bore against her left zero room to step forward. Love still burned, yet reality demanded she let go.
Refusing to show a crack of pain in front of the Qiaos, she steadied her breath: "Stay on your knees one second longer, and I swear I will never let Wenyuan go."
Madam Qian choked off mid-sob, traded a glance with Qiao Shouren, and both elders scrambled to help each other up.
#CH 134
#TITLE: Hunger Strike Fails to Undo the Betrothal
Seeing both elders rise took a tiny weight off Qianli Xun—if they'd stayed kneeling, she might really have lost ten years off her lifespan!
Never showing their faces until today, and the second they walked in, they struck straight at her softest spot.
Pointless as asking was, one question burned inside her: "Master Qiao, Wenyuan and I love each other—why tear us apart over false rumors?"
Qiao Shouren spoke blunt: "Even before rumors broke, I opposed the match because you had been through a mutual divorce. Only Prince Yi talked me round into proposing to the Zhu house—then rumors exploded. True or false, Wenyuan can never marry a woman mocked by the whole city."
Prince Yi had stepped up for her and Wenyuan back then too... So no human villain had ruined them—they were simply fated to meet and never meant to stay.
"Leave now. Tell Wenyuan to meet me beneath the Divine Willow outside the city tomorrow at the hour of wei. You two will have your answer."
Qiao Shouren hated letting his son see Miss Zhu alone, yet had no better option: "See that Miss Zhu keeps the word you gave us today."
Qianli Xun offered only a dry, bitter smile.
Matron Wei snapped, "Our Young Lady spoke plain as day—still don't trust her, Master Qiao? Leave at once! You're not welcome at Wangyue Pavilion; don't show up here again!"
Driven out by Matron Wei's sharp tongue, Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian had no face to linger and left.
Every drop of strength drained from Qianli Xun; she sank limp into her chair without a word.
Heart aching for her, Matron Wei urged, "Go lie down in your room, Young Lady—a bed beats a hard chair."
Supported by both maids, Qianli Xun summoned the last of her strength to walk back to her chamber, collapse onto bed, and pull the quilt over her head...
After the Miao betrothal freed him from lockup, Qiao Wenxian couldn't face Shuzhen until he broke off the Miao engagement, so he had waged war demanding to annul it.
Yet neither argument nor pleading moved his elders; when Father threatened suicide again, he fell quiet—locking himself in his own room without needing Father's padlock.
With the spring wedding to Miao Zhiyu looming closer as the winter chill deepened, desperation drove him to a hunger strike.
Truth be told, it was half theater for his parents—Nan Shuang slipped him snacks on the quiet so his health wouldn't collapse, remembering Shuzhen's plea to take care of himself so she wouldn't see a wreck once he won.
Still, stolen snacks couldn't replace hot meals; he dropped another size thinner and played up his frailty in front of his parents until tears streamed down their faces.
Guilt tugged at him watching them weep, yet for a life with Shuzhen, he steeled himself and refused to budge.
Even pushed this far, Father wouldn't yield—how could they break a sealed betrothal and ruin Miss Miao's reputation?—ordering him to forget Miss Zhu.
So Qiao Wenxian lay weak and resolute: "Unless Shuzhen tells me to my face she's giving up, I won't stop."
And that had sent Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian trudging through snow to Wangyue Pavilion.
Back at Qiao Manor, Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian entered their son's courtyard, handed their snowy capes to maids, and walked into the inner bedchamber.
Eyeing their gaunt, listless son sprawled on the bed, Qiao Shouren sighed: "Look at the state of you—was it worth it? Miss Zhu looked livelier than you by a mile!"
At Shuzhen's name, life sparked in Qiao Wenxian as he propped himself up: "Father saw Shuzhen?!"
Before Qiao Shouren spoke, Madam Qian sank tearful onto the bedside: "Your father and I saw her. Not a trace of sadness on her—clearly she doesn't care about you at all! She sent word for you to meet her beneath the Divine Willow tomorrow at the hour of wei."
Qiao Wenxian bolted upright: "Shuzhen wants to see me?! Did Father and Mother agree to cancel the Miao betrothal?!"
Qiao Shouren cut cold: "Breaking the betrothal is out of the question! We're letting you see her because we saw straight through her—she has no feelings left for you! Go hear it from her own lips and snap out of it."
Alarm flashed across Qiao Wenxian: "What did you say to Shuzhen?!"
Madam Qian lied smooth: "With our standing, would we ever bully her? Seeing how devoted you are, we even offered—for love's sake—to let her enter as your concubine! Yet she refused flat, said everything between you two is finished, and called you out tomorrow to make a clean break..."
"No! How dare you ask her to be a concubine?! Shuzhen only spoke cold out of anger—I'm going to find her right now!"
