Pushing past Madam Qian, Qiao Wenxian swung out of bed, caught himself half-dressed, and barked, "Nan Shuang, dress me—bring my red robe! I can't let Shuzhen see me looking like a ghost..."
Just as Nan Shuang reached for the robe, Qiao Shouren thundered: "Enough! How long will this circus drag on?! Stop! Are you weaker than a woman?! Miss Zhu sat cool as ice, while here you are starving yourself, demanding annulments, fretting she'll cry seeing you pale—are you even a son of the Qiao house, a scholar admired across Lin'an?!"
His father's lash combined with days of half-starvation made Qiao Wenxian sway, almost pitching over.
Nan Shuang jumped in to catch him and ease him into a chair.
Madam Qian sobbed: "My boy! She isn't worth wrecking yourself over! Waaah!"
Face blank as stone, Qiao Wenxian murmured chill: "You two forced me to this."
Qiao Shouren ordered, "Quit crying and come with me—leave him alone to think straight."
Dabbing her eyes, Madam Qian walked out.
Reaching the outer room, Qiao Shouren paused, stepped back in, and softened his voice: "The Miao wedding stands. Miss Zhu has already moved on, so act like a man. Still, your mother and I will take one step back: if you can talk her into being your concubine tomorrow, both wife and concubine enter Qiao Manor together come spring."
With that, Qiao Shouren threw on his cape and strode out into the snow, Madam Qian trailing behind.
Snow fell thicker outside, blanketing the stones where Qiao Shouren and Madam Qian left crisp footprints.
Beyond the courtyard gate, Madam Qian wiped tears against the swirling flakes and asked, "Husband, would you really let Miss Zhu enter as Wenyuan's concubine?"
Qiao Shouren stopped dead: "Her, Wenyuan's concubine? In her next life! With a reputation that filthy?!"
Puzzled, Madam Qian asked, "Then why tell him that?"
Qiao Shouren smiled sly: "First, toss Wenyuan a scrap of hope so he doesn't collapse or duck tomorrow's meeting out of fear. Second, from what I saw of Miss Zhu today, she'd die before accepting concubine—safe as houses making that promise!"
"Nobody outsmarts you, Husband—just breaks my heart for Wenyuan." Fresh tears brimmed in Madam Qian's eyes.
Inside, aching for his master, Nan Shuang urged, "Let me fetch food from the kitchen, Young Master—get some strength back before seeing Youqi Jushi tomorrow."
Not a day had passed without Qiao Wenxian yearning for Shuzhen; he had held off visiting only hoping to fix everything first, then rush to her beaming, "Shuzhen, let's get married!"
Now he had to face her with nothing fixed—knowing tomorrow's meeting might end everything between them.
Equal parts desperate to see her and terrified to see her, he let out a bitter laugh at himself: "Hmph—engaged to another woman, what right do you have asking her to wait? If she doesn't quit now, when would she? Expect her to grow old alone waiting on you?"
Not catching his master's muttering, Nan Shuang asked again, "What do you feel like eating, Young Master? I'll tell the kitchen."
Dropping the hunger strike—he couldn't look like a corpse facing Shuzhen tomorrow—Qiao Wenxian waved a hand: "Whatever's ready."
Nan Shuang lit up: "Right away! I'll run to the kitchen!"
Stepping into the yard, Nan Shuang bumped right into Concubine Mi arriving beneath an umbrella held by one maid while another carried a food carrier.
Concubine Mi chided him: "Where are you racing off to?"
Nan Shuang answered fast: "Concubine Mi! Young Master wants food—I was running to the kitchen."
"Save your legs—didn't you see my maid carrying a food carrier?"
Stepping inside while one maid folded the umbrella at the door and took her snowy cape, Concubine Mi sent Nan Shuang trailing right back in behind her.
Entering the inner chamber and seeing her worn-out son, Concubine Mi's eyes reddened at once: "Quit torturing yourself, Wenyuan. Mama stewed bird's-nest porridge to build your strength back."
Lifting his eyes to the woman who gave him birth, Qiao Wenxian asked, "Mama, you carried me ten months—you ought to want me happy. Why are even you against me marrying Shuzhen?"
"Silly boy, it's because I gave birth to you that I want the best for you—and that means never letting you marry a divorced woman with a ruined name!"
"Only Shuzhen is the one I want to marry."
"You're too young to grasp what marriage really is. Listen to your elders—we won't steer you wrong."
"Young? I've traveled wide, seen Bianliang, attended His Majesty's banquets—what haven't I seen? Don't treat me like a child."
"See the whole world and you're still green until you've been wed. Why is Miss Zhu twice as coolheaded as you? Because she's been married and divorced once—she knows what marriage really takes."
Concubine Mi's remark pierced straight through Qiao Wenxian—could Shuzhen's love never have burned as fierce as he believed?
"Enough brooding. Eat up, make a clean break with Miss Zhu tomorrow, and come home to live a good life."
Another twist of the knife inside him: "Fine, I'll eat. Maybe a full stomach hurts less."
Setting the steaming bird's-nest porridge on the table, the maid stepped back while Nan Shuang helped his master to a chair.
Lifting his spoon, memories of sweet days with Shuzhen rose up, bringing a faint smile to his lips. He refused to believe Shuzhen had stopped loving him—his parents had to be lying! Tomorrow, face to face, he would know the truth.
#CH 135
#TITLE: Parting Ways Beneath the Divine Willow
Snow fell past midnight before ceasing, laying a thick white blanket across Wangyue Pavilion. At dawn, servants set to sweeping the garden paths clear.
Knowing her mistress's heartache, Matron Wei told Kuqiao and Xiaguo not to wake her—let her lie abed as long as she wanted, just keep warm meals coming in case hunger struck.
Qianli Xun had been wide awake for hours, refusing to get up so nobody would see her puffy, swollen eyes. All night under the quilt she had ordered herself—"Don't cry, don't cry, whatever you do don't cry"—and sobbed her eyes out anyway.
Her very first real dive into love, ending like this—what on earth had she done wrong?
Not a single thing! All blame belonged to the rat behind those rumors—catch him and she'd pummel him ten days straight, no, a whole year straight!
Doubt crept in over Master Jingguang's teaching: she had clung to Right Thought and Right Mindfulness, walking clean and straight—why still get smeared until she and Wenyuan were ripped apart?
Was some invisible hand pulling the strings of destiny? If so, Qianli Xun wanted to grab that hand, make it hers, and seize control of her own fate.
Though snow had stopped, leaden clouds hung low and melting snow made the air feel even colder than yesterday.
A chill crept right under her quilt; aching from lying too long, she sat up, parted the bed curtains, and saw the brazier glowing bright red—so the chill came from inside her own chest.
Realizing no warm quilt could thaw the frost inside her, lying there felt pointless.
She called out: "Kuqiao!"
Hearing her, Kuqiao hurried into the inner chamber carrying fresh hot tea—and one look at those puffy red eyes told her Miss had cried half the night.
"You're awake, Miss. Drink a bowl of hot tea first—just whisked."
As Qianli Xun took the tea bowl, Kuqiao hooked back the bed curtains on both sides.
Hot tea slid down, warming her core and pushing back the chill. She had to face Wenyuan at the hour of wei—time to pull herself together.
Taking the empty bowl, Kuqiao brought fresh robes and helped her dress: "The morning meal went cold, so the kitchen will send hot dishes right over. Wash up, eat something, and we'll head out with plenty of time."
Only then did Qianli Xun realize how late she had lain in bed, the overcast sky hiding the hour.
After washing up, she had Kuqiao apply powder to mask her swollen eyes.
Careful as Kuqiao brushed it on, the puffiness refused to hide.
"Run to the kitchen and bring back a hot hard-boiled egg."
"Right away!" Catching on, Kuqiao darted off.
Returning with a piping-hot egg tucked inside her sleeve, Kuqiao tested the heat until just right, then rolled it gently over each of her mistress's closed eyelids.
Eyes shut, Qianli Xun drank in the comforting warmth, feeling the congested puffiness loosen and fade.
"Works like magic, Miss—the swelling around your eyes went right down!" Kuqiao put the egg aside.
Checking the bronze mirror, Qianli Xun saw her eyes looked normal again.
"Pick out a bright lip rouge."
Afraid pain might bleed onto her face before Wenyuan, maybe bold color would mask the cracks.
Kuqiao chose a vivid crimson and painted her mistress's lips.
Kitchen maids brought fresh hot dishes, laid the table, cleared the cold platters, and bowed out.
Despite skipping both dinner last night and breakfast today, not a hint of hunger stirred; the steaming dishes held zero appeal.
Still, she had to force some down—she'd need strength to speak the cruel words ahead.
Sitting down, she lifted her chopsticks and forced down bite after bite, every mouthful tasting like dry wax—learning for the first time how hard simply swallowing food could be.
Halfway through, she couldn't stomach another bite, and the hour had come. Putting on her warm winter cape, clutching her hand warmer, she stepped out with Kuqiao.
Out in the yard, Xiaguo popped out of the maids' quarters: "Miss! Take me along too!"
Saying nothing, Qianli Xun walked straight on, and Xiaguo hurried right after—wild with longing for Young Master Qiao, refusing to miss a single chance to lay eyes on him.
Snow stood piled along both edges of the city streets as hawkers braved the freezing air, market bustle undimmed by winter.
Only the thick white blankets capping every roof and bare bough turned Lin'an into a silver-and-jade landscape.
Qianli Xun had zero eye for snow scenery; no winter frost could match the cold inside her chest.
...
Stepping down from the carriage at the foot of the hill, deep snow met her boots. Few folk climbed the hill in weather like this, leaving the white drift near spotless.
Clutching her hand warmer, snow crunched beneath every step as she walked toward the Divine Willow. Dead grass lay buried deep, bare shrubs huddled tight, opening a clear vista—and far ahead stood the Divine Willow.
Amid unbroken white, the willow wore its own cloak of frost, stark and breathtaking against the winter sky.
And against that sea of white stood one vivid crimson figure—a silhouette Qianli Xun knew down to her bones, haunting her mind ever since Youlin Retreat.
Slowing her steps as she drew near, she both ached to reach him and dreaded reaching him.
Qiao Wenxian had chosen his thick crimson cape on purpose to lend color to his pale face—whatever Shuzhen had to say today, he meant to show her his best self.
Spotting three figures moving across the white field, Nan Shuang whispered, "Young Master, Youqi Jushi is here."
Though seeming to study snow-laden willow branches, Qiao Wenxian's whole soul had been tuned behind him. Hearing she had arrived, he spun round with an irrepressible rush of joy—love still burned, impossible to hide!
Stepping forward to pull her into his arms like last time, he stopped dead as Shuzhen stepped back.
His arms dropped empty: "Shuzhen, I... I missed you so much..."
A thousand words surged in his throat, none able to cross his lips.
Wenyuan had wasted so thin; bright robes and a forced smile couldn't hide the hollow weariness in his face. Pain twisted inside Qianli Xun—when love brought neither soul peace, letting go was the kindest cut.
"How foolish you are, Wenyuan—gambling your own life over me isn't worth it! Eat well, drink well, and look after yourself from now on. Look at me: knowing I'd see you today, I ate extra!"
Kuqiao sighed silently: "Why lie, Miss? You barely ate half a bowl!"
Eyes glued solely to Young Master Qiao, Xiaguo felt her heart twist seeing him two sizes thinner.
Standing behind his master, Nan Shuang stared at Xiaguo—pretty enough to rival Youqi Jushi! If only she'd look his way...
Shuzhen's breezy tone sent dread rippling through Qiao Wenxian.
"Shuzhen, I know you're angry—my parents arranged the Miao betrothal behind my back! I only starved myself to force them to cancel it..."
"Wenyuan! Miao Zhiyu is a fine girl—talented and good-hearted. Even when the whole city cursed me, she stood up for me! Knowing you'll marry her puts my mind at ease..."
"Why say things like that?! You're the one I want to marry—you've always known that! Shuzhen..." Catching her hands wrapped around the hand warmer, Qiao Wenxian pleaded, "Come away from Lin'an with me! To the ends of the earth, anywhere—as long as I'm with you, nothing else matters!"
For one wild heartbeat Qianli Xun almost said yes—then reason clamped down hard. Pulling her hands back onto her hand warmer, she kept her voice flat: "You'd regret it sooner or later."
Her pulled-back hands sent panic surging through him; refusing rejection, he swore, "I'll never regret it!"
Qianli Xun forced a cold laugh: "Just realizing today how selfish you are, Wenyuan—thinking only of your own happiness! Did you ever stop to think what happens to your parents if you run off? Or to Miao Zhiyu?"
Stepping closer with reddened eyes locked onto hers: "Then tell me—what are we supposed to do?!"
Unable to hold his gaze lest she cave and run off with him right now, Qianli Xun turned aside toward the Divine Willow, watching a clump of snow slip from a bough and vanish into the drift below.
"Let's break up, Wenyuan."
Hearing the word he dreaded most, Qiao Wenxian squeezed his eyes shut as two tears rolled down, then whipped his gaze back to her: "I don't agree!"
Qianli Xun forced a thin smile: "Hilarious—what right do you have to disagree? You're an engaged man! Expect me to marry into your house as your concubine?"
"Would you?!" Hope flared sharp in his voice—it was the one bridge left!
Steeling every ounce of resolve—ending things clean was why she came today—she waited until her gaze felt cold as steel before facing him square: "Even if I never marry and grow old all alone, I will never be anyone's concubine."
Stung raw, reason snapped inside Qiao Wenxian: "Wait right here, Shuzhen—I'm marching straight to the Miao house to break off the betrothal right now!"
Spinning on his heel, he started off toward the far side of the pond. Nan Shuang hurried after to steady him, only to get shoved away.
Snow ringed the pond while thick ice sealed its surface—freezing the water beneath and freezing Qiao Wenxian's love along with it.
As he stumbled across the snow toward the far bank, Qianli Xun cried out behind him: "It's useless, Wenyuan! Never mind you can't break it off—even if you did, I still wouldn't marry you!"
His stumbling steps froze. Whipping back in raw agony, he shouted, "Why?! How can you say something so cruel?!"
Lifting heavy boots through the snow, Qianli Xun walked closer: "Because... I refuse to build my happiness on other people's pain! Break that betrothal and your family breaks, and Miao Zhiyu breaks even worse. Thank you, Wenyuan—thank you for giving me a beautiful romance. Now... it's time to end it. Let us part clean and wish each other peace."
Turning away before he could answer, tears poured down her face like rain; she locked her shoulders tight against sobs so he wouldn't see her back shaking.
Watching their mistress break apart tore at both maids—above all Xiaguo, aching for Miss and Young Master Qiao alike. When Miss walked away, they had no choice but to follow.
Hearing "part clean and wish each other peace" broke the last dam inside Qiao Wenxian: he collapsed to his knees in the snow and sobbed his heart out.
Behind Qianli Xun echoed Wenyuan's ragged, heartbroken weeping...
#CH 136
#TITLE: Silent Company on the Road Home
Qianli Xun marched back far faster than she had come, terrified one breath slower would make her turn around.
Only once Wenyuan's weeping faded behind the hills did her strength give out; the hand warmer tumbled from her grip into the snow, melting a wet ring where it landed...
Just as her knees buckled and both maids lunged to catch her, Prince Yi stepped out of nowhere and caught her steady in his arms.
Catching her breath for a second, Qianli Xun shoved Prince Yi off and kept walking.
Scooping up the hand warmer, Kuqiao bowed quick to Prince Yi and hurried after her mistress.
Racing ahead even faster, Xiaguo blocked her mistress's path, dropped to her knees in the snow, and wept: "Miss, Young Master Qiao is so pitiful—let me go comfort him for you!"
Everything clicked at last for Qianli Xun. In a drained, cool tone she answered, "Do whatever you want—nothing to do with me."
Xiaguo kowtowed in thanks: "Thank you, Miss!"
Stepping around Xiaguo, Qianli Xun trudged on while Shen Jianchen followed in silence behind her.
Jealousy burned inside him for Wenyuan—that the one woman he could never win would bleed like this for another man! "If you loved me and chose me," he grumbled silently, "I'd never let you hurt like this."
Reaching the carriages, Qianli Xun climbed straight into hers without waiting for Kuqiao's arm.
Prince Yi's carriage stood parked right beside it, Bai Feng waiting at the reins.
Much as Kuqiao had missed Bai Feng, seeing her mistress wrecked left her zero mood for romance. Stepping up to follow Miss aboard, Prince Yi stepped in her way.
"Ride with Bai Feng. I have words for Youqi Jushi."
Against a royal prince's command, Kuqiao couldn't argue.
Holding out the hand warmer: "Please give this back to my mistress, Your Highness."
Taking it, Shen Jianchen climbed into Qianli Xun's carriage.
Overjoyed, Bai Feng took Kuqiao's hand and helped her up into Prince Yi's carriage.
Holding onto the hand warmer instead of handing it right over—afraid she'd boot him straight back out—Shen Jianchen sat across from her in total silence, keeping quiet company.
Qianli Xun acted as if he were thin air, too drained to scold him or shove him out, letting him watch her while she kept her eyes lowered over her raw ache.
The moment her mistress departed, Xiaguo scrambled out of the snow and ran straight back toward Young Master Qiao.
Beneath the Divine Willow, Qiao Wenxian still knelt in the snow weeping.
"Get up, Young Master—that snow's freezing, you'll ruin your knees!" Nan Shuang tried hauling him up, got shoved off again, and stood helpless until spotting Xiaguo running up.
"Young Master, it's Xiaguo! Youqi Jushi must have sent word!"
Lifting blurred, tear-soaked eyes, Qiao Wenxian saw Xiaguo hastening toward him.
Scrambling up, he stumbled straight to her and caught her arms: "Did Shuzhen take it back?! Did she agree to elope with me?!"
Tenderness brimmed in Xiaguo's eyes: "Miss didn't send me. Seeing Young Master hurting broke my heart, so I came back to check on you."
Dropping her arms in hollow disappointment, Qiao Wenxian turned and began trudging away.
Xiaguo ran ahead to block him: "Young Master Qiao, there are ten thousand women in this world besides Miss! She gave up on you, yet someone else right here loves you..."
Stepping around her, Qiao Wenxian stumbled on.
Xiaguo circled ahead again: "Look at me, Young Master Qiao! Everyone says I look a little like Miss! Miss refused to be your concubine—I'm willing!"
Before his master even reacted, Nan Shuang's jaw hit the snow—so THAT was why Xiaguo turned up her nose at him! Aiming straight for his master!
Eyes rimmed raw red, Qiao Wenxian glanced at her—never noticed before, yet yes, she did carry a faint shadow of Shuzhen. A hollow laugh escaped him: "So what if you look like her? You aren't her."
"I can read now, I know how to whisk tea, I've read books—even 'Collected Poems of Wang Youcheng'! And I'll read heaps more..." Desperate to win him while his heart lay shattered, Xiaguo rattled off every credential she had.
"Go away and leave me in peace." Speaking chill as ice, Qiao Wenxian brushed past her once more, stumbling toward the far bank.
As Xiaguo moved to block him again, Nan Shuang stepped straight in front of her: "My master doesn't want you! Stop hounding him and scram!"
Halting, Xiaguo shouted after Qiao Wenxian's retreating back: "I won't give up, Young Master Qiao! One day you'll say yes to me!"
Nan Shuang scoffed: "You'd be lucky to marry someone like me—dreaming of my master? Quit hallucinating!"
Xiaguo snapped back: "You're the one hallucinating! Watch when I become Young Master Qiao's concubine—see if you dare talk to me like that then!"
With a loud "Hmph!", Nan Shuang ran off to catch his master.
Once the carriage rolled onto the city avenues and Shen Jianchen felt sure Qianli Xun wouldn't kick him out, he tucked the hand warmer into her lap: "It's freezing—hold this to warm your hands."
Without pushing it back, Qianli Xun took it and curled her fingers around the warm brass. Maybe she really did crave a scrap of warmth right now, and Prince Yi's timing hit dead on. Even so, she stayed sharp—knowing he was fishing for an opening while her guard sat low, refusing to let him slip past her defenses.
Happy as Kuqiao was sitting beside Bai Feng, worry for her mistress kept her tense.
Bai Feng comforted her: "Don't worry over Youqi Jushi—His Highness says she'll grieve a spell and bounce right back."
Kuqiao blinked: "Since when does Prince Yi understand my mistress that well?"
Bai Feng thought a second: "Youqi Jushi is Princess's best friend—His Highness probably heard it from Princess."
Kuqiao frowned deeper: "Don't you think it's weird, Bai Feng?"
"What's weird?"
"My mistress is Princess's best friend—yet when Miss gets heartbroken today, Princess didn't come, Prince Yi came instead! Tell me what's up with that!"
"Simple! His Highness spoils Princess—freezing snow out here, didn't want her catching cold, so he came to look after Youqi Jushi in her place!"
"What kind of logic is that?! Prince Yi's an unmarried man to my mistress—doesn't Princess mind?"
"Stumped me there! Forget all that—rare enough we get time alone, talk about something nicer!"
Still unable to shake her gloom, Kuqiao sighed: "Young Master Qiao and Miss were a match made in heaven—why on earth couldn't they end up together, ending in tears like this?"
"His Highness says it's all because of parents' orders and matchmakers' words. Don't worry about us—I've got no parents, so between you and me it's pure free will!"
Realizing she was luckier than her own mistress, Kuqiao teased, "No parents, sure, yet you've got Prince Yi! What if he orders you to marry someone else?"
"If His Highness ever forces me, I'll quit being his guard and run away with you to Bianliang! Got plenty of buddies there and savings stacked up from serving His Highness—we'll open a little shop and live cozy!"
Kuqiao blushed: "Who said I'm running to Bianliang with you? I have to wait on Miss!"
"Easy—don't want Bianliang, I'll stay right here in Lin'an and sign on as Youqi Jushi's guard instead!"
Kuqiao laughed while chiding him: "Talking like Prince Yi already forced you into a wedding!"
"Just saying! His Highness is open-minded—he'd never force me."
The carriage pulled up outside Wangyue Pavilion and came to a halt.
Qianli Xun stayed seated a silent beat before speaking: "Thank you for riding along, Prince Yi. I'm heading inside—please don't do things like this again. It's a waste of effort."
Ignoring the warning, Shen Jianchen asked with gentle care: "Feeling any better?"
"How I feel is none of Prince Yi's concern."
"How is it none of my concern? When you hurt, I hurt too."
What a grandmaster player, Qianli Xun marveled: quiet escort, silky lines—any heartbroken girl whose armor slipped an inch would fall straight into his lap!
Lucky she wasn't any ordinary girl to melt under sweet talk.
"Prince Yi is the Emperor's son, high above all—yet here you are escorting a commoner and whispering sweet nothings. Planning to marry Mengrong and take me as your concubine on the side? Let me give you the exact answer I gave Wenyuan today: even if I grow old alone without ever marrying, I will never be anyone's concubine."
Pushing open the carriage door, she hopped down, leaving Prince Yi sitting alone inside.
A bitter laugh escaped Shen Jianchen: "When did I ever want you as a concubine? Is that really how you see me? I just can't stop caring about you—because you're the only woman I want to marry."
Already waiting outside, Kuqiao saw her mistress march straight into Wangyue Pavilion, bade Bai Feng a hurried goodbye, and chased inside.
When His Highness still didn't climb down, Bai Feng nudged, "Time to head back, Your Highness."
Jolting as if from a trance, Shen Jianchen stepped out, gazed into Wangyue Pavilion's gate seeing nothing, and fought down the urge to stride right through.
Letting out a sigh, he climbed back into his own carriage for Youlin Retreat.
Inside Wangyue Pavilion, Qianli Xun marched straight into her bedchamber and shut the door.
One step behind, Kuqiao stood outside the closed door knowing without looking that Miss was curled back under the quilt nursing her broken heart.
Matron Wei walked up: "Back already?"
Kuqiao nodded: "Even sadder than yesterday."
"Wouldn't be human if she weren't! Every hurdle has to be crossed once, then it's behind her. Leave her in peace—check the door later; if she calls, go in, if not, enjoy a breather. Nothing catastrophic happened; three days of sleep and she'll bounce right back." Scanning the yard, Matron Wei frowned: "Where's Xiaguo?"
Stepping close, Kuqiao whispered, "Found out today Xiaguo's got a crush on Young Master Qiao! Right while Miss was falling apart, she dropped to her knees begging to go comfort Young Master Qiao!"
Matron Wei bristled: "Knew that girl didn't know her place—didn't dream she'd aim all the way up at Young Master Qiao, ditching a heartbroken mistress to chase him! Shameless! Wait till she gets back—I'll give her a proper dressing-down!"
Kuqiao put in a mild word: "Forget it, Matron—even Miss didn't hold it against her."
"Young Lady lets it go out of generosity; I step in as an elder to teach her basic decency! Send her straight to my room the second she walks through the gate." Turning sharp, Matron Wei marched off.
With her mistress needing no tending, Kuqiao returned to the maids' quarters, worried for Miss as she took out Bai Feng's clay figurine and murmured to it: "Miss is kind, good, and brilliant—why was her love life cursed before amnesia and cursed all over again after?"
Mid-murmur she caught herself and laughed—a clay doll wasn't a Bodhisattva, what use praying to it? Seeing Bai Feng so rarely lately had turned talking to his figurine into a silly habit.
#CH 137
#TITLE: Her Best Friend Returns to Wangyue Pavilion
Crushed by Young Master Qiao's rejection, Xiaguo found herself stranded at the foot of the hill with Miss's carriage gone and no rides in sight, forced to trudge all the way back into town on foot. Freezing and exhausted, she thank goodness had loose silver on her and hired a carriage back to Wangyue Pavilion.
Warmer inside the bustling city than out by the hills, snow was already melting off eaves and bare boughs—plops of wet slush dropping from branches while steady *drip, drip, drip* echoed from rooftops.
Paying the driver outside Wangyue Pavilion, Xiaguo marched straight into the maids' quarters, flung herself onto bed, and burst into sobs: "Waaah!"
Having put away her figurine to embroider, Kuqiao watched Xiaguo bawl without a word and snapped, "Ditching Miss right while she was breaking down—doesn't look like it got you anywhere! Look in a mirror at your station; how could Young Master Qiao ever look twice at you?!"
Sobbing back, Xiaguo cried, "Quit rubbing salt in it, Sister Kuqiao! Waaah! What crime is loving Young Master Qiao?! Just because we're maids, aren't we allowed to love a man like him?! Waaah!"
Mention of a maid's lot struck a chord in Kuqiao, softening her edge: "I only scolded you for ditching Miss! She'd just forced out cruel words to break up with Young Master Qiao, and you jump right onto him—how do you think Miss felt? Pure salt in her wound!"
"Waaah! I didn't think straight! Waaah! I'll go kneel at Miss's feet and beg forgiveness right now!" Xiaguo scrambled up toward the door.
Kuqiao blocked her: "Miss locked herself indoors the second she got back—don't bother her. Matron Wei told you to report to her room the minute you returned."
Guessing Kuqiao had spilled everything when Matron Wei asked why she wasn't back, Xiaguo wiped her eyes on her sleeve: "Honest to a fault, aren't you, Sister Kuqiao—couldn't cover for me once!"
Kuqiao didn't blink: "I'm still mad at you—why on earth would I lie for you? Go get a proper lecture from Matron Wei!"
Sulking and aggrieved, Xiaguo dragged herself out toward Matron Wei's quarters.
Matron Wei practically ran half of Wangyue Pavilion, inspiring equal parts envy and dread in Xiaguo.
Maids at Zhu Manor whispered Matron Wei had started out as an ordinary maid in her youth—how had she climbed into half a mistress whom even Master Zhu treated with deference?
Outside Matron Wei's door, Xiaguo called in a small voice: "Matron, Xiaguo is here."
From inside came Matron Wei's voice: "Come in."
Head hung low, Xiaguo stepped inside—more nervous than she had ever been before her mistress.
Sitting on the couch, Matron Wei took one look at Xiaguo's swollen red eyes and knew she had cried up a storm.
Once Xiaguo halted, Matron Wei asked, "Just got back, or came back with Young Lady?"
"Knows already and still asks!" Xiaguo thought, answering meekly: "Just got back."
"Why didn't you come back with Young Lady? You're the personal maid she picked herself!"
Xiaguo lied: "Miss told me to stay behind and comfort Young Master Qiao for her."
Matron Wei's voice turned steel: "Bold-faced lie! Young Lady went today to make a clean break with Young Master Qiao—why would she ask you to comfort him?!"
Xiaguo dropped to her knees: "Is loving Young Master Qiao a crime, Matron?!"
"Daring to talk back?! Let me count your sins for you! First, Young Lady saved you from ruin, yet you coveted the man your benefactor loved! Second, as her personal maid, you abandoned your wounded mistress to chase another household's master—forgetting your place! Third, born humble, you harbored wild delusions—thinking a pretty face buys your way into noble society! Without Young Lady taking you in, would you ever have met gentlemen like him? Meeting him wasn't enough—you shamelessly offered yourself as his concubine! And look what it got you—thrown straight back in your face!"
Xiaguo gaped—did Matron Wei have supernatural ears?! How did she know she had offered to be his concubine?!
"How did Matron know all that?!"
"A child could guess what nonsense you spouted to Young Master Qiao! You've shamed our Young Lady enough—I'll give you some silver and send you back to the countryside!"
Indignation flared in Xiaguo: "What right does Matron have to fire me?! I'm Miss's maid—only Miss gets to send me away! If Miss says leave, I'll pack up for the countryside without a peep!"
Matron Wei's tone stayed iron: "Young Lady handed management of this house to me—I hold full authority over every maid, and Young Lady won't breathe one word against my decision."
Xiaguo burst into frantic sobs: "Waaah! Spare me this once, Matron! I'll never harbor wild ideas again, waaah! Even if Matron sends me away, let me wait till Miss comes out so I can say goodbye—we've been mistress and maid together! Waaah!"
"At least you have a shred of conscience left."
Right then a maid called from outside: "Matron, Princess Jiahe is here to visit!"
With royalty at the gate, Matron Wei set Xiaguo aside: "Get up and go back to the maids' quarters. No time to argue with you while a noble guest waits—once Young Lady feels better, we'll settle your fate."
Stepping off the couch, Matron Wei hurried out to welcome the princess.
Xiaguo let out a shaky breath, blessing Princess Jiahe for showing up right in the nick of time!
Worried sick over Qianli Xun, Shen Jianchen had racked his brains back at Youlin Retreat and seen only one lifeline: ask Mengrong to step in.
Unaware of today's breakup beneath the willow, Qiao Mengrong grew worried the instant he told her and volunteered right away to go stay with Shuzhen and comfort her.
Relieved, Shen Jianchen escorted Mengrong to Wangyue Pavilion's gate—and seeing Matron Wei come out to receive them told him Qianli Xun was hurting bad, making him itch to march straight inside himself.
Bowing low, Matron Wei said, "Forgive the wasted trip, Prince Yi and Princess Jiahe—our Young Lady isn't up to seeing guests today."
Qiao Mengrong smiled: "Not a wasted trip at all! I'm staying overnight at Wangyue Pavilion tonight to have a good heart-to-heart with Shuzhen."
Gratitude beamed on Matron Wei's face: "Our Young Lady is blessed three lifetimes over to have a friend like Princess! Right this way, Princess!"
Worrying about Prince Yi, Qiao Mengrong glanced back, wondering whether to invite him in for a cup of tea.
Reading her mind, Shen Jianchen said, "I'm staying at Qiao Manor tonight to keep Wenyuan company."
Reassured, Qiao Mengrong stepped inside Wangyue Pavilion with Xilan.
Only once Mengrong vanished inside did Shen Jianchen climb back into his carriage for Qiao Manor.
All the way there, guilt chewed at him for exploiting Mengrong's sweet heart again—if she ever learned the truth, what would she think of him?
Guiding Princess Jiahe to the still-closed bedchamber door, Matron Wei called through: "Young Lady, Princess Jiahe is here and wants to stay the night with you."
Hearing Mengrong had come, Qianli Xun scrambled off bed, glancing at the window—already pitch dark! Winter skies snuffed daylight out so early.
Walking into the outer room, she opened the door, threw back the heavy curtain, and flung her arms around Mengrong without even a coat on:
"You're here at last! One more hour and I would've trekked through the dark to Youlin Retreat myself!"
Even in the dim glow of courtyard lanterns, Matron Wei spotted her mistress's puffy, reddened eyes.
"Get back inside—it's freezing and you're barely dressed!" Pulling Shuzhen straight indoors, Qiao Mengrong stepped in with Xilan right behind.
With Princess Jiahe here, Qianli Xun could no longer bar her maids.
Out in the yard, Matron Wei called, "Kuqiao! Xiaguo! Come wait on Princess and Young Lady!"
Hearing Matron Wei call her lifted a boulder off Xiaguo—maybe Matron wouldn't kick her out after all! Hurrying out with Kuqiao, she stepped inside to serve.
Xiaguo checked the brazier and added fresh charcoal while Kuqiao draped a warm robe over her mistress.
Matron Wei suggested, "Eat some dinner, Young Lady—can't let Princess go hungry."
Catching on at once that Shuzhen hadn't eaten, Qiao Mengrong jumped in: "I rushed over without dinner at Youlin Retreat—mind if I mooch a meal here?"
Xilan smiled inward: Princess had eaten a full dinner already, pretending just to make Youqi Jushi eat—what a loyal friend!
No appetite, yet unable to starve her guest, Qianli Xun nodded: "What did the kitchen make?"
"Your favorites—two-color lotus seed chicken soup and wild vegetable steamed buns! I'll have them brought right over." Matron Wei hurried off to the kitchen.
Those were the exact two dishes she had eaten on her first day waking up in Lin Manor—delicious enough she never tired of them whenever Matron Wei had them cooked.
Moments later kitchen maids laid out the steaming supper.
Xiaguo stepped out to the tea table to whisk tea ahead of time for after dinner, while Kuqiao and Xilan waited at the table.
Not hungry at all, Qiao Mengrong only sipped half a bowl of soup and left the buns untouched.
Seeing straight through her, warmth welled in Qianli Xun: "Already ate and still putting on a show!"
"Busted! Only because of you—if you ate right, I wouldn't have to fake it! Make this the first and last time, okay?"
"Deal—definitely the last! I've sworn off romance for good."
"Nonsense! Once bitten by a snake, scared of rope for ten years? What's that other saying—right, refusing to eat for fear of choking!"
"Save your proverbs. Every day I realize the other me had it right—no dating, no marriage, pure focus on career! Back to my original self from now on."
Princess Jiahe tilted her head: "The other you? Strange way to put it."
"Not strange at all—doesn't Princess have another self inside her too?"
"Another me?"
Turning inward, Qiao Mengrong felt two distinct voices in her own chest: one insisting Brother still loved her just as before, the other whispering this man wasn't the Brother she used to know.
A glimmer of awe crossed her eyes: "Maybe I really do have another self... You say the deepest things, Shuzhen!"
Her friend's praise couldn't melt the ache just yet: "How did you know I was having a rough day?"
"I didn't—Brother told me you made a clean break with Young Master Qiao today and said Young Master Qiao was devastated. Figured you had to be hurting too, so I rushed over."
Prince Yi sure knew how to spin a yarn! Sitting right across from her in the carriage all the way home, yet telling Mengrong how devastated Wenyuan was as if he'd been holding Wenyuan's hand the whole time!
Annoyance pushed aside her grief: "Sounds like Prince Yi was comforting Wenyuan today?"
"Of course he comforted Young Master Qiao—how else would he know how broken up he was?"
"What if Prince Yi lied to you?"
"No way! Brother never lies to me—besides, why would he lie about something like that?"
More convinced than ever that Prince Yi was a top-tier smooth operator, Qianli Xun warned herself inside to keep her shields locked tight so he could never tangle her up and hurt Mengrong.
Talking on, she honored Mengrong's kindness by finishing her whole bowl of soup and eating two steamed buns.
With her closest confidante right beside her, tonight she meant to pour out every ounce of her heart.
#CH 138
#TITLE: Comforting Two Broken Hearts by Night
After dinner, reluctant to sleep on a full stomach, the two sat around the glowing brazier sipping the tea Xiaguo had whisked, dismissing all maids for private talk.
When Xilan worried who would wait on Princess later, Qianli Xun waved her off: "Go rest—I'll wait on Mengrong myself."
"I've got two hands and two feet—who needs waiting on?" Qiao Mengrong laughed, turning to Xilan: "Go sleep, come back in the morning."
With Princess's order laid down, Xilan trailed Xiaguo and Kuqiao out and shut the door behind them.
Studying Shuzhen's face, Qiao Mengrong saw the redness around her eyes had faded over dinner, though her breezy tone still sounded forced.
"If it hurts, Shuzhen, cry it all out right in front of me—then stop thinking about Young Master Qiao and live your own life."
"Want me bawling a second time? Already cried my tank dry—not another single tear for this mess."
"Of course I don't want you crying again!" Lifting her tea bowl for a sip, Qiao Mengrong praised, "Great tea—your maid whisks rings around me."
Xiaguo had made that tea—bringing back the image of her kneeling in the snow begging to go comfort Wenyuan.
When had that girl fallen for Wenyuan right under her nose without Qianli Xun having a clue? Was Xiaguo that good at hiding, or was she herself that blind?
Either way, Qianli Xun felt an odd twinge: a maid could chase Wenyuan with zero chains, while she stood hemmed in by a hundred walls.
Loving someone needed zero reason; not being able to love someone brought ten thousand reasons—and any single one built an iron wall between them.
"Mengrong, would you ever agree to be Prince Yi's concubine?"
Caught off guard, Qiao Mengrong froze—she had never once imagined such a thing.
Seeing her blank stare, Qianli Xun gave a crooked smile: "Blessed by an imperial marriage decree, how could you fathom commoners' woes?"
"Did you actually think about becoming Young Master Qiao's concubine?"
Qianli Xun shook her head: "Never once. Wenyuan tested me today, and I turned him down flat."
"Good for you! After the way the Qiaos treated you, stooping to concubine would be letting them off way too easy!"
Warmed by her fierce loyalty, Qianli Xun teased, "Your surname is Qiao too—why aren't you siding with the Qiaos?"
"Different Qiao—I have zero blood relation to their Qiao family! I only side with what's right. Sigh... you and Young Master Qiao were a match made in heaven; tearing you apart is such a crime."
Lowering her eyes to the glowing embers, Qianli Xun spoke the line she hated saying most:
"Wenyuan and I were fated to meet, never meant to stay. Reaching this end was bound to happen. Shame human eyes can't see the future—know ahead of time and you'd spare yourself walking down a dead end."
Qiao Mengrong saw that beneath the pain, Shuzhen had made peace with letting go—no need for empty lectures, just a loyal pair of ears.
"Do you regret ever loving Young Master Qiao?"
Qianli Xun shook her head: "Not a bit. Wenyuan was my first love—I'll keep that memory tucked warm in my heart forever."
Qiao Mengrong grinned: "First love? Heard you had a thing for some Young Master Xu before marrying Lin Jue!"
"Amnesia wiped him clean—to the post-amnesia me, Wenyuan was the first man I ever loved. First love shines bright because it's the first: brief as a night-blooming epiphyllum, dazzling as fireworks—gone in a flash, leaving the glow behind in memory."
"Funny how both you and Brother went through amnesia."
Her remark nudged that old hunch about Prince Yi and Shen Jianchen back to life—before Qianli Xun shook it off as homesickness again.
"Hardly the same! He came back from the dead; I got beaten into amnesia by Lin Jue."
Expecting Mengrong to chime right back, Qianli Xun met silence instead.
Shuzhen's talk of first love had plunged Qiao Mengrong into her own thoughts.
Prince Yi was her first love AND the man she was going to wed—luckier than Shuzhen by far.
Yet Shuzhen had hit a raw nerve too: ever since coming back from death with his memory wiped, her own first love felt just like a vanishing night flower.
"What are you brooding over, Mengrong?" Guessing she was thinking of Prince Yi, Qianli Xun sighed inside: how would sweet Mengrong survive married to a playboy prince?
Still, she had to tip her hat to Prince Yi's game: endless patience, thoughtful gestures, silky lines, asking nothing back—she was tempted to hang a sign around his neck reading "Northern Song's Number-One Heartbreaker"!
"Oh, just wondering what Brother and Young Master Qiao are talking about right now."
Hating to watch her best friend get played, Qianli Xun poked a hole in the lie: "Actually, Mengrong, after Wenyuan and I broke up today, Prince Yi escorted me all the way home—he wasn't with Wenyuan..."
Qiao Mengrong didn't even blink: "I know! Brother told me already—said he worried you might do something reckless, so for my sake he looked after you on Young Master Qiao's behalf. Then right after dropping you off, he bumped into Young Master Qiao at the city gate and comforted him too! Brother really knocks himself out for you two."
Qianli Xun's eyes bulged and her jaw dropped—Prince Yi had covered every track in advance, playing Mengrong like a fiddle!
"Prince Yi escorting another woman—doesn't that bother you?!"
"What 'other woman'? You're my best friend! Brother looking after you puts my mind at ease."
Torn between warmth and heartache for Mengrong, Qianli Xun's own grief slipped right into the background—already cried clean out behind closed doors earlier.
"Mengrong, Prince Yi's handsome, rich, and royal—stop letting him escort other girls alone, or some girl might fall for him!"
"Did you fall for Brother?"
"Are you kidding?! Not in a million years!"
"See? That's why I'm happy to let Brother help you!"
Exasperated, Qianli Xun blurted, "Are you naive?! Aren't you afraid Prince Yi might fall for ME?!"
"Asking that straight out proves there's nothing shady between you two—besides, you're my best friend, I trust you."
Poor Mengrong—zero clue about the modern warning to guard against fire, thieves, and best friends!
"Of course you can trust me. Still, whatever excuse Prince Yi cooks up to look after me next time, say no—stay sharp! Besides, I don't need his help."
"You're so sweet looking out for me, Shuzhen—I'll listen! Sure you don't need Brother's help, though? You've asked him for favors plenty of times."
"For your sake, I won't bother him ever again."
Drowsiness rolled over them; Qianli Xun pulled Mengrong into bed, meaning to chat a bit more, yet the second their heads hit the pillows, both dropped fast asleep.
Meanwhile, after leaving the Divine Willow, Qiao Wenxian had skipped Youlin Retreat—with Shuzhen gone, who cared where he slept?
A hollow fog settled over him, making him wonder why he even walked this earth. Unable to choose his own bride, what kind of man was he? Death crossed his mind, only for duty to his family to chain him back down.
How was he supposed to drag through the years ahead—just breathing like an empty shell?
Back at Qiao Manor, he shut himself in his room, replaying every sweet hour with Shuzhen—then her iron stance in the snow ending it all, twisting the knife deeper every second.
He hated himself for even hinting at making her a concubine—an insult to her pride and to their love.
At dusk Prince Yi arrived bearing wine and dishes from Juxiang Tower. Setting out the feast, Bai Feng and Nan Shuang slipped off to another room to talk.
Taking a seat first, Shen Jianchen called out, "Brought Capital Spring wine—come have a few cups with me and drown your sorrows!"
Lying dull-eyed on his bed lost in misery, Qiao Wenxian dragged himself up at mention of wine, shuffled into the outer room, dropped into a chair, filled his own cup, and downed it in one gulp.
As he reached to pour a second, Prince Yi caught the pot: "Pace yourself! I came to help you let off steam—pass out cold and how do we talk?"
"What's talking fix?! Won't undo the betrothal or bring Shuzhen back!" Pouring another cup and tossing it back, he slammed the cup down and broke into sobs: "Waaah!"
"A real man doesn't shed easy tears—why start bawling?"
Choking through tears: "Can't even choose my own wife—what kind of man am I?! Why shouldn't I cry?! Waaah!" Pouring another cup, he drained it whole.
"You and Youqi Jushi are over—face it. Instead of wallowing, climb out and shoulder the duties waiting for you."
Checking his sobs, Qiao Wenxian spat bitter: "Duties?! Hmph—if not for family duty, I'd be dead already!"
"Forget Youqi Jushi. You two were never meant to be."
"Forget? How?! I've loved her forever—how do you erase that overnight?!"
"Only by letting go can you start a new life."
"New life? Hmph! Couldn't care less—without Shuzhen, every woman's the same!"
Shen Jianchen knew that ache down to his marrow—losing Qianli Xun felt no different. Filling both cups, he raised his: "Come on, drink with me—two poor fools in the same boat."
Lifting his cup, confusion pierced Qiao Wenxian's misery: "With a beauty right beside you and an imperial betrothal everyone envies, how are you a poor fool?"
Unable to tell Wenyuan the truth about Qianli Xun, Shen Jianchen clinked his cup: "Here's to the women we love and can never have."
Draining his cup, Shen Jianchen looked up to find Wenyuan holding an empty cup and staring at him in puzzlement.
"Why stare at me like that?"
Setting down his cup as wine warmed his cheeks, Qiao Wenxian slurred, "Can't have? Princess Jiahe is right in your palm—who can't you have? Don't tell me Prince Yi has another woman in his heart?"
"Another woman? Wish I could swap—can't do it. My heart's stuck on her alone."
"Talking in riddles, Prince Yi—makes no sense."
Too drowned in his own pain to puzzle over Prince Yi, Qiao Wenxian kept pouring cup after cup without touching a morsel of food until drunkenness rolled in and his wishes spilled loose:
"Know what, Prince Yi?... Shuzhen turning me down... was all fake... She was just... too mad... talking out of anger..."
Even knowing it was drunken rambling, unease pricked Shen Jianchen: "How do you know Youqi Jushi didn't mean it?"
"She told me once... wanted to marry me... give me... lots and lots of kids... even sat right on my lap... haha, falling asleep in my arms, fully dressed..." Slumping over the table, Wenyuan passed out cold.
Shen Jianchen let out a quiet sigh: "Better asleep—no hurting tonight, and tomorrow brings a fresh day."
Calling Bai Feng and Nan Shuang in, they hauled Young Master Qiao onto his bed.
With night grown deep, Prince Yi stayed overnight at Qiao Manor—delighting Qiao Shouren, who had guest quarters made up at once.
#CH 139
#TITLE: Uncovering the Mastermind at Last
First thing next morning, Shen Jianchen waited in his carriage outside Wangyue Pavilion for Qiao Mengrong. Last night had shown him Wenyuan would never forget Youqi Jushi—carrying her in his heart for life.
What about Qianli Xun? Would she carry Wenyuan in her heart forever too?
Peering out the carriage window as Wangyue Pavilion's household saw Qiao Mengrong off, he saw only Matron Wei and a cluster of maids, no Qianli Xun—was she still heartbroken?
The second Qiao Mengrong climbed inside and sat down, Shen Jianchen blurted out, "How is Youqi Jushi?"
Remembering Shuzhen's warning last night, Qiao Mengrong eyed him with caution: "Why is Brother so concerned about Shuzhen?"
Shen Jianchen scrambled to cover: "Not for her—for Wenyuan! Saw last night he'll never get over Youqi Jushi, probably carry her in his heart his whole life, which'll wreck his marriage later. Just wanted to know where Youqi Jushi's head is—if she's going to pine for him forever too."
Satisfied by his excuse, Qiao Mengrong dropped her guard: "She hurts, sure, yet she's sorted it all out—won't stay tangled up over Young Master Qiao anymore..."
A faint, hidden smile tugged at Shen Jianchen's mouth.
"Though she also swore off men and romance for good—said love hurts too much, so she's living solo from now on."
His smile died on the spot. Qianli Xun was a woman of her word—melting her heart now would be ten times harder.
A long sigh slipped out: "Sigh..."
"Why sighing, Brother?"
Covering smooth: "Just a shame watching two people in love torn apart."
Thinking of themselves, Qiao Mengrong smiled soft: "Brother and I are so lucky."
Without replying, Shen Jianchen felt a heavy reluctance as the carriage pulled away from Wangyue Pavilion.
...
For Qianli Xun, this winter promised to bite twice as cold. She made up her mind to keep her braziers roaring, hole up in Wangyue Pavilion, and wait for spring.
Her half-year mark wasn't even close, yet her story with Wenyuan had already hit the final page—arriving out of nowhere, ending just as abrupt.
Asking herself honestly: had she stopped loving Wenyuan overnight? No. Yet she ordered herself to act like she had—fake it long enough and habit turned act into truth.
No romance? Time to pour into her career! Besides writing huaben scripts, she meant to turn Wangyue Pavilion into a second Qianli Fist Manor.
Where to find students? Posting flyers to recruit apprentices wasn't realistic right now; better pick servants inside Wangyue Pavilion with decent martial aptitude and train them to keep busy.
She knew Kuqiao and Xiaguo too well—zero martial bones between them.
Gathering the whole staff and sizing them up, she picked ten: three maids and seven male servants.
"From today on, drop your chores and train in Qianli Fist with me—clear?"
Overjoyed, all ten shouted in unison: "Clear!"
"Good! Follow me to the back courtyard!"
Matron Wei didn't interfere—poor Young Lady had suffered enough; whatever kept her smiling went.
Since returning from beneath the Divine Willow, Xiaguo hadn't dared speak a word to her mistress, terrified Miss would pack her back to the countryside like Matron Wei threatened.
Walking toward the back courtyard alongside Kuqiao, Xiaguo rushed ahead and dropped to her knees: "Beat me or scold me all you want, Miss—just don't send me away!"
Qianli Xun halted: "What are you kneeling for? Ground's freezing—you'll catch cold!"
"Not getting up until Miss promises not to send me away!"
"When did I ever say I was sending you away?"
"Miss didn't say it, yet I messed up—sooner or later you'll kick me out..."
Recalling Xiaguo's crush on Wenyuan—too numb to care yesterday—now that Xiaguo brought it up, a prickle of annoyance did surface. Having her own maid moon over her boyfriend would irk anyone.
"How long has this been going on?"
Xiaguo held nothing back: "Ever since before your divorce, Miss, when you met Young Master Qiao by West Lake—that's when I fell for him."
The very day Qianli Xun had fallen for Qiao Wenxian at first sight—how could she forget?
"Started pretty early, didn't you."
"I figured once Miss married Young Master Qiao, I'd get to wait on him—maybe even become his concubine someday..."
Kuqiao snapped, "Xiaguo, shut your mouth!"
Swallowing her annoyance—what was there left to guard now that she and Wenyuan were finished?—Qianli Xun said, "Keep talking."
"The night Miss stayed at Youlin Retreat, I snuck over and confessed to Young Master Qiao, and he turned me down. Yesterday he turned me down again. I could've hidden it from you, Miss, yet you've treated me too well to lie—so I worked up the nerve to confess today. Please don't kick me out!"
Qianli Xun wanted to scold her, then stopped—who the girl crushed on was her own business! Still, she needed to say something to show she was over Wenyuan for good.
"You're my maid—where's your backbone?! Throwing yourself at a man and begging to be a concubine?! Quit embarrassing me out there!"
Walking on toward the back courtyard, she paused two steps later: "Still kneeling? Get up and come to the back yard with Kuqiao!"
"Thank you, Miss!" Jumping up thrilled, Xiaguo hurried after her.
From then on, between drilling her ten martial students and writing huaben scripts, Qianli Xun's days filled tight and brisk. Before long, Wenyuan stopped crossing her mind during daylight—and not thinking meant not hurting.
At night alone in bed he still crept back, though the sting had dulled to a quiet wistfulness—what a shame such a lovely romance had ended so soon.
Her ten trainees had decent enough build, though short of true master potential. Qianli Xun didn't mind—she was keeping busy, not looking for a grandmaster heir to Qianli Fist.
Throwing themselves into practice with fierce energy, all ten nailed the basics within a month.
Pleased, Qianli Xun moved them onto real combat moves—envisioning seven male guards watching her gates and three bodyguard maids trailing her everywhere. What noble lady in Lin'an could top that entourage?!
...
On Laba Festival, while Zhu Xiyan sat eating Laba porridge, Lin Jue arrived with news: they had caught a rumor-monger.
Changing into plain clothes with no servants, Zhu Xiyan followed Lin Jue to an abandoned house tucked in a lonely corner of town.
Soldiers guarded the door, swinging it open as the Prefect and Commandant Lin stepped up.
Inside lay a man trussed tight with rope, a rag stuffed in his mouth.
Lin Jue signaled a soldier to yank the gag out.
The instant his mouth cleared, the prisoner yelled, "Mercy, Your Lordship! I didn't spread any rumors—somebody framed me!"
Lin Jue sneered: "My men don't nab folks without proof! Refuse to confess and you'll taste a stout cudgel."
"I didn't do anything, Your Lordship—what's there to confess?!"
Lin Jue barked, "Men! Give him a round of warning strokes!"
A soldier strode in hefting a thick wooden staff while two others pinned the man face-down on the floor; heavy blows rained down as the prisoner howled in agony.
Offering Zhu Xiyan a chair, Lin Jue assured him, "Wait a minute, Lord Prefect—we'll pry the truth right out of him."
Zhu Xiyan nodded, more impressed with Lin Jue by the minute.
Twenty strokes in, the prisoner broke, shrieking, "Mercy, Your Lordship! I'll talk! I'll talk!"
Raising a hand, Lin Jue halted the beating; the soldier lowered the club and stepped back.
"If I talk, Your Lordship has to promise to spare me!"
Zhu Xiyan said, "Talk first; I'll decide once I hear it."
Panting, the man spilled: "I was just following orders—paid to walk around reading off those rumor sheets!"
Zhu Xiyan pressed: "Who gave you orders?"
"Our boss!" Naming the gang boss, he added the boss was probably lounging at Chunxiang Brothel right now.
Lin Jue dispatched men to Chunxiang Brothel at once; moving fast, they hauled back the exact gang boss who had met Chen Haoyu in the alley.
Dragged into the dark abandoned room, one look at his underling's blood-soaked rear and the soldier's thick club killed any urge to lie—he dropped straight to his knees.
"I confess everything, Your Lordship—just spare my hide! I only took silver to do a job; I've got zero grudge against Miss Zhu!"
Face dark as thunder, Zhu Xiyan demanded, "Spit it out—who paid you to pull this filthy stunt?!"
The gang boss swallowed: "Truth be told, he's even tied to the Zhu house..."
Hearing a tie to his own family spiked Zhu Xiyan's rage: "Who?!"
"Young Master Chen—Chen Haoyu!"
Shock shot Zhu Xiyan straight to his feet: "Are you sure you're not lying?!"
"Give me ten extra guts and I wouldn't dare lie to you, Your Lordship! Young Master Chen held a grudge against Miss Zhu, cooked up this smear campaign for revenge, had his own people write out the sheets for us, and paid us heaps of silver!"
Forced to believe it, Zhu Xiyan sank back into his chair in dead silence.
"Every word's the gospel truth, Your Lordship—nothing personal from us, please forgive us!" the boss begged.
Unwilling to widen the scandal—and knowing these hired thugs didn't merit death—Zhu Xiyan waved a hand.
Lin Jue snapped at both men: "His Lordship is merciful and letting you off—get lost before he changes his mind!"
Scrambling up, the boss hoisted his untied underling and started for the door.
Zhu Xiyan barked: "Hold on!"
Both froze in terror, dropping back to their knees: "Mercy, Your Lordship!"
Zhu Xiyan warned them cold: "Walk out that door and breathe not one word of today to a living soul—above all not to Young Master Chen—or I will show no mercy!"
The boss swore, "Today never happened!"
Lin Jue roared, "Still kneeling?! Scram!"
Scrambling up again, the boss dragged his underling out into the lane.
With the culprit turning out to be Chen Haoyu, Lin Jue sensed the tricky bind and asked, "Shall I arrest Young Master Chen, Lord Prefect?"
Without a blink, Zhu Xiyan ordered steel-firm: "Grab him! Keep the Chen family in the dark. Much obliged, Commandant Lin—and keep your lips sealed tight on this."
"Rest easy, Lord Prefect—not a syllable leaks from me."
All the way back to Zhu Manor, Zhu Xiyan couldn't wrap his head around it: as far as he knew, Chen Haoyu and Shuzhen never even crossed paths—why on earth did that brat hate Shuzhen enough to wreck her name and taint the whole Zhu house?!
Once he got that little menace in his hands, he'd grill the truth out of him AND teach him the discipline the Chens failed to give him!
#CH 140
#TITLE: Cracking the Whip on Chen Haoyu
That day Chen Haoyu slipped out of Chen Manor solo without a single lackey, worried a crowd would attract attention now that Lin Jue was digging harder—better keep a low profile.
Halfway to a gambling den, a burlap sack dropped out of nowhere over his head, plunging him into pitch black as strong ropes bound him tight.
Chen Haoyu yelled, "Who the hell are you?! Ask around before tying up Grandpa Chen—my boys'll break your arms and legs!"
Then Lin Jue's voice answered cool: "Save your lungs, Young Master Chen. His Lordship the Prefect requests your presence."
Realizing his smear campaign had blown wide open, Chen Haoyu shut his mouth and let them hoist him onto the carriage, racking his brains how to wriggle past Zhu Xiyan without getting flayed.
Drawing a blank after a minute, he called out, "Lin Jue—you in here?"
"What if I am?"
"Pull this sack off my head! We know each other—what's the point keeping me blindfolded?"
Reaching over, Lin Jue yanked the sack off and tossed it aside.
Squinting until his eyes adjusted to the dim light, Chen Haoyu asked, "Where are you hauling me?"
"To see His Lordship the Prefect."
"What's he going to do to me?"
"No idea. First tell me why you smeared Zhu Shuzhen."
Chen Haoyu smirked: "Bet you've eaten plenty of dirt from her too, Lin Jue! Look at it this way—I avenged you! Shouldn't you be thanking me?"
Remembering the day and night Zhu Shuzhen had kept him trussed in ropes, Lin Jue muttered, "Sure, I got burned by her too—yet even I wouldn't sink as low as you, getting the whole city cursing her and wrecking her marriage so nobody wants her."
Chen Haoyu poked at him: "Funny—you sound like you're secretly enjoying it!"
"Me?! What makes you think that? If I were enjoying it, would I be hauling you to the Prefect?"
"Come on—stuck in officialdom chasing promotions, of course you jump when Zhu Xiyan snaps his fingers! Not me—I'm a free commoner with zero fear of Zhu Xiyan. Let's cut a deal: you love power, so you must love silver too. I'll pay you off—plus I avenged you against Zhu Shuzhen! Cash plus favors—how about letting me walk?"
"Hmph! Sure I love power and silver, and sure you gave Zhu Shuzhen a taste of hell—yet I'm still not letting you go! Because while smearing Zhu Shuzhen, you made a laughingstock out of ME too!"
Chen Haoyu blinked: "When did my rumors mock you?!"
"Claiming Zhu Shuzhen had affairs behind my back—isn't that mocking me as a spineless husband who couldn't control his wife?!"
Chen Haoyu chuckled: "Big tough guy with delicate feelings! Fair point—I was so busy sticking it to Zhu Shuzhen I forgot about you. My bad!"
"Hmph! What good's 'my bad'? Wait till you face the Prefect—you're in for a world of pain!"
For several days Zhu Xiyan had turned the Chen angle over with utmost care: not only were the two families in-laws, the Chens were Lin'an's wealthiest clan, controlling half the city's commerce—rattle the Chens and Lin'an's entire economy shook.
Everything about the Chen house was sterling except one rotten son—wrecking his own house was their problem, yet wrecking the Zhu house crossed every line. Both Chen Haoyu and the Chen clan had to pay!
Decision made, the instant Lin Jue sent word, Zhu Xiyan changed robes, grabbed a whip, left every servant behind without breathing a word to his family, and rode solo to the abandoned dark house to wait for Chen Haoyu.
Leaning against the wall stood a bulging burlap sack—looked stuffed with old straw.
Walking over to lay the sack flat on the floor, Zhu Xiyan felt its heft: grain, not straw! Odd to find a sack of grain left in a deserted shack.
Before he could wonder further, footsteps sounded outside. Zhu Xiyan stepped back into his chair and sat rigid, face dark with magisterial sternness.
The door swung open; Lin Jue shoved Chen Haoyu inside and closed it again.
Peering through the gloom, Chen Haoyu made out Zhu Xiyan sitting stone-faced in his chair.
Putting on a breezy front, Chen Haoyu said, "If Lord Prefect wanted to see me, just send my sister to fetch me from Chen Manor—why have Lin Jue haul me into a shack?"
Ignoring him, Zhu Xiyan turned to Lin Jue: "Go attend to your duties, Commandant Lin—I'll take it from here."
Reading between the lines that this was now private family business—and eager to go report the breakthrough to Prince Yi—Lin Jue bowed:
"I'll take my leave, Lord Prefect. Send word to Lin Manor anytime you need me." Bowing out, he shut the door behind him.
In that brief flash of daylight as the door opened, Chen Haoyu caught the volcanic rage on Zhu Xiyan's face and thought, "Cooked! Wonder what punishment he's cooking up..."
"Kneel!"
Soft-spoken, yet heavy as a gavel.
Guilty to the bone, Chen Haoyu dropped straight to his knees without a peep.
"Our two houses are in-laws on warm terms—why on earth would you sink to gutter tricks to ruin Shuzhen's honor and smear the Zhu name?!"
"Uncle Zhu!" Chen Haoyu turned on the charm fast. "I never meant to touch the Zhu family's name! Your daughter bullied me too hard, so I lost my head wanting to mess with her—didn't dream it'd blow up so huge! You know how rumors are—once loose, nobody can stop them..."
Rage boiling over, Zhu Xiyan shot up and cracked his whip straight across the burlap sack beside him: "So you KNEW rumors can't be stopped?! Then why pull a dirty stunt like that?! Begging for a beating, aren't you!"
Two more ferocious cracks lashed the grain sack.
Even untouched, Chen Haoyu flinched at the sheer whistling force of that whip—one hit on bare flesh would rip skin clean off!
"Blame Zhu Shuzhen for picking on me! If she hadn't bullied me, I never would've hit back! Want to whip someone, go home and whip your own daughter!"
Furious, Zhu Xiyan whipped the sack again until the burlap split open, spilling moldy grain across the dirt floor with a sharp musty stench.
Terror froze Chen Haoyu's tongue—shredding a heavy burlap sack like paper! One stroke on him would kill him dead! He clamped his mouth shut tight.
Winded from swinging, Zhu Xiyan sank back into his chair with a heavy sigh: "Whatever set you off, the damage is done. If you're a real man, think how you're going to fix the mess you made."
Baffled, Chen Haoyu ventured cautious: "What do you mean, Uncle Zhu?"
Lifting sharp eyes to him, Zhu Xiyan asked, "First tell me—do you want to clean up the mess your rumors brought on Shuzhen and the Zhu family?"
In truth, Chen Haoyu couldn't be happier watching Zhu Shuzhen crash and burn, and couldn't care less about the Zhu house—yet kneeling under a raised whip, he wasn't suicidal!
"Yes! Of course I want to fix it! Good news is nobody cares about the rumors anymore, so Shuzhen's and the Zhu family's trouble is already over! No need to stress, Uncle Zhu—let me off this once, and I swear I'll never pull a stunt like that again!"
"Hmph! Let you off? Dream on! Sure the gossip died down, yet you blew up Shuzhen's fine match with the Qiao family, forced me to cast her out alone to weep her eyes out, and left her with zero prospects for a decent marriage!" Two genuine tears rolled down Zhu Xiyan's cheeks.
Seizing the opening, Chen Haoyu offered smooth: "Easy fix, Uncle Zhu—hear my plan!"
Hope flickered in Zhu Xiyan: "What plan?"
"Bring Zhu Shuzhen back home—with family around she'll cheer right up! And with your clout as Prefect of Lin'an, landing your daughter a fine match won't be hard at all..."
That wasn't the line Zhu Xiyan wanted. Jumping up, he swung the whip toward Chen Haoyu, checked himself mid-air, and slashed the split grain sack again, sending another flood of moldy grain pouring across the floor.
Jolted half out of his skin—what had he said wrong?!—Chen Haoyu yelped:
"I'm genuinely repenting and offering ideas, Uncle Zhu—why swing the whip again?!"
Pointing the whip handle straight at Chen Haoyu's nose, Zhu Xiyan declared: "Since you wrecked Shuzhen's marriage, man up and MARRY HER YOURSELF! Restore both Shuzhen's and the Zhu family's honor!"
Over the past few days, Zhu Xiyan had cooked up this masterstroke: double the tie between in-laws, force Chen Haoyu to own his crime, AND secure a wealthy home for his daughter—three birds with one stone!
Hauling Chen Haoyu into this dark shack was pure shock-and-awe to scare him into agreeing first.
Chen Haoyu's eyes nearly popped out of his skull—what a sly old fox, trying to dump his unwanted daughter straight onto him! Not in a hundred, a thousand, ten thousand years!
"Don't flatter me, Uncle Zhu—I'm no real man! Can't possibly marry your daughter! Besides, Zhu Shuzhen would never say yes!"
Expecting refusal, Zhu Xiyan had his next move ready. Rolling up his right sleeve, whip gripped in his left hand, he seized Chen Haoyu by the scruff: "Whether you agree or not isn't your call! Come with me to face your parents right now and seal your betrothal to Shuzhen before your elders!"
Trussed helpless in ropes, Chen Haoyu wailed: "Uncle Zhu! Lord Prefect! Master Zhu! I admit I'm scum—beat me raw with that whip, lock me in Lin'an's dungeon for a year, anything—just don't force my parents to betroth me to Zhu Shuzhen! I'm begging you!"
Deaf to his pleas, Zhu Xiyan kicked the door open, hauled Chen Haoyu out, heaved him into the carriage with his driver's help, and barked: "To Chen Manor!"
Once the wheels rolled, Chen Haoyu fell silent. Looking at Zhu Xiyan's iron jaw, the old fox really meant to bully the Chens into a betrothal today! Regret bit hard—had he seen this coming, he'd never have paid a single copper for rumors!
With a few blocks left to Chen Manor, he racked his brains for any trick to stop Zhu Xiyan shoving his unwanted daughter down his throat.
#CH 141
#TITLE: The Chen Family Agrees to Double the Bond
Reaching Chen Manor's main gate, Zhu Xiyan stormed straight inside—whip in one hand, bound Chen Haoyu hauled by the other.
Assuming Second Young Master had pulled some colossal disaster to get tied up by the Prefect, gatekeepers skipped bowing and stepped forward: "Hold on, Lord Prefect—talk things out calmly, why tie up our Young Master?"
Zhu Xiyan snapped, "Run tell Chen Jianglin I'm here to help discipline his son!"
Not daring another word, a servant sprinted off to report.
Unfamiliar with Chen Manor's maze of paths, Zhu Xiyan followed the direction the servant had bolted.
Halfway in, Qinggua ran up blocking his way: "Lord Prefect, how did my master offend you to get you this furious—bringing a whip and tying him up?!"
"Shut up and lead the way!" Perfect—a free guide!
Qinggua glanced at his bound master: "Should I lead the way, Young Master?"
Chen Haoyu roared, "The Prefect's standing right here—why ask me?! Lead!"
Qinggua answered: "Yes, Young Master, leading now—your orders, so don't blame me later!"
Bound above the waist yet nimble below, Chen Haoyu swung a kick at Qinggua: "One more peep and I'll kick you dead!"
Tugged along by Zhu Xiyan while Qinggua hopped clear, the kick missed clean.
Letting out a cheeky "Heheh," Qinggua trotted ahead: "This way, Lord Prefect!"
Dragging Chen Haoyu deeper inside, Zhu Xiyan met Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan hurrying out with maids.
Chatting indoors when the servant burst in saying the Prefect had hauled their son home trussed up with a whip in hand, both realized in a flash why their wild son had played angel at home lately—running home for cover after a massive screw-up!
Heart aching yet furious at seeing her son bound, Yao Qingyan joined Chen Jianglin in bowing: "Lord Prefect."
Zhu Xiyan declared, "No Prefect of Lin'an today—only the Zhu house and the Chen house! Your son committed an outrageous wrong; I brought him here so we can discipline him together and settle how he'll make amends and restore the Zhu family's face."
Catching the hint that his in-law wanted to settle this privately—saving Chen Manor public disgrace—Chen Jianglin switched to warmer address:
"In-law, what on earth did Haoyu do to make you tie him up yourself? Tell me, and I'll tan his hide proper!"
Zhu Xiyan said, "Not out here—indoors."
"This way, In-law!"
Chen Jianglin ushered everyone into Chen Manor's reception hall.
Still gripping Chen Haoyu tight without sitting, Zhu Xiyan requested, "Servants get in the way—clear the room."
Yao Qingyan waved them out: "All of you, step outside."
Filing out, Qinggua cast a worried glance back at his master.
Chen Haoyu flashed Qinggua a sharp wink; catching on at once, Qinggua slipped past the door and sprinted straight for Old Madam Xia's quarters.
Alone in the hall, Zhu Xiyan shoved Chen Haoyu straight down onto the floor: "Kneel!"
Scowling, Chen Haoyu dropped to his knees—no plan cooked up during the ride, forced to wing it, sworn on his life never to marry Zhu Shuzhen.
Looking at his hopeless son filled Chen Jianglin with fury and shame, swallowing his temper before the Prefect:
"Have a seat and tell us, In-law."
All three sat, Yao Qingyan glancing to her husband to speak.
Catching her look, Chen Jianglin pressed, "Tell me straight, In-law—what crime did Haoyu commit? I'll settle accounts for you right now!"
Zhu Xiyan slapped the whip onto the table: "What crime? A monstrous one! Let him confess himself!"
Yao Qingyan demanded of her son, "What did you do to push our in-law this far?!"
Cornered with zero escape, Chen Haoyu laid it out: "I paid thugs to spread those rumors about Zhu Shuzhen..."
Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan gasped in unison: "WHAT?!"
Without another word, Chen Jianglin leaped up and cracked his palm across his son's cheek: "You worthless bastard! Raising hell outside wasn't enough—you targeted Miss Zhu?! Don't you know a maiden's honor is her life?! Got the whole city cursing her and wrecked her fine match with the Qiao family—you... you...!"
Blind with rage, Chen Jianglin snatched the whip off the table and brought it slashing across Chen Haoyu's back.
Chen Haoyu howled bloody murder: "Ow! Killing me! Are you even my real dad?! Zhu Shuzhen bullied me—can't I even hit back?!"
Zhu Xiyan sat watching unmoved—a spoiled brat like this needed a real thrashing.
Two more lashes landed; as Chen Jianglin raised his arm for a fourth, Yao Qingyan broke in: "Husband, watch you don't cripple our son!"
Dropping his arm with a ragged sigh, Chen Jianglin set the whip back on the table, saw his wife's reddened eyes, sat down, and buried his face in his hands.
Though his father had clearly pulled his punches, Chen Haoyu hammed it up loud: "Mother! Look—Father ripped my back to shreds! Burning like fire!"
With their in-law sitting right there, Yao Qingyan refused to coddle him: "Pulling a wicked stunt like that, your father went easy on you! Can a few bruises compare to the agony Miss Zhu suffered?!"
Seeing neither parent taking his side, Chen Haoyu sulked into silence.
At last Zhu Xiyan spoke: "Even beating him to death won't ease the rage in my chest—or give my Shuzhen back her good name."
Lowering his hands, Chen Jianglin looked across at Zhu Xiyan: "Tell me, In-law—what will ease your heart?"
Right on cue! Zhu Xiyan dropped his terms: "Your son wrecked Shuzhen's match with Young Master Qiao and tarnished her name, leaving her hard-pressed to find a worthy house. If the Chen family agrees to double our bond through marriage, I'll wipe the slate clean."
Chen Jianglin perked up at once, trading a glance with Yao Qingyan; after a quiet beat, she nodded.
Chen Jianglin jumped in: "Splendid idea, In-law! The Chen family welcomes doubling our bond!"
"Done! No going back on your word—come propose at Zhu Manor seven days from today!" Refusing to let a golden deal slip, Zhu Xiyan nailed down the date on the spot.
Chen Jianglin agreed with all his heart: the cleanest fix possible, and having his wild son marry Jiangnan's celebrated poetess was a stroke of luck beyond merit!
Plus, Wenxin praised Shuzhen to the skies every visit, leaving both parents delighted to welcome her into the family.
"Rest easy, In-law—seven days it is! Every matchmaker and every betrothal gift, down to the last detail!"
Dropping his injury act in sheer panic, Chen Haoyu yelled, "Father! Mother! Zhu Shuzhen and I clash like fire and water—we'd murder each other married! Plus she's divorced and three years older than me—I refuse!"
Chen Jianglin snapped back: "Once wed under one roof you'll get along! As for divorce, even Emperor Zhenzong didn't mind—who are you to turn up your nose?! And three years older? Ever hear 'marry a wife three years older, hug a brick of solid gold'?!"
Right then Old Madam Xia shuffled in supported by a maid—spotting her grandson bound on his knees with his outer robe slashed to ribbons! Luckily thick winter padding had saved his skin.
Even so, heartbreak swept the old lady as she rushed to his side: "Does it hurt, my boy?!"
Chen Haoyu laid it on thick: "Hurts to death, Grandmother!"
Furious, Old Madam Xia barked at Qinggua: "Standing around like a post?! Untie Young Master right now!"
Qinggua darted forward to loosen the knots.
Marching up to her son, Old Madam Xia pointed a trembling finger in Chen Jianglin's face: "Unfilial wretch! Call yourself a father?! Tying your own son up and whipping him—when did your father ever beat you like this?! Hurt one hair on my grandson and I'll never forgive you!"
Stepping up to steady her elbow, Chen Jianglin scrambled to appease: "Calm down, Mother—my fault, all right? I'll never discipline him like that again! Come sit down."
Shoving him off, Old Madam Xia snapped, "I have no son like you! Strike my grandson once more and I'll strike you ten times back!"
With Old Madam Xia standing in fury, neither Chen Jianglin nor Yao Qingyan dared sit, hovering with sheepish smiles to coax her down.
Freed from his ropes, Chen Haoyu stood off to one side enjoying the show.
Seeing firsthand how Chen Haoyu got molded into a hopeless rake, Zhu Xiyan stood and offered a courteous bow: "Don't blame Jianglin, Old Madam—I tied Haoyu up myself. He made a grave mistake and had to face the music. Everything's settled now, though: the Zhus and Chens are doubling our marriage bond, so sit back and enjoy the good news!"
Only then noticing the Prefect, Old Madam Xia offered a quick nod: "Forgive an old woman not greeting Lord Prefect first—this hopeless son of mine keeps bullying my grandson until the poor boy dreads coming home! Just started staying home and he whips him again—can't stand seeing my boy happy!"
Mid-rant, "doubling our marriage bond" registered; catching her breath, she asked, "What did Lord Prefect mean by doubling our marriage bond?"
Before Zhu Xiyan could answer, Chen Haoyu stepped in to hold her arm: "Stand up for me, Grandmother! The Prefect and my parents are forcing me to marry Miss Zhu! You don't know—Miss Zhu is a total tigress! Marry her and my life turns into daily torture—please save me!"
Melted to butter by her grandson clinging to her, Old Madam Xia beamed: "Quit talking nonsense! Even if Grandmother hasn't met Miss Zhu, I know her fame, read her poems, and hear your sister rave about her—gentle, lovely, refined, and brimming with learning! Landing her makes Grandmother thrilled for you!"
Zhu Xiyan grinned wider: "Seven days from now we seal the betrothal—does Old Madam approve?"
Old Madam Xia nodded like a pellet drum: "Approve! Approve! High time Haoyu settled down—seal it fast and wed fast! Only thing I don't get is why on earth you tied my grandson up and whipped him over happy news?!"
Stepping up to take Old Madam Xia's other arm, Yao Qingyan said, "Let me walk you back to your room, Mother, and explain the whole story."
Agreeing, Old Madam Xia paused at the door to order: "Qinggua, take Young Master to change clothes and check his skin—if there's a bruise, apply salve!"
Qinggua bowed: "Right away, Old Madam!"
Once they cleared out, Chen Jianglin cupped his hands to Zhu Xiyan: "Holding no grudge and still giving your daughter to our house leaves me speechless with gratitude, In-law. Rest assured, the day Shuzhen steps into Chen Manor, we'll cherish her like our own daughter."
Joy bubbled inside Zhu Xiyan—calamity flipped into triumph: Shuzhen secured a rich house, Zhu face stood intact, and he had doubled ties with Lin'an's richest clan! Months of gloom blew clean away.
"Family doesn't talk like strangers. Time for me to head back."
Snatching his whip off the table, Zhu Xiyan strode out, Chen Jianglin escorting him all the way to the front gate.
#CH 142
#TITLE: Beaten Black and Blue After Visiting Wangyue Pavilion
Fuming back to his own quarters, Chen Haoyu racked his brains over how to kill this betrothal. After all his scheming, winding up as Zhu Shuzhen's fallback husband made his blood boil!
Bringing fresh clothes, Qinggua helped him shed his slashed robe and checked his back carefully—not a single welt!
"Master still loves you, Young Master—pulled his strikes so only the robe tore!"
"Trying to freeze me to death?! Put my clothes on and shut up!"
